# MikoshiLang bundled library audit

Branch `audit/bundled-library` (base `origin/main` 7e29a55). Target semantics: **Wolfram Language**.
Generated by `scripts/library_audit_report.py` from `mikoshilang.library.STATUS` and the differential
cases in `tests/library/`.

**Rule:** every exposed function either returns a correct result or raises a clear error
(`NotImplementedError` for unsupported or quarantined functionality, `ValueError` for invalid input). It must
never return a silently wrong answer. A function stays unevaluated only where Wolfram would also return it
unevaluated, for example symbolic arguments to a numeric routine.

## Method

* Every head the legacy library exposed (620 heads in `tests/library/inventory_heads.txt`, covering 617 loaded
  functions / 753 rules) was reimplemented or re-checked in an area module under `mikoshilang/library/`. The
  legacy `extended*.py` rule tables are no longer dispatched.
* **References:** SymPy, mpmath (to 30+ digits), SciPy, NumPy and NetworkX, plus values from the Wolfram
  documentation and CODATA 2018. Expected values are written down independently of the implementation.
  Several inputs are used per function, including edge cases: 0, negative, empty, singular, non-integer,
  disconnected and out-of-domain inputs.
* Each verified, fixed or promoted head has at least 3 cases. Each error or quarantined head has a case that
  asserts it raises. Each kernel head has at least one case. `tests/library/test_coverage.py` enforces all of
  this, and also checks that every one of the remaining quarantined names raises `NotImplementedError`.
* **Test matrix (local):** Python 3.9, 3.12 and 3.13 with `.[all,test]`, plus a minimal `pip install .`
  (3.12). CI runs the same matrix.

## Notable wrong answers found and fixed

| Area | Legacy behaviour | Now |
|---|---|---|
| elementary | `ArcTan[x, y]` argument order swapped | Wolfram order `ArcTan[x, y]` = arg(x + iy) |
| elementary | `Round` did not round half to even; `Max`/`Min` returned floats (`Max[1/2, 0.4]` → 0.5) | banker's rounding; the exact extremal element is returned |
| elementary | `Sign`, `Floor`, `Ceiling` returned floats | integers |
| numbertheory | `FactorInteger[1]` wrong; `ExtendedGCD` output order wrong; `JordanTotient`, `DiophantineSolve` always crashed | fixed, tested against SymPy |
| special | `Beta[1/2, 1/2]` stayed unevaluated; `InverseErf`/`BetaRegularized` not mapped | `Pi`; mapped to SymPy/mpmath |
| calculus | `TaylorSeries` off by one order; `InflectionPoints[x^4]` reported 0; `CriticalPoints` dropped tuple solutions; `DirectionalDerivative` not normalised; `Minimize` was a local float search; `RSolve` always crashed; `Sum[1/k]` did not diverge | Wolfram results |
| linalg | eigenvalue order and `Divergence` truncation | Wolfram ordering; raises on mismatched dimensions |
| optimisation | `LinearProgramming` used the wrong constraint sense | Wolfram `m.x >= b` |
| lists | `SubsetQ` argument order reversed; `Interleave` truncated silently | fixed |
| statistics | `Mode` returned only the first mode of multimodal data; `Quantile` used NumPy interpolation; `Kurtosis` used excess kurtosis | Wolfram definitions |
| statistics | distribution constructors accepted invalid parameters (`NormalDistribution[0, -1]`) | raise |
| transforms | Fourier transforms used SymPy's convention; SymPy also silently assumes k > 0, so `FourierTransform[1/(1+t^2), t, w]` gave `Sqrt[Pi/2] E^w` and `FourierTransform[Sin[t], t, w]` gave `0` | Wolfram convention, numerically verified at both signs of ω; distributional results raise |
| transforms | `FFT` rounded its output; `InverseFFT` dropped imaginary parts; `HilbertTransform` returned the analytic signal | exact or complex results |
| numerics | `GaussQuadrature` ignored n; `SimpsonsRule` silently changed odd n | real n-point Gauss–Legendre rule; odd n raises |
| logic | `Iff`/`Xnor` coerced symbols to `True`; the CNF/DNF/TruthTable family received undefined SymPy functions instead of Booleans; `BooleanMinimize[a \|\| !a]` was not `True` | stay unevaluated on symbols; exact Boolean algebra |
| graphs | `ChromaticNumber` was greedy (an upper bound only); `BetweennessCentrality` was normalised | exact backtracking; Wolfram unnormalised |
| applied | physical constants were rounded (`LightYear` = 9.461e15); 1-argument constant forms ignored their argument; legacy formulas used g = 9.81 and c = 3e8; `BohrRadius[n]` returned a0 | exact CODATA/SI values, `hbar`/`R`/`F`/`σ`/`Vm` derived exactly; extra arguments raise; `BohrRadius[n]` = n² a0 |
| applied | `CircleArea[-1]` = `Pi`; Carnot efficiency could be negative; finance rates ≤ −100% accepted | domain checks raise |
| plots | `Plot3D`, `ContourPlot`, `ParametricPlot`, `PolarPlot` were shadowed by inert descriptors; `Plot2D` drew an empty figure for bad input | real figures; errors raise |
| names | `Catalan`, `Trace`, `Degree` and `Root` had non-Wolfram meanings (`Catalan[n]` = Catalan number, `Trace[m]` = matrix trace, `Degree[p, x]` = polynomial degree, `Root[a, n]` = a^(1/n)) | Wolfram meanings: `Catalan` constant, evaluation `Trace`, `Degree` = Pi/180, `Root[f, k]` algebraic numbers in Wolfram root order (see below) |
| strings | legacy `Simplify` accepted strings and evaluated them | `sym()` rejects Python strings |
| knowledge | `EntitySearch` returned Wikidata's raw order (an engineer before Q42 for "Douglas Adams"); type hints were appended to the query, which found nothing; `EntityProperties`, `WikipediaSummary`, `Disambiguate` and `Entity` were never wired into the evaluator | relevance-ranked by sitelinks; P31/description filters; network-gated heads |

## Formerly unsupported functions (second pass)

The 8 heads that used to raise an error, and the one quarantined head, now have their Wolfram semantics.
Each has at least 3 differential cases in `tests/library/test_wolfram_fixes.py`.

| Head | Now | Limits |
|---|---|---|
| `Root[f, k]` | k-th root of the polynomial `f[x]`, where `f` is a pure function. Roots are in Wolfram order: real roots ascending, then complex roots by real part, then by \|Im\|, with conjugate pairs adjacent (negative imaginary part first). SymPy's CRootOf order differs and is remapped. The polynomial is reduced to the irreducible factor that carries the root. Linear, quadratic and binomial factors give radicals; otherwise the result is `Root[minpoly &, j]`, which `N` evaluates to any precision. Checked against `mpmath.polyroots`. | Rational coefficients for exact results; approximate coefficients give a number; symbolic coefficients stay unevaluated. `Root[{f, x0}]` and other newer forms are not supported. |
| `Catalan` | Catalan's constant (SymPy `Catalan`); `N[Catalan, n]` gives any precision. | `Catalan[...]` raises and points to `CatalanNumber`. |
| `Degree` | The constant Pi/180: `30 Degree` is `Pi/6` and `N[Degree]` is 0.0174533. | `30 Degree` evaluates to `Pi/6`, whereas Wolfram keeps `30°` until a function needs the value. `Degree[...]` raises and points to `Exponent`. |
| `Trace` | Wolfram evaluation trace. Each form is wrapped in `HoldForm` and subevaluations are nested lists: `Trace[1 + 2 3]` is `{{2 3, 6}, 1 + 6, 7}`. `Trace[expr, form]` filters by pattern. The matrix trace is `Tr`. | Internal steps taken by library functions may show up. `Trace[expr, form, s]` raises. |
| `Refine` | Assumptions-based refinement using SymPy `refine`/`ask`, assumption-carrying symbols, and interval reasoning for one-variable inequalities (`Refine[x > 0, x > 1]` is `True`). | Supported assumptions: inequalities, `Equal`/`Unequal`, `Element[x, Reals \| Integers \| Rationals \| Primes \| Complexes \| Algebraics]`, `&&`, `\|\|`, `!`. Anything else raises. |
| `Assumptions` / `$Assumptions` / `Assuming` | `$Assumptions` defaults to `True`. `Assuming[a, expr]` scopes it. The `Assumptions -> a` option works for `Simplify`, `Refine` and `Integrate`. `Integrate` returns `ConditionalExpression[value, cond]` when SymPy reports a convergence condition; it returns the plain value when the assumptions imply that condition and raises when they contradict it. This also fixes `Integrate[Exp[-a x], {x, 0, Infinity}]`, which previously hit the recursion limit. | `Assumptions[...]` used as a function raises (it is an option name). `FullSimplify` is not implemented. |
| `LaplaceODESolve` | Not a Wolfram built-in; kept as a correct helper. It solves linear constant-coefficient ODEs by Laplace transform and returns `{{y[t] -> sol}}` for t > 0. Initial values that are not given appear as `y[0]`, `y'[0]`, and so on. Every solution is substituted back into the ODE and initial conditions before it is returned. | Non-linear or variable-coefficient equations raise and point to `DSolve`. |
| `InverseZTransform` | Rational `F(z)`: partial fractions in 1/z give `c Binomial[n + k - 1, k - 1] p^n` and `DiscreteDelta[n - m]` terms (causal, n ≥ 0). Each result is checked against 12 coefficients of the series at z = ∞. | Non-rational `F` stays unevaluated; non-causal `F` (one that grows as z → ∞) raises. |
| `YoungTableaux` | `YoungTableaux[shape]`: all standard Young tableaux. `YoungTableaux[n]`: all tableaux with n cells. Counts match `HookLengthFormula` and the involution numbers. | Refuses more than 100,000 tableaux. |
| `DiscreteDelta`, `UnitStep` (new) | Evaluate numerically as in Wolfram (previously inert). | Symbolic arguments stay unevaluated. |

Related fixes made along the way:

* `y'[0]` no longer turns into `Subs[...]` inside arithmetic.
* The parser now reads `a - 1` as `Plus[a, -1]`, Wolfram's FullForm.

## Live-service tests (`-m live_service`)

Before the audit, 12 of 50 failed. **All 12 were caused by our code**, not by the services:

* the ranking and type-hint bugs (5 tests);
* the 4 unwired legacy heads (6 tests);
* one test that expected the unwired module's `"CC0 (Wikidata)"` licence label. The shipped evaluator uses
  `"CC0"` plus the entity URL as provenance, so the test was updated.

After the fixes: **50/50 pass** (run twice). These tests are excluded from default runs and CI
(`addopts = -m 'not live_service'`) because they depend on mutable remote data.

## Wolfram batch 1

Batch 1 adds Wolfram built-ins for lists, strings, string patterns and associations, plus the language objects they need (operator forms, `Key`, `Missing`, `Span`, `UpTo`, `Repeated`, `Optional`, ...). These heads have status `added`. Kernel builtins whose semantics were extended to match Wolfram are recorded as `fixed`. Each has at least 3 differential cases in `tests/library/test_wolfram_batch1.py`, checked against the output examples on the Wolfram reference pages. Overall progress towards the documented Wolfram built-ins is tracked in `docs/wolfram_coverage_roadmap.md`.

## Wolfram batch 2

Batch 2 adds numerics and special functions. Areas `numerics (batch 2)`, `special functions (batch 2)` and `number theory (batch 2)` cover:

- the precision model: number marks `x`p`, `x``a` and `*^`; `Precision`, `Accuracy`, `SetPrecision` and `SetAccuracy`; and precision propagation. A result has the lowest precision among its inputs, and a machine number among the inputs makes the result a machine number.
- `Rationalize`, `Chop`, `RealDigits` and `FromDigits`.
- rounding with steps.
- mpmath-backed special functions, which are exact at special values and arbitrary precision under `N`.
- numerical calculus: `NIntegrate`, `NSum`, `NProduct`, `NSolve`, `NRoots`, `FindRoot`, `NMinimize`, `NMaximize`, `Fit` and `FindFit`.
- `Interpolation` and `NDSolve`, which return `InterpolatingFunction` objects.

Existing heads whose semantics were corrected are recorded as `fixed`. Each head has at least 3 differential cases in `tests/library/test_wolfram_batch2.py`. The cases are checked against values from the Wolfram reference pages, or against mpmath/SciPy computed at higher precision.


## Status counts

| Status | Heads | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| verified | 119 | legacy behaviour confirmed correct against SymPy / mpmath / SciPy / NetworkX / Wolfram references |
| fixed | 502 | legacy behaviour was wrong, crashed or was non-Wolfram; reimplemented and tested |
| promoted | 24 | was quarantined; now properly implemented and tested |
| added | 1417 | new Wolfram built-in added in Wolfram batch 1, differentially tested against Wolfram documentation examples |
| kernel | 25 | implemented by the evaluator kernel before the library is consulted; Wolfram results confirmed |
| error | 0 | raises NotImplementedError with the Wolfram-correct replacement (legacy meaning conflicted with Wolfram) |
| quarantined | 0 | raises NotImplementedError (legacy implementation wrong and not worth fixing) |
| **total reviewed** | **2087** | 620 pre-audit inventory heads, all with a status, plus 1467 promoted/aliased heads |

Remaining unreviewed quarantined names: **5282** (all raise NotImplementedError; see below).

Differential test cases in tests/library: **9668**.

## Every function

Cases = number of differential test cases in which the head occurs.


### algebra

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Assuming` | promoted | 5 | Assuming[assum, expr] evaluates expr with $Assumptions = $Assumptions && assum (held arguments), restoring $Assumptions afterwards, as in Wolfram |
| `Assumptions` | fixed | 13 | Wolfram option name: Assumptions -> cond is accepted by Simplify, Refine and Integrate (default $Assumptions, which is True unless set or changed by Assuming). Assumptions[...] used as a function raises ValueError (legacy Assumptions[expr] always returned {}) |
| `Coefficient` | fixed | 7 | legacy used Poly.nth, failing for non-polynomial expressions and negative powers; now Coefficient[e, x] and Coefficient[e, x, n] on the expanded expression (n may be 0 or negative) |
| `CoefficientList` | fixed | 3 | returned coefficients from the highest power down; Wolfram lists them from x^0 upwards; nested lists for {x, y} |
| `Collect` | verified | 3 | Collect[e, x] and Collect[e, {x, y}] |
| `CompleteTheSquare` | fixed | 3 | a (x - h)^2 + k form (MikoshiLang); non-quadratics now raise instead of returning an Error expression |
| `ComplexExpand` | fixed | 3 | legacy treated variables as complex (ComplexExpand[Re[x + I y]] stayed symbolic); Wolfram assumes all variables are real |
| `Degree` | fixed | 8 | Wolfram constant Degree = Pi/180 (30 Degree -> Pi/6, Sin[30 Degree] -> 1/2, N[Degree] -> 0.0174533); Degree[...] used as a function raises ValueError pointing to Exponent; legacy Degree[p, x] returned the polynomial degree |
| `Discriminant` | verified | 3 | SymPy discriminant (same normalisation as Wolfram) |
| `ExpToTrig` | verified | 3 | rewrite exponentials as trig/hyperbolic functions |
| `PartialFractions` | verified | 3 | MikoshiLang alias of Apart[e, x] |
| `PolynomialDivision` | verified | 3 | {quotient, remainder} (MikoshiLang) |
| `PolynomialQuotient` | verified | 3 | quotient over the rationals (SymPy div) |
| `PolynomialRemainder` | verified | 3 | remainder over the rationals (SymPy div) |
| `PowerExpand` | fixed | 4 | legacy used powsimp (which combines powers, the opposite operation); now Wolfram PowerExpand: (a b)^c -> a^c b^c, (a^b)^c -> a^(b c), Log[a b] -> Log[a] + Log[b], Sqrt[x^2] -> x |
| `QuadraticFormula` | fixed | 4 | roots {(-b + Sqrt[b^2 - 4ac])/(2a), (-b - Sqrt[...])/(2a)} (MikoshiLang); a = 0 now raises instead of dividing by zero |
| `RationalizeDenominator` | fixed | 3 | legacy multiplied by conj(d)/conj(d) (a no-op) and only simplified; now SymPy radsimp, e.g. 1/(1 + Sqrt[2]) -> -1 + Sqrt[2] |
| `Refine` | fixed | 23 | Refine[expr, assum] / Refine[expr] (uses $Assumptions) / Refine[expr, Assumptions -> a]: SymPy refine with assumption-carrying symbols and Q-propositions (Refine[Sqrt[x^2], x > 0] -> x, Refine[Abs[x], x < 0] -> -x, Refine[Sin[n Pi], Element[n, Integers]] -> 0, Refine[x > 0, x > 1] -> True); relations that do not follow stay as they are. Legacy raised |
| `Resultant` | verified | 3 | Sylvester resultant (SymPy) |
| `Simplify` | fixed | 28 | SymPy simplify; Simplify[e, assum] and Simplify[e, Assumptions -> a] (default $Assumptions) refine with the assumptions before and after simplifying (Simplify[Sqrt[x^2], x > 0] -> x); legacy rejected assumptions; W05: options ComplexityFunction, TransformationFunctions, ExcludedForms and Trig -> False |
| `TrigExpand` | verified | 3 | SymPy expand_trig followed by Expand |
| `TrigReduce` | verified | 3 | products and powers of trig functions to multiple angles (SymPy fu TR8) |
| `TrigToExp` | verified | 3 | rewrite trig/hyperbolic functions as exponentials |

### algebra (W05)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `$Assumptions` | added | 7 | system variable: the default assumptions (True); set with Block/Assuming and used by Simplify, Refine, Integrate, AddSides/MultiplySides and the Function* property tests |
| `$MaxPiecewiseCases` | added | 3 | system variable: the maximum number of cases PiecewiseExpand creates (100, as Wolfram); larger expansions raise instead of returning a partial result |
| `$MaxRootDegree` | added | 3 | system variable: the maximum degree of a polynomial in Root objects and root isolation (1024, as Wolfram); RootIntervals, IsolatingInterval and NSolveValues raise for higher degrees |
| `AddSides` | added | 4 | AddSides[rel, x] adds x to every side of an equation, inequation, inequality or chain (also inside And, ConditionalExpression and Piecewise); AddSides[rel1, rel2] adds corresponding sides (equation + relation keeps the relation; two inequalities must point the same way, else the second is reversed); True/False stay |
| `ApartSquareFree` | added | 3 | ApartSquareFree[expr, x] / ApartSquareFree[expr] (one variable): polynomial part plus fractions over the powers of the square-free factors of the denominator (ApartSquareFree[(x^2 - 2)/(x^4 - 2 x^2 + 1)] -> -1/(-1 + x^2)^2 + 1/(-1 + x^2)), exact over the rationals and rational functions of other symbols; threads over lists, equations and inequalities; the result is checked to equal the input; the Modulus and Trig options raise |
| `ApplySides` | added | 3 | ApplySides[f, rel]: applies f to every side of an equation, inequation, inequality or chain (also inside And, ConditionalExpression and Piecewise), purely structurally as Wolfram (no check that an inequality stays valid); True/False stay |
| `BooleanConsecutiveFunction` | added | 4 | BooleanConsecutiveFunction[k, n][x1, ..., xn]: True when k consecutive arguments are True (linear system); {k, True} makes it circular; {{k1, k2}, {n1, n2}} (with optional circularity {{k1, k2}, {c1, c2}}) uses k1 x k2 windows in an n1 x n2 array (arguments flattened row by row or given as an array). Applied to arguments it gives the equivalent DNF Or of window conjunctions (Wolfram keeps the operator unevaluated; BooleanConvert of it gives the same DNF); BooleanConsecutiveFunction[spec, {vars}] / [spec, {vars}, form] give the formula directly |
| `BooleanMaxterms` | added | 4 | BooleanMaxterms[{i1, ...}, {a, b, ...}]: the conjunction of the maxterms ik (maxterm i has the variable where the bit of i is 1 and its negation where it is 0, Wolfram's convention, so it is False exactly at the input 2^n - 1 - i) as a minimal CNF (BooleanMaxterms[{1, 3, 5}, {a, b, c}] -> (!a \|\| c) && (!b \|\| c)); BooleanMaxterms[spec, n] -> BooleanFunction[k, n]; optional form argument |
| `BooleanMinterms` | added | 3 | BooleanMinterms[{i1, ...}, {a, b, ...}]: the Boolean function True exactly at the minterms ik (k's binary digits give the inputs, first variable most significant; 0/1 or True/False vectors also accepted) as a minimal DNF (BooleanMinterms[{1, 3, 5}, {a, b, c}] -> (!a && c) \|\| (!b && c)); BooleanMinterms[spec, n] and BooleanMinterms[{vectors}] give BooleanFunction[k, n]; a third argument "DNF"/"CNF"/"ANF"/... converts with BooleanConvert |
| `BooleanStrings` | added | 6 | Wolfram option name: TextString[expr, BooleanStrings -> {"t", "f"}] writes True and False as the given strings (TextString is implemented in batch W04) |
| `CliffordAlgebra` | added | 5 | CliffordAlgebra[{{p1, ...}, {n1, ...}, {z1, ...}}] (generators squaring to 1, -1 and 0, all pairwise anticommuting) -> a NonCommutativeAlgebra with those relations ("Multiplication" and scalar options accepted); used by NonCommutativeExpand etc. |
| `CoefficientArrays` | added | 3 | CoefficientArrays[polys, {x1, ...}] -> {c0, c1, c2, ...} with polys == c0 + c1.x + c2.x.x + ...: c_k has the coefficient of x_i1 ... x_ik at i1 <= ... <= ik and 0 elsewhere (Wolfram's default), or spread equally over all orderings with "Symmetric" -> True; equations are taken as lhs - rhs; a single polynomial gives a scalar c0. MikoshiLang has no sparse storage, so the arrays are dense (Wolfram returns SparseArray objects; Normal gives these arrays) |
| `ComplexityFunction` | added | 3 | Wolfram option name: Simplify / FullSimplify[..., ComplexityFunction -> f]: of the equivalent forms found, the one with the smallest f[form] is returned (default LeafCount-like) |
| `ComponentExpand` | added | 5 | ComponentExpand[expr] / [expr, assumptions]: VectorSymbol/MatrixSymbol/ArraySymbol with explicit dimensions, and symbols declared with Element[a, Vectors[{n}] \| Matrices[{m, n}] \| Arrays[dims]], are replaced by arrays of their components Indexed[a, {i, ...}] and the expression is evaluated (Det, Dot, Tr, ... then act on explicit arrays); VectorLess/VectorLessEqual/VectorGreater/VectorGreaterEqual become componentwise And |
| `Cubics` | added | 5 | Wolfram option name: Solve[..., Cubics -> False] and Roots[..., Cubics -> False] give Root objects instead of radicals for irreducible cubics (default True) |
| `CylindricalDecomposition` | added | 7 | CylindricalDecomposition[ineqs, {x, y}] / [ineqs, x]: equivalent condition in cylindrical form (x-intervals with y between explicit sections, e.g. -1 < x < 1 && -Sqrt[1 - x^2] < y < Sqrt[1 - x^2]) for polynomial (in)equalities with rational coefficients in one variable, or two variables with degree <= 2 in the second (exact CAD: leading coefficients, discriminants, resultants, exact cell samples); "Function" gives a CylindricalDecompositionFunction; quantifiers, 3+ variables, parameters and other forms ("Boundary", ...) raise |
| `CylindricalDecompositionFunction` | added | 3 | CylindricalDecompositionFunction[cond, {x, y}] (returned by CylindricalDecomposition[..., "Function"]): applied to numbers it tests membership (cdf[x0, y0] -> True/False); Normal gives the cylindrical condition |
| `DirectedInfinity` | added | 4 | DirectedInfinity[z]: an infinite quantity in the direction of z. DirectedInfinity[] and DirectedInfinity[0 \| Indeterminate] -> ComplexInfinity; a positive real -> Infinity, a negative real -> -Infinity; other numeric directions are normalised to z/Abs[z] (DirectedInfinity[1 + I] -> DirectedInfinity[(1 + I)/Sqrt[2]]); symbolic directions stay |
| `DivideSides` | added | 3 | DivideSides[rel, x] / DivideSides[rel] (divides by the right-hand side) / DivideSides[rel1, c == d]: as MultiplySides with division (x != 0 and sign conditions, GenerateConditions and Assumptions options) |
| `ExcludedForms` | added | 3 | Wolfram option name: Simplify / FullSimplify[..., ExcludedForms -> {patt, ...}]: subexpressions matching patt are left untouched (treated as opaque symbols) while the rest is simplified |
| `FindEquationalProof` | added | 5 | FindEquationalProof[thm, axioms]: for an equation thm (optionally ForAll-quantified, proved for fresh constants) and equational axioms (ground or ForAll-quantified equations), searches a proof by congruence closure over the ground axioms plus the instances of the quantified axioms on the subterms of the problem (iterated twice); a found proof is sound and is returned as ProofObject[<\|"Logic" -> "EquationalLogic", "Theorem" -> thm, "Axioms" -> axioms, ...\|>]. Predicate-logic statements (Implies, predicates) raise; a failed search raises (it does not prove the statement false) |
| `FindInstance` | added | 4 | FindInstance[expr, vars] / [expr, vars, dom] / [expr, vars, dom, n] / [expr, vars, n]: up to n instances {{x -> v, ...}} satisfying expr, every one verified by substitution (exactly or at 50 digits). Equations are solved with Solve after giving simple values (0, 1, -1, 2, ...) to the surplus variables; pure inequalities use SymPy solveset (univariate) or a search over simple points; Integers uses a bounded search (equations solved for the last variable); Booleans uses SatisfiabilityInstances. Without domain, variables are real when the condition has inequalities (as Wolfram). {} is returned only when the system is proved to have no solution (0-dimensional Solve or an empty solveset); otherwise a search that finds nothing raises |
| `FromCoefficientRules` | added | 3 | FromCoefficientRules[{{e1, e2, ...} -> c, ...}, {x1, x2, ...}] -> Σ c x1^e1 x2^e2 ... (inverse of CoefficientRules); a single variable may be given without a list |
| `FunctionAnalytic` | added | 4 | FunctionAnalytic[f, x] / [{f, cond}, x] / [f, x, Complexes]: True if f is analytic on the domain (no point of FunctionSingularities in it); same decision procedure as FunctionContinuous |
| `FunctionBijective` | added | 3 | FunctionBijective: FunctionInjective && FunctionSurjective with the same arguments |
| `FunctionContinuous` | added | 4 | FunctionContinuous[f, x] / [{f, cond}, x] / [f, x, Complexes]: True if f is real-valued (over Reals) and continuous on the whole domain, decided from the structural discontinuity condition of whitelisted elementary heads (as FunctionDiscontinuities) intersected with the domain; False only when a bad point provably exists; multivariate: polynomials / entire compositions. Undecidable cases raise |
| `FunctionConvexity` | added | 10 | FunctionConvexity[f, x] / [{f, cond}, x]: 1 convex, -1 concave, 0 both (affine), Indeterminate otherwise; StrictInequalities -> True for strict convexity. Univariate: sign of f'' on the smooth pieces plus the jump of f' at Abs kinks; multivariate: polynomials of degree <= 2 (constant Hessian, eigenvalue signs) and f = g(linear form). Undecidable cases raise |
| `FunctionDiscontinuities` | added | 4 | FunctionDiscontinuities[f, x] / [{f, cond}, x] / [f, x, Complexes]: a condition describing where f is not continuous (over Reals also where it is not real-valued), built structurally from whitelisted heads as Wolfram does: Tan[x] -> Cos[x] == 0, Sqrt[x] -> x <= 0, Floor[g] -> Sin[Pi g] == 0, Gamma[x] -> Sin[Pi x] == 0 && x <= 0, Log[x] over Complexes -> x == 0 \|\| (Im[x] == 0 && Re[x] <= 0); False if there are none; unsupported heads raise |
| `FunctionInjective` | added | 4 | FunctionInjective[f, x] / [{f, xcond}, x] / [{f, xcond, ycond}, x, y] / [f, x, Complexes]: over Reals f is continuous on its natural domain (which must be an interval), so it is injective iff strictly monotone (derivative sign proofs); over Complexes polynomials (degree 1), Exp/Sin/Cos/Tan of linear arguments (False) and Log (True). Parameters, vector maps and other cases raise |
| `FunctionMeromorphic` | added | 4 | FunctionMeromorphic[f, x] / [{f, cond}, x]: True if f is meromorphic on the (complex) domain: poles from rational parts, Tan/Sec/Cot/Csc, Gamma are allowed; branch cuts of Log and fractional powers are not; Exp/Sin/... of arguments with poles (essential singularities) and unsupported heads raise |
| `FunctionMonotonicity` | added | 5 | FunctionMonotonicity[f, x] / [{f, cond}, x]: 1 non-decreasing, -1 non-increasing, 0 constant, Indeterminate otherwise (or if f is not real on the domain); StrictInequalities -> True for strict monotonicity. Univariate elementary functions continuous on an interval domain (derivative sign, with Abs split at its zeros); undecidable cases raise |
| `FunctionPoles` | added | 4 | FunctionPoles[f, x] / [{f, cond}, x]: list of {pole, multiplicity} over the complex numbers for functions built from polynomials, entire functions and Tan/Sec/Cot/Csc of polynomial arguments: explicit poles from denominators (multiplicity from the Laurent leading term, removable points dropped) and, without a condition, periodic families {ConditionalExpression[..., C[1] in Integers], k} for c Tan[g]^k-type terms; functions with branch points stay unevaluated (as in Wolfram); others raise |
| `FunctionSign` | added | 6 | FunctionSign[f, x] / [{f, cond}, x]: 1 if f >= 0, -1 if f <= 0, 0 if f == 0 on the (real) domain, Indeterminate if f takes both signs or is not real somewhere; StrictInequalities -> True asks for f > 0 / f < 0. Univariate elementary functions over Reals; proofs by solveset (Abs split at its zeros) and verified sample points; anything undecidable raises |
| `FunctionSingularities` | added | 3 | FunctionSingularities[f, x] / [{f, cond}, x] / [f, x, Complexes]: a condition describing where f is not analytic (as FunctionDiscontinuities, plus zeros of Abs arguments, boundary points of Sqrt/ArcSin domains: Abs[Tan[2 Sin[x]]] -> Cos[2 Sin[x]] == 0 \|\| Tan[2 Sin[x]] == 0) |
| `FunctionSpace` | added | 3 | Wolfram option name: option of FindSequenceFunction / FindGeneratingFunction ("Polynomial", "RationalFunction", ...); those functions belong to batch W06, which decides whether a setting is supported |
| `FunctionSurjective` | added | 4 | FunctionSurjective[f, x] / [{f, xcond}, x] / [{f, xcond, ycond}, x, y] / [f, x, Complexes]: over Reals the range on the natural domain (SymPy function_range) must contain the codomain (all reals, or the ycond set); over Complexes polynomials of degree >= 1 and Sin/Cos are onto, Exp/Log/Tan are not. Parameters and vector maps raise |
| `GenerateConditions` | added | 8 | Wolfram option name: Integrate, Sum, Product, Limit, FourierTransform, LaplaceTransform, MellinTransform ...: GenerateConditions -> True wraps a result valid only under conditions in ConditionalExpression |
| `GenericCylindricalDecomposition` | added | 3 | GenericCylindricalDecomposition[ineqs, {x, y}]: {full-dimensional part, boundary condition}: the open cells of the CAD (same scope as CylindricalDecomposition) and an Or of polynomial == 0 conditions containing every lower-dimensional solution cell (False if there is none) |
| `GrassmannAlgebra` | added | 4 | GrassmannAlgebra[{x1, ...}] (anticommuting generators with xi^2 == 0; default multiplication Wedge, or "Multiplication" -> NonCommutativeMultiply) -> a NonCommutativeAlgebra with those relations |
| `HermiteDecomposition` | added | 3 | HermiteDecomposition[m] -> {u, r} for an integer matrix: u unimodular (Det = ±1), u.m == r, r the Hermite normal form (row echelon, positive pivots, entries above a pivot reduced into [0, pivot)), unique; u is unique when m is square and nonsingular (otherwise one valid u); non-integer entries raise |
| `HermiteReduce` | added | 3 | HermiteReduce[m]: the Hermite normal form r of an integer matrix (the second element of HermiteDecomposition) |
| `IncludeSingularSolutions` | added | 4 | Wolfram option name: DSolve[ode, y[x], x, IncludeSingularSolutions -> True] adds the singular solutions (envelopes) of a first-order nonlinear ODE, each checked by substitution |
| `IsolatingInterval` | added | 3 | IsolatingInterval[a] / IsolatingInterval[a, dx]: for an exact algebraic number a, an interval {lo, hi} with rational endpoints (Gaussian-rational corners {lower-left, upper-right} for a non-real a) containing a and no other root of its minimal polynomial, of width below dx when given; a rational number gives {a, a}; endpoints are certified but differ from Wolfram's choice |
| `MatrixPolynomialValue` | added | 4 | MatrixPolynomialValue[poly, m, x] -> poly with x replaced by the square matrix m (constant term times the identity, powers as MatrixPower); MatrixPolynomialValue[{c0, c1, ...}, m] -> Σ ck m^k (Horner scheme, exact or numeric) |
| `MaxExtraConditions` | added | 3 | Wolfram option name: Solve[..., MaxExtraConditions -> 0] (the default) gives generic solutions; other settings (n, All, Automatic) raise a clear error |
| `MaxRoots` | added | 3 | option of NSolveValues (and Solve/NSolve in Wolfram): the maximum number of roots returned; MaxRoots -> n keeps the first n solutions, Infinity keeps all |
| `MultiplySides` | added | 3 | MultiplySides[rel, x] / MultiplySides[rel1, c == d]: multiplies every side; for an inequality the direction follows the sign of x (a negative x swaps the sides, a == b > -1/5 > c times -5 -> chain of swapped relations); with unknown sign GenerateConditions -> All (default) gives Piecewise[{{rel x, x > 0}, {reversed, x < 0}}, rel], True/Automatic give ConditionalExpression[..., x > 0], False the unconditional form and None stays unevaluated; for equations the condition is x != 0 (Automatic drops it); Assumptions and $Assumptions decide the sign |
| `NSolveValues` | added | 5 | NSolveValues[eqns, vars] / NSolveValues[eqns, vars, dom]: the values of the NSolve solutions ({v1, ...} for one variable, {{x1, y1, ...}, ...} for several), with NSolve's options (WorkingPrecision) and MaxRoots -> n (at most n solutions, Wolfram order) |
| `NonCommutativeAlgebra` | added | 3 | NonCommutativeAlgebra[] / NonCommutativeAlgebra[<\|prop -> val, ...\|>] with "Multiplication", "Addition", "Unity", "Zero", "ScalarVariables", "CommutativeVariables", "Generators", "Relations", "CommutationRelations": an inert algebra description used by the NonCommutative* functions (validated; unknown properties raise). The {mult, n} shorthand is not supported |
| `NonCommutativeCollect` | added | 3 | NonCommutativeCollect[poly, x] / [poly, {x, Left}] / [poly, {x, Right}] / [..., alg]: groups the expanded terms by the pattern of x-factors (default: x-runs with one varying gap between them; Left: leading x power; Right: trailing x power) and factors the varying part, e.g. x**(3y + 4z)**x**x; terms whose patterns differ in more than one gap are left separate |
| `NonCommutativeExpand` | added | 17 | NonCommutativeExpand[expr] / [expr, alg]: expands ** (or the algebra's multiplication/addition) products over sums, moving scalars (numbers, ScalarVariables) to coefficients and CommutativeVariables to the front of each word, and reduces modulo the algebra relations (Groebner basis). Repeated factors are written out (a ** a) where Wolfram displays a power |
| `NonCommutativeGroebnerBasis` | added | 3 | NonCommutativeGroebnerBasis[{p1, ...}, vars] / [polys, alg]: reduced two-sided Groebner basis (Buchberger-Mora with overlap S-polynomials; monic, sorted by leading word) for the monomial order of vars ({x, y} elimination, {{x, y}} degree-lexicographic), together with the algebra relations; computations that do not close within degree 12 raise (noncommutative bases can be infinite); one-sided (Left/Right) bases are not supported |
| `NonCommutativeMonomialList` | added | 3 | NonCommutativeMonomialList[poly, vars] / [poly, alg] / [poly, vars, alg]: the terms (coefficient times word) in decreasing monomial order: {x, y} is an elimination order (degree in the last variable first, then the word, later variables larger), {{x, y}} degree-lexicographic; reduced modulo the algebra relations first |
| `NonCommutativeMultiply` | added | 3 | a ** b ** c (parsed with precedence between Dot and Power): associative (Flat) noncommutative product that otherwise stays symbolic; NonCommutativeExpand and the NonCommutative* functions expand and reduce it |
| `NonCommutativePolynomialQ` | added | 3 | NonCommutativePolynomialQ[expr, vars] / [expr, vars, alg]: True if expr is a noncommutative polynomial in vars with scalar (or commutative-variable) coefficients |
| `NonCommutativePolynomialReduce` | added | 3 | NonCommutativePolynomialReduce[p, {q1, ...}, vars] / [..., alg]: {{f1, ...}, r} with pure functions fi (sums of c u ** #1 ** v) so that p == f1[q1] + ... + r and no word of r contains a leading word of a qi (reduction in the monomial order of vars); verified by re-expansion |
| `NonCommutativePolynomialReduction` | added | 3 | NonCommutativePolynomialReduction[p, {q1, ...}, vars] / [..., alg]: the remainder of NonCommutativePolynomialReduce |
| `NonCommutativeVariables` | added | 3 | NonCommutativeVariables[expr] / [expr, alg]: sorted list of the noncommutative generators in expr (scalars, ScalarVariables, CommutativeVariables and the unity excluded); lists are searched elementwise |
| `PadeApproximant` | added | 4 | PadeApproximant[f, {x, x0, {m, n}}] / {x, x0, n} (= {n, n}): the rational function P/Q with deg P <= m, deg Q <= n, Q(x0) = 1 whose series agrees with f to the highest possible order (extended Euclidean algorithm, so degenerate cases such as PadeApproximant[Cos[x], {x, 0, 1}] -> 1 are handled); a pole or zero (x - x0)^k of f is factored out as Wolfram does (Exp[x]/x); x0 may be symbolic or Infinity (series in 1/x); fractional powers (Puiseux series) raise; the result's series is checked |
| `PartialFractionElements` | added | 4 | PartialFractionElements[f, x] -> <\|"Decomposition", "PolynomialPart", "FractionalPart", "PolynomialCoefficients", "FractionalCoefficients" ({{pole, multiplicity, {c_m, ..., c_1}}, ...} with c_k the coefficient of 1/(x - pole)^k), "Poles", "PoleMultiplicities"\|>, the complete decomposition over the algebraic numbers (poles in radicals); PartialFractionElements[f, x, prop] gives one property; poles are sorted as Wolfram (real ascending, then complex); the decomposition is checked to equal f |
| `Piecewise` | added | 7 | Piecewise[{{v1, c1}, ...}, def] (HoldAll, as Wolfram): conditions are evaluated in order, False cases are dropped, the first True case gives its value (later cases are dropped), Piecewise[{}, d] -> d, the default is 0; values are evaluated only when they can be returned. Symbolic cases stay; Integrate, D, Limit and PiecewiseExpand work with it through SymPy's Piecewise |
| `PiecewiseExpand` | added | 4 | PiecewiseExpand[expr] / [expr, cond] / [expr, Reals]: nested Piecewise, If, Max, Min, UnitStep, Boole, Ramp, Clip, KroneckerDelta (and Abs, Sign over Reals) become one Piecewise (SymPy piecewise_fold plus condition simplification; cases are equivalent to Wolfram's, conditions may be written differently); Floor/Ceiling/Round of a univariate expression are expanded when the condition bounds its range (at most $MaxPiecewiseCases cases); inequalities with piecewise sides are expanded to conditions |
| `PolynomialExpressionQ` | added | 4 | PolynomialExpressionQ[e, x \| {x, ...}] / [e, vars, test]: True if e is built from the variables and coefficients free of them with Plus, Times and non-negative integer Power (purely structural, as Wolfram: x Sin[x]^2 + x Cos[x]^2 is not, x - 1/x is not; variables may be any expressions such as x[1] or Sin[x]); test is applied to the coefficients |
| `PolynomialExtendedGCD` | added | 3 | PolynomialExtendedGCD[p, q, x] -> {g, {r, s}} with r p + s q == g, g the monic GCD and deg r < deg q - deg g, deg s < deg p - deg g (unique cofactors; coefficients may be rational functions of other symbols, as Wolfram); Modulus -> prime works over GF(p) with representatives 0..p-1; checked |
| `PolynomialHermiteDecomposition` | added | 3 | PolynomialHermiteDecomposition[m] / [m, x] -> {u, h}: u.m == h with u unimodular (constant nonzero determinant) and h upper triangular (row echelon) with every entry above a pivot of lower degree than the pivot, over the rational numbers (or rational functions of other symbols). The Hermite form is canonical only up to constant multiples of the rows; MikoshiLang makes the pivots monic (Wolfram's row scaling differs). Modulus and approximate coefficients raise |
| `PolynomialHermiteReduce` | added | 3 | PolynomialHermiteReduce[m] / [m, x]: the Hermite form h of a univariate polynomial matrix (second element of PolynomialHermiteDecomposition; pivots monic) |
| `PolynomialReduction` | added | 3 | PolynomialReduction[p, {p1, ...}, {x1, ...}]: the remainder of p on division by the pi (fully reduced with respect to their leading terms; unique when the pi are a Gröbner basis), coefficients in the field of rational functions of the other symbols; MonomialOrder -> Lexicographic \| DegreeLexicographic \| DegreeReverseLexicographic, Modulus -> prime; the identity p == Σ ai pi + r is checked |
| `PolynomialSmithDecomposition` | added | 3 | PolynomialSmithDecomposition[m] / [m, x] -> {u, s, v}: u.m.v == s with u, v unimodular and s diagonal with each diagonal entry dividing the next (the invariant factors), over the rationals or rational functions of other symbols; invariant factors are unique up to constants and are made monic here (Wolfram leaves constant multiples). Checked |
| `PolynomialSmithReduce` | added | 3 | PolynomialSmithReduce[m] / [m, x]: the Smith form s (monic invariant factors on the diagonal) |
| `PolynomialSumOfSquaresList` | added | 4 | PolynomialSumOfSquaresList[p, {x, ...}]: polynomials {q1, ...} with p == q1^2 + q2^2 + ... (verified exactly) from an exact LDL^T factorization of a positive semidefinite Gram matrix over the half-degree monomials (ordered by degree, later variables first, so x^2 + x y + y^2 -> {x/2 + y, Sqrt[3] x/2} as Wolfram); free Gram entries (at most 3) are searched over simple rationals. Wolfram picks a different decomposition when the Gram matrix is not unique. Raises when no decomposition is found (p may not be a sum of squares) |
| `ProofObject` | added | 3 | ProofObject[<\|...\|>] returned by FindEquationalProof; proof["Theorem"], ["Theorems"], ["Axioms"], ["Logic"], ["Method"], ["Properties"] (ProofGraph/ProofDataset/ProofNotebook are not supported and raise) |
| `Quartics` | added | 3 | Wolfram option name: Solve/Roots[..., Quartics -> False] give Root objects instead of radicals for irreducible quartics (default True) |
| `RationalExpressionQ` | added | 3 | RationalExpressionQ[e, x \| {x, ...}] / [e, vars, test]: True if e is built from the variables and coefficients free of them with Plus, Times and integer Power (structural, as Wolfram: Sqrt[x] is not) |
| `RootIntervals` | added | 3 | RootIntervals[{p1, p2, ...}] / RootIntervals[p] / RootIntervals[polys, Complexes] -> {intervals, which}: disjoint isolating intervals with rational endpoints for all distinct real roots (with Complexes, also rectangles {lower-left, upper-right} with Gaussian-rational corners for the non-real roots), and for each interval the positions of the polynomials having that root. Each real interval contains exactly one root in its interior, or is {r, r} for a rational root; isolation is certified from exact root separation; the interval endpoints differ from Wolfram's (they are not unique) |
| `SemialgebraicComponentInstances` | added | 3 | SemialgebraicComponentInstances[ineqs, {x, y}] / [ineqs, x]: sample points {{x -> a, y -> b}, ...}, at least one in each connected component of the solution set (one per true CAD cell, so possibly more than one per component); same scope as CylindricalDecomposition |
| `SolveAlways` | added | 3 | SolveAlways[eqns, vars]: values of the parameters for which the equations (and inequations) hold for all values of vars: every coefficient of the numerators as polynomials in vars must vanish (an inequation p != 0 needs p to be a nonzero constant); the parameter system is solved exactly and each solution is substituted back. Non-polynomial dependence on vars and Or raise |
| `StrictInequalities` | added | 7 | Wolfram option name: FunctionSign, FunctionMonotonicity, FunctionConvexity: StrictInequalities -> True asks for strict sign / monotonicity / convexity (default False) |
| `SubresultantPolynomialRemainders` | added | 3 | SubresultantPolynomialRemainders[p, q, x]: the subresultant polynomial remainder sequence {p, q, ...} (each term a multiple of the corresponding Euclidean remainder; the last is a multiple of the GCD), computed with SymPy's determinant-based subresultant PRS; Modulus raises |
| `SubresultantPolynomials` | added | 3 | SubresultantPolynomials[p, q, x]: the subresultant polynomials {S_0, ..., S_n} (n = deg q <= deg p) from the Sylvester submatrices, S_0 = Resultant[p, q, x] and S_n = lc(q)^(deg p - deg q - 1) q; deg p < deg q and Modulus raise |
| `Subresultants` | added | 3 | Subresultants[p, q, x]: the principal subresultant coefficients {psc_0, ..., psc_n} (n = deg q <= deg p): psc_j is the leading coefficient of the j-th subresultant (determinant of the Sylvester submatrix), psc_0 = Resultant and psc_n = lc(q)^(deg p - deg q); the first k vanish exactly when p and q have k common roots; deg p < deg q and Modulus raise |
| `SubtractSides` | added | 3 | SubtractSides[rel, x] subtracts x from every side; SubtractSides[rel] subtracts the right-hand side (a == b -> a - b == 0); SubtractSides[rel1, rel2] subtracts corresponding sides (an inequality is reversed when subtracted) |
| `TransformationFunctions` | added | 3 | Wolfram option name: Simplify / FullSimplify[..., TransformationFunctions -> {Automatic, f, ...}]: f is applied to the forms found (Automatic = the built-in transformations) and the simplest result kept |
| `Trig` | added | 7 | Wolfram option name: Expand / Factor[..., Trig -> True] expand multiple angles and treat Sin, Cos, ... as polynomial elements (Expand[Sin[x + y], Trig -> True] -> Cos[y] Sin[x] + Cos[x] Sin[y]); Simplify[..., Trig -> False] simplifies without trigonometric identities |
| `TrigFactorList` | added | 3 | TrigFactorList[expr]: {{c, 1}, {f1, e1}, ...} with expr == c Π fi^ei, after writing Tan, Cot, Sec, Csc (and hyperbolic analogues) in Sin and Cos, factoring as polynomials in the trigonometric functions and simplifying each factor with the Pythagorean identities (TrigFactorList[Cosh[x]^2 - Cosh[x]^4] -> {{-1, 1}, {Sinh[x], 2}, {Cosh[x], 2}}); the product is checked to equal expr; the chosen factors can differ from Wolfram's sum-to-product forms |
| `UnateQ` | added | 4 | UnateQ[f]: True if the Boolean function f is positive unate (monotone non-decreasing) in all its variables; UnateQ[f, {x, !y, ...}] checks positive unateness in x and negative unateness in y (only the listed variables); pure functions and BooleanFunction take a list of True (positive), False (negative) or _ (ignore) per argument; decided from the complete truth table |
| `Undefined` | added | 3 | symbol for an undefined value: ConditionalExpression[e, False] and an empty Piecewise case give Undefined; the Function* tests treat it as a point outside the domain. (Arithmetic propagation 1 + Undefined -> Undefined is not automatic: the sum stays as it is, it is never given a value) |
| `VerifySolutions` | added | 3 | Wolfram option name: accepted by Solve (default Automatic). MikoshiLang always verifies solutions of equations involving radicals, so VerifySolutions -> False never adds the spurious solutions Wolfram can return (the answer is the verified subset) |
| `WeylAlgebra` | added | 3 | WeylAlgebra[{{x1, ...}, {d1, ...}}]: di ** xi == xi ** di + 1, all other pairs commute -> a NonCommutativeAlgebra (normal order: variables before derivations) |
| `ZeroTest` | added | 5 | Wolfram option name: RowReduce, MatrixRank, NullSpace[..., ZeroTest -> f]: f[entry] decides whether a pivot candidate is zero (default Automatic: exact) |

### algebra (batch 4)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ArgMax` | added | 3 | ArgMax[f, x] / ArgMax[{f, cons}, x]: a maximising point, as Maximize |
| `ArgMin` | added | 3 | ArgMin[f, x] / ArgMin[{f, cons}, x]: a minimising point (the boundary point if the infimum is not attained), as Minimize; {x, y} gives a list |
| `CoefficientRules` | added | 4 | CoefficientRules[p, {x1, ...}] -> {{e1, ...} -> c, ...} for the nonzero terms, in lexicographic order (third argument: "Lexicographic", "DegreeLexicographic", "DegreeReverseLexicographic"); CoefficientRules[p] uses Variables[p]; CoefficientRules[0, vars] -> {} |
| `CountRoots` | added | 4 | CountRoots[p, x]: number of real roots counted with multiplicity; CountRoots[p, {x, a, b}] in the closed interval [a, b] (real a, b) or the closed rectangle with corners a, b (complex a, b); exact (Sturm sequences, SymPy) |
| `Decompose` | added | 3 | Decompose[p, x] -> {p1, ..., pn} with p = p1[p2[...pn[x]]] (functional decomposition, SymPy); indecomposable p gives {p}; checked by composing back |
| `Element` | added | 63 | Element[x, dom] for dom in Reals, Integers, Rationals, Algebraics, Complexes, Primes, Booleans: True or False when decidable for explicit numbers (Element[Sqrt[2], Rationals] -> False, Element[Pi, Algebraics] -> False), unevaluated for symbols (used in assumptions); Element[{x1, ...}, dom] tests every element (False if any is False) |
| `Eliminate` | added | 4 | Eliminate[eqns, vars]: polynomial equations with vars eliminated (lexicographic Gröbner basis elimination), returned as p == 0 relations joined with And (Wolfram may arrange the two sides differently); True when nothing remains |
| `Expand` | fixed | 31 | Wolfram Expand: products and positive integer powers are distributed through nested sums and products ((x + 1)^2 (y + 1) -> 1 + 2 x + x^2 + y + 2 x y + x^2 y; legacy left (1 + x)^2 unexpanded inside products), not inside function arguments or denominators; Expand[e, x] expands only the parts containing x; threads over lists and equations; Modulus/Trig options raise; W05: option Trig -> True (expand multiple angles; trigonometric functions treated as polynomial elements) |
| `ExpandAll` | added | 3 | ExpandAll[e]: Expand applied to every subexpression, including function arguments and denominators (ExpandAll[Sin[(x + 1)^2]] -> Sin[1 + 2 x + x^2]); ExpandAll[e, x] only parts containing x |
| `ExpandDenominator` | added | 3 | expands the denominator of a rational expression (ExpandDenominator[1/((x + 1) (x - 1))] -> 1/(-1 + x^2)), numerator kept |
| `ExpandNumerator` | added | 3 | expands the numerator of a rational expression (Numerator/Denominator as Wolfram), denominator kept |
| `Exponent` | fixed | 8 | Exponent[p, x]: highest power of x (Exponent[0, x] = -Infinity), also for sums of non-integer powers; batch 4: Exponent[p, x, h] applies h to the list of distinct exponents (in increasing order, 0 for a constant term): Exponent[x^3 + x, x, Min] -> 1, Exponent[1 + x^2, x, List] -> {0, 2}; threads over lists |
| `Factor` | fixed | 14 | factors over the rationals (SymPy factor); batch 4: Extension -> a or {a, ...} (algebraic extension), GaussianIntegers -> True, Modulus -> p (prime; coefficients shown in 0..p-1 as Wolfram); threads over lists and equations; note the kernel distributes a numeric content over a single sum (2 (x^2 - 2) displays expanded); W05: option Trig -> True (expand multiple angles; trigonometric functions treated as polynomial elements) |
| `FactorList` | added | 4 | FactorList[p] -> {{c, 1}, {f1, e1}, ...}: the numeric content first (always present, 1 when trivial), then the irreducible factors over the rationals with exponents; Extension, GaussianIntegers, Modulus |
| `FactorSquareFree` | added | 3 | square-free factorisation (SymPy sqf): FactorSquareFree[x^3 - x^2 - x + 1] -> (-1 + x)^2 (1 + x) |
| `FactorSquareFreeList` | added | 3 | FactorSquareFreeList[p] -> {{c, 1}, {f1, 1}, {f2, 2}, ...} (numeric content first, then square-free factors with their multiplicities) |
| `FactorTerms` | added | 3 | FactorTerms[p] pulls out the overall numeric factor (FactorTerms[2 x^2 - 4] = 2 (-2 + x^2), which the kernel displays distributed); FactorTerms[p, x] also pulls out factors free of x; the leading coefficient of the remaining polynomial is made positive |
| `FactorTermsList` | added | 3 | FactorTermsList[p] -> {numeric factor, polynomial}; FactorTermsList[p, {x, ...}] -> {numeric factor, factor free of the variables, remaining polynomial} |
| `FullSimplify` | added | 8 | FullSimplify[e] / FullSimplify[e, assum]: the smallest (operation count, then length) of a wider set of equivalence-preserving rewrites than Simplify (simplify, factor, cancel, together, expand, trigsimp, fu, powsimp, radsimp, sqrtdenest, gammasimp, expand_func, exp rewriting, logcombine), applied after and before refining with the assumptions; every candidate is checked numerically against the input; threads over lists and equations; W05: options ComplexityFunction, TransformationFunctions, ExcludedForms and Trig -> False |
| `FunctionDomain` | added | 5 | FunctionDomain[f, x]: the largest real domain of f as a condition on x (FunctionDomain[1/x, x] -> x < 0 \|\| x > 0, FunctionDomain[Log[x^2 - 1], x] -> x < -1 \|\| x > 1) for elementary f (SymPy continuous domain; functions with jumps such as Floor raise); checked by sampling; other domains raise |
| `FunctionExpand` | added | 3 | FunctionExpand[e]: special functions rewritten in simpler functions (SymPy expand_func: Gamma[x + 3] -> x (1 + x) (2 + x) Gamma[x], Binomial[n, 2] -> (n - 1) n/2, Beta -> Gamma ratio, PolyGamma and Zeta at special arguments), checked numerically; FunctionExpand[e, assum] refines with assumptions |
| `FunctionPeriod` | added | 5 | FunctionPeriod[f, x]: the fundamental period (FunctionPeriod[Sin[x]^2, x] -> Pi; SymPy's periodicity can return a multiple, so T/k is tried for k = 2..24 and verified symbolically/numerically); non-periodic polynomials and rational functions give 0 (Wolfram); undecided cases stay unevaluated |
| `FunctionRange` | added | 4 | FunctionRange[f, x, y]: the range of the real function f as a condition on y (FunctionRange[x^2, x, y] -> y >= 0, FunctionRange[Sin[x], x, y] -> -1 <= y <= 1) via SymPy function_range over the domain; elementary f in one variable without parameters |
| `GroebnerBasis` | added | 5 | GroebnerBasis[{p1, ...}, {x1, ...}] (lexicographic, the default Wolfram MonomialOrder; also DegreeLexicographic, DegreeReverseLexicographic): reduced basis, each polynomial made primitive with integer coefficients and a positive leading coefficient, listed from the smallest leading monomial (Wolfram's order); GroebnerBasis[polys, vars, elims] eliminates elims; equations are moved to one side; Modulus -> p |
| `HornerForm` | added | 3 | HornerForm[p] / HornerForm[p, x] / HornerForm[p, {x, y}]: nested Horner form (SymPy horner); rational functions: HornerForm of numerator and denominator |
| `IrreduciblePolynomialQ` | added | 4 | True if the polynomial cannot be factored over the rationals (numeric content ignored, as units of Q); constants give False; Extension, GaussianIntegers, Modulus options |
| `MaxValue` | added | 3 | MaxValue[f, x] / MaxValue[{f, cons}, x]: the maximum (or supremum) value, as Maximize |
| `Maximize` | fixed | 5 | Maximize[f, x] (unconstrained, as before) and batch 4: Maximize[{f, cons}, x] for one variable, as Minimize |
| `MinValue` | added | 3 | MinValue[f, x] / MinValue[{f, cons}, x]: the minimum (or infimum) value, as Minimize |
| `MinimalPolynomial` | added | 4 | MinimalPolynomial[s, x]: the minimal polynomial of the algebraic number s over the rationals, primitive with integer coefficients and positive leading coefficient (Wolfram); MinimalPolynomial[s] gives a pure function; transcendental input raises |
| `Minimize` | fixed | 8 | Minimize[f, x] / Minimize[f, {x, ...}] (unconstrained, as before) and batch 4: Minimize[{f, cons}, x] for one variable with polynomial/rational/Abs constraints: global minimum over the feasible set from critical points, closed endpoints and limits at open ends and ±Infinity -> {min, {x -> x0}}; an infimum that is not attained gives the boundary point ({0, {x -> 0}} for {x, x > 0}; {-Infinity, {x -> -Infinity}} when unbounded), as Wolfram (which also warns); constraints in several variables raise |
| `MonomialList` | added | 3 | MonomialList[p, {x1, ...}] -> the terms (coefficient times monomial) in lexicographic order (third argument: monomial order); MonomialList[p] uses Variables[p] |
| `PolynomialGCD` | added | 4 | greatest common divisor of polynomials with rational coefficients (rational functions: gcd of numerators over lcm of denominators, as Wolfram); Modulus -> p; PolynomialGCD[p] = p |
| `PolynomialLCM` | added | 3 | least common multiple of polynomials (factored, as Wolfram); Modulus -> p |
| `PolynomialMod` | added | 4 | PolynomialMod[p, m]: integer m reduces every coefficient into 0..m-1 (rational coefficients need m coprime to the denominators); a polynomial m in one variable with a unit leading coefficient gives the remainder of p modulo m (no division by the leading coefficient otherwise: raises); lists of moduli apply in turn |
| `PolynomialQ` | added | 5 | PolynomialQ[e, x] / PolynomialQ[e, {x, ...}]: True if e is a polynomial in the variables (coefficients may be anything free of them; nonnegative integer powers only); PolynomialQ[e] in all its variables |
| `PolynomialQuotientRemainder` | added | 3 | PolynomialQuotientRemainder[p, q, x] -> {quotient, remainder} with p = quotient q + remainder and deg remainder < deg q (rational coefficients); a zero divisor raises |
| `PolynomialReduce` | added | 3 | PolynomialReduce[p, {p1, ...}, {x1, ...}] -> {{a1, ...}, b} with p = Σ ai pi + b and b reduced with respect to the leading terms (lexicographic order, SymPy reduced; for a Gröbner basis the remainder is unique) |
| `PossibleZeroQ` | added | 4 | PossibleZeroQ[e]: True if e simplifies to 0 or is numerically 0 (40 digits) at several sample points of its variables, False otherwise (a heuristic, as in Wolfram) |
| `Reduce` | added | 16 | Reduce[cond, x] / Reduce[cond, x, dom] in one variable: polynomial equations over the complexes (x == r1 \|\| ..., distinct roots), periodic equations (C[1] ∈ Integers && (x == ...)), polynomial, rational and Abs inequalities with And/Or over the reals (inequalities imply real x, as Wolfram) as interval conditions, and over the integers (finite ranges listed, else x ∈ Integers with bounds); linear equations with symbolic coefficients split into cases ((b == 0 && a == 0) \|\| (a != 0 && x == -b/a)); polynomial equation systems in several variables (Or of And of equations). Results of inequalities are checked by sampling; quantifiers and multivariate inequalities raise |
| `Roots` | added | 6 | Roots[lhs == rhs, x] -> x == r1 \|\| x == r2 \|\| ... for polynomial equations (repeated roots repeated, as Wolfram; radicals up to degree 4 unless Cubics/Quartics -> False, Root objects beyond); a single root gives x == r; no roots gives False |
| `Solve` | fixed | 30 | Solve[eqns, vars], Solve[eqns, vars, dom] (Reals, Integers, Complexes): polynomial roots with multiplicity in Wolfram order (real ascending, then complex), radicals up to degree 4 (Cubics/Quartics -> False give Root objects) and Root[...] for higher degree (legacy returned {} for x^5 - x + 1 == 0), periodic families as ConditionalExpression[..., C[1] ∈ Integers] (legacy returned only {0, Pi} for Sin[x] == 0), denominators' zeros excluded, polynomial/rational systems (underdetermined systems solved for the later variables, as Wolfram), linear and finite quadratic Diophantine equations over the Integers; every solution is substituted back; inequalities raise (use Reduce); transcendental equations SymPy cannot solve completely raise instead of returning a partial list |
| `SolveValues` | added | 3 | SolveValues[eqns, x] -> {v1, v2, ...}; SolveValues[eqns, {x, y}] -> {{x1, y1}, ...}; same solver and checks as Solve (domains, multiplicity, periodic families) |
| `SymmetricPolynomial` | added | 3 | SymmetricPolynomial[k, {x1, ..., xn}]: the k-th elementary symmetric polynomial (0 for k > n, 1 for k = 0) |
| `SymmetricReduction` | added | 3 | SymmetricReduction[f, {x1, ..., xn}] -> {p, q}: p the symmetric part written in the elementary symmetric polynomials of the xi, q the remainder, f = p + q; SymmetricReduction[f, xs, {s1, ..., sn}] writes p in the si; checked by expansion |
| `ToRadicals` | added | 4 | ToRadicals[e] rewrites Root objects of degree <= 4 (or solvable by radicals in SymPy) as radicals, matched to the root numerically; other roots stay Root[...] |
| `ToRules` | added | 3 | ToRules[eqns]: x == a && y == b -> {x -> a, y -> b}; a disjunction gives one rule list per case as a Sequence (so {ToRules[...]} is a Solve-style list); False gives nothing |
| `TrigFactor` | added | 3 | TrigFactor[e]: sums of sines and cosines turned into products (sum-to-product, Fu's TR9/TR10i), then factored (TrigFactor[Sin[x] + Sin[y]] -> 2 Cos[x/2 - y/2] Sin[x/2 + y/2]); the result is checked numerically; threads over lists |
| `Variables` | added | 3 | Variables[poly]: the independent variables (symbols and non-polynomial subexpressions such as Sin[z]) of a polynomial or rational expression, sorted (Variables[x^2 + y Sin[z]] -> {x, y, Sin[z]}) |

### associations (batch 1)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `AssociateTo` | added | 3 | AssociateTo[a, k -> v] (or a list of rules) updates the association stored in symbol a and returns it |
| `Association` | added | 7 | normalises <\|...\|>: lists of rules and nested associations are flattened in, a repeated key keeps its first position and takes the last value; non-rule arguments raise |
| `AssociationMap` | added | 4 | AssociationMap[f, {k1, ...}] gives <\|k1 -> f[k1], ...\|>; on an association f is applied to each rule and must return a rule |
| `AssociationQ` | added | 3 | True for a valid association |
| `AssociationThread` | added | 4 | AssociationThread[{k...}, {v...}] or [{k...} -> {v...}]; lengths must agree; repeated keys keep the last value |
| `DeleteMissing` | added | 3 | drops Missing[...] elements of a list (or entries of an association); DeleteMissing[list, n] works down to level n (n a positive integer or Infinity) |
| `KeyComplement` | added | 3 | entries of the first association whose keys are in none of the others |
| `KeyDrop` | added | 3 | entries whose keys are not listed; rule lists and lists of associations thread |
| `KeyDropFrom` | added | 3 | KeyDropFrom[a, key or keys] removes entries from the association stored in a |
| `KeyExistsQ` | added | 3 | True if the key is present (SameQ on keys) |
| `KeyFreeQ` | added | 3 | True if no key matches the pattern |
| `KeyIntersection` | added | 3 | each association restricted to the keys common to all, in the order of the first association |
| `KeyMap` | added | 3 | KeyMap[f, assoc] applies f to every key (later entries win when keys collide) |
| `KeyMemberQ` | added | 3 | True if some key matches the pattern |
| `KeySelect` | added | 3 | entries whose key k gives crit[k] True |
| `KeySort` | added | 3 | entries ordered by canonical order of keys, or by the ordering function p |
| `KeySortBy` | added | 3 | entries ordered by canonical order of f[key] (stable for ties) |
| `KeyTake` | added | 3 | entries for the given keys in the order the keys are listed (absent keys ignored); rule lists give rule lists; lists of associations thread |
| `KeyUnion` | added | 3 | each association extended to the union of keys (in order of first appearance); absent keys get Missing["KeyAbsent", k] (or f[k] with KeyUnion[list, f]) |
| `KeyValueMap` | added | 3 | {f[k1, v1], f[k2, v2], ...} |
| `Keys` | added | 7 | keys of an association in order; Keys of a rule list gives each rule's key; lists of associations thread; Keys[a, f] applies f to each key |
| `Lookup` | added | 7 | Lookup[assoc, key] (Missing["KeyAbsent", key] if absent), Lookup[assoc, key, default], a list of keys gives a list, a list of associations threads; rule lists act as associations |
| `Merge` | added | 4 | Merge[{a1, a2, ...}, f] gives <\|k -> f[{values for k}]\|> with keys in order of first appearance; rule lists act as associations |
| `MissingQ` | added | 3 | True for Missing[...] expressions |
| `Values` | added | 3 | values of an association in order (RuleDelayed values evaluated); rule lists and lists of associations thread; Values[a, f] applies f to each value |

### calculus

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ArcCurvature` | added | 4 | ArcCurvature[{x1[t], ..., xn[t]}, t] = Sqrt[\|r'\|^2 \|r''\|^2 - (r'.r'')^2]/\|r'\|^3 (t real; gives \|x'y'' - y'x''\|/(x'^2 + y'^2)^(3/2) in 2D, \|r' x r''\|/\|r'\|^3 in 3D); ArcCurvature[f, x] for the graph y = f(x): Abs[f'']/(1 + f'^2)^(3/2) |
| `ArcLength` | fixed | 8 | ArcLength[f, {x, a, b}] = ∫ Sqrt[1 + f'^2] (graph of f, MikoshiLang); batch 4: Wolfram's parametric form ArcLength[{x1[t], ..., xn[t]}, {t, a, b}] = ∫ Sqrt[Σ xi'^2] dt, ArcLength[Line[{p1, ...}]] (polyline), ArcLength[Circle[c, r]] = 2 π r; numeric input gives numeric output |
| `CriticalPoints` | fixed | 3 | solutions of grad f = 0 as rule lists (real and complex, as Solve); legacy silently dropped solutions SymPy returned as tuples (most multivariate cases) |
| `Curl` | fixed | 7 | 3D curl and the 2D scalar curl; batch 4: Curl[F, vars, chart] for Polar (scalar), Cylindrical and Spherical (orthonormal components, Wolfram) |
| `DefiniteIntegral` | fixed | 4 | definite integral DefiniteIntegral[f, {x, a, b}] (MikoshiLang); legacy silently computed an indefinite integral for a bare variable, now that raises |
| `DirectionalDerivative` | fixed | 3 | grad f . u/\|u\| with an exact unit vector; legacy converted the direction to floats and did not normalise it (so it returned grad f . u) |
| `Div` | fixed | 6 | Div[{f1, ..., fn}, {x1, ..., xn}] (Cartesian); batch 4: Div[F, vars, chart] for Polar, Cylindrical, Spherical (orthonormal components, Wolfram) |
| `Divergence` | fixed | 3 | sum of dF_i/dx_i (MikoshiLang; Wolfram Div); mismatched lengths now raise (legacy zip-truncated) |
| `ExactODEQ` | verified | 3 | True when M dx + N dy = 0 is exact (dM/dy == dN/dx) |
| `FindMinimum` | fixed | 3 | Wolfram FindMinimum[f, {x, x0}] / [f, {{x, x0}, {y, y0}}] -> {fmin, {x -> xmin, ...}} (local, numeric, BFGS via SciPy); the legacy form [f, {x, y}, {x0, y0}] is accepted and returns the same format; non-convergence raises |
| `GeneratingFunction` | fixed | 3 | GeneratingFunction[{a0, a1, ...}, x] = a0 + a1 x + ... (MikoshiLang finite form); exact (legacy converted coefficients to floats); Wolfram's GeneratingFunction[expr, n, x] is not supported |
| `Grad` | fixed | 8 | Grad[f, {x1, ..., xn}] (Cartesian; a vector field gives the Jacobian); batch 4: Grad[f, vars, chart] for "Cartesian", "Polar" {r, θ}, "Cylindrical" {r, θ, z}, "Spherical" {r, θ, φ} (θ polar angle) in the orthonormal basis (Wolfram) |
| `Gradient` | verified | 3 | list of partial derivatives (MikoshiLang; Wolfram Grad) |
| `HessianMatrix` | verified | 3 | matrix of second partial derivatives |
| `InflectionPoints` | fixed | 3 | real points where f'' changes sign; legacy returned every root of f'' (so x^4 reported 0, which is not an inflection point) and complex roots |
| `IntegratingFactor` | fixed | 3 | mu(x) or mu(y) making M dx + N dy exact; the test is now applied to the simplified ratio and failure raises (legacy returned an Error expression) |
| `JacobianMatrix` | verified | 3 | matrix dF_i/dx_j |
| `LaplaceODESolve` | fixed | 12 | MikoshiLang helper (not a Wolfram built-in): LaplaceODESolve[eqn \| {eqn, y[0] == a, y'[0] == b, ...}, y, t] solves a linear constant-coefficient ODE by the Laplace transform and returns {{y[t] -> sol}} like DSolve, valid for t > 0. Initial values that are not given appear as y[0], y'[0], ... (Laplace form) rather than C[k]. Every solution is substituted back into the ODE and the initial conditions before it is returned. Non-linear or variable-coefficient equations raise (use DSolve). Legacy transformed the raw equation and solved for an unrelated symbol, so it never returned a solution |
| `Laplacian` | fixed | 7 | Laplacian[f, {x1, ..., xn}] (Cartesian); batch 4: Laplacian[f, vars, chart] for Polar, Cylindrical, Spherical (Wolfram) |
| `LaurentSeries` | fixed | 3 | LaurentSeries[f, x, a, n]: Laurent expansion up to (x - a)^n without the O-term (legacy kept an O-term and was exclusive in n) |
| `LinearProgramming` | fixed | 4 | legacy solved A.x <= b with floats; Wolfram LinearProgramming[c, m, b] minimises c.x subject to m.x >= b and x >= 0; now exact (SymPy simplex); infeasible/unbounded problems raise |
| `LyapunovDerivative` | fixed | 3 | dV/dt = grad V . F along x' = F(x) (MikoshiLang); mismatched lengths now raise (legacy zip-truncated) |
| `MaClaurinSeries` | fixed | 4 | MaClaurinSeries[f, {x, n}] about 0 up to x^n (legacy used the point slot and an exclusive order); the 3-element form {x, 0, n} is accepted, a non-zero centre raises |
| `NthDerivative` | fixed | 4 | NthDerivative[f, x, n] = D[f, {x, n}]; n must be a non-negative integer (legacy truncated reals) |
| `ODEClassify` | verified | 3 | list of SymPy ODE hint names for eq, y, x (MikoshiLang) |
| `PhasePlane` | fixed | 3 | equilibrium points of x' = F(x) as rule lists (MikoshiLang; no plot); legacy silently dropped tuple-form solutions |
| `Residue` | verified | 3 | Residue[f, {x, a}] (Wolfram) and Residue[f, x, a] |
| `SeparableODE` | fixed | 3 | general solution {{y[x] -> ...}} of a separable first-order ODE (DSolve format); non-separable equations raise; legacy returned a SymPy equation |
| `Table` | kernel | 33 | Table is a held kernel form; the legacy Table[a, b] rule was unreachable and removed |
| `TaylorPolynomial` | verified | 3 | TaylorPolynomial[f, x, a, n]: Taylor polynomial of degree n (MikoshiLang) |
| `TaylorSeries` | fixed | 3 | TaylorSeries[f, {x, a, n}] keeps terms up to (x - a)^n (legacy stopped at (x - a)^(n - 1)); default n = 5 |
| `Wronskian` | verified | 3 | Wronskian[{f1, ..., fn}, x] (determinant, as Wolfram) |

### calculus (batch 4)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ComposeSeries` | added | 3 | ComposeSeries[s1, s2, ...] = s1(s2(...)) for power series about 0 where the inner series vanish at 0; the order is the one the truncations allow (ComposeSeries[Series[Exp[x], {x, 0, 5}], Series[Sin[x], {x, 0, 5}]] -> 1 + x + x^2/2 - x^4/8 - x^5/15 + O[x]^6) |
| `ConditionalExpression` | added | 9 | ConditionalExpression[expr, cond]: expr when cond is True, Undefined when cond is False, nested conditions combine with And (ConditionalExpression[ConditionalExpression[x, a], b] = ConditionalExpression[x, a && b]), otherwise unevaluated (returned by Integrate, Sum, Solve and the Fourier coefficient functions for parameter conditions; arithmetic on ConditionalExpression objects is not propagated).  Single implementation shared by batches 4 and 6. |
| `D` | fixed | 21 | batch 4: D[f, x, y, ...], D[f, {x, n}], D[f, {{x1, x2, ...}}] (gradient) and {{...}, n} (arrays of higher derivatives), threads over lists and equations (implicit differentiation D[x^2 + y[x]^2 == 1, x]), NonConstants -> {y}, derivatives with respect to f[x]; chain rule results are Wolfram Derivative forms (D[f[g[x]], x] -> f'[g[x]] g'[x]; previously D[f[g[x]], g[x]] g'[x]); symbolic orders stay unevaluated; D[f] with no variable returns f |
| `DSolve` | fixed | 24 | batch 4: every solution returned by DSolve (ODEs and ODE systems in one variable, initial conditions, y[x] or y forms) is now substituted back into the equations and checked (symbolically, else numerically at sample points); PDEs raise. Free constants may be numbered differently from Wolfram (the solution family is the same) |
| `DSolveValue` | added | 4 | DSolveValue[eqns, y[x], x] gives the solution value, DSolveValue[eqns, y, x] a pure function, DSolveValue[eqns, expr, x] the value of expr; several solutions give a list; checked like DSolve |
| `Derivative` | added | 4 | Derivative[n1, n2, ...][f] for pure functions and built-in mathematical functions gives a pure function (Derivative[1][Sin] -> Cos[#1] &, Derivative[2][#^3 &] -> 6 #1 &); Derivative[n...][f][x...] evaluates for known f (Sin'[x] -> Cos[x], previously unevaluated) and stays symbolic for undefined f (f'[x], Derivative[1, 0][f][x, y]); Derivative[0][f] -> f |
| `DiracDelta` | added | 6 | Wolfram DiracDelta[x1, ...]: 0 when any argument is a nonzero real number, unevaluated otherwise (DiracDelta[0] stays); complex numeric arguments raise; integrates and transforms through SymPy (Integrate[f[x] DiracDelta[x - a], {x, -Infinity, Infinity}] -> f[a]); D[HeavisideTheta[x], x] -> DiracDelta[x] |
| `Dt` | added | 9 | total derivatives: Dt[f, x] treats every other symbol as a function of x (Dt[x^2 y, x] -> 2 x y + x^2 Dt[y, x]), Dt[f, {x, n}], Dt[f] is the total differential (Dt[x y] -> y Dt[x] + x Dt[y]); Constants -> {a, ...}; Dt[y, x] for an independent symbol y stays unevaluated; numeric constants (Pi, E, ...) have derivative 0 |
| `FourierCoefficient` | added | 7 | FourierCoefficient[f, t, n] = 1/(2π) ∫_{-π}^{π} f e^(-int) dt (Wolfram default FourierParameters {1, 1}; other parameters raise); integer or symbolic n (symbolic n is integer: a separate n == 0 value appears as Piecewise[{{c0, n == 0}}, g[n]]); the general term is checked against direct integrals at n = ±1, ±2, 3, 5 |
| `FourierCosCoefficient` | added | 3 | FourierCosCoefficient[f, t, n] = 2/π ∫_0^π f cos(nt) dt, with 1/π ∫_0^π f dt for n = 0 (Wolfram); symbolic n checked against direct integrals |
| `FourierCosSeries` | added | 3 | FourierCosSeries[f, t, k] = Σ_{n=0}^{k} FourierCosCoefficient[f, t, n] Cos[n t] (even extension of f on [0, π]) |
| `FourierSeries` | added | 3 | FourierSeries[f, t, k] = Σ_{n=-k}^{k} FourierCoefficient[f, t, n] E^(I n t) (Wolfram default parameters; others raise) |
| `FourierSinCoefficient` | added | 3 | FourierSinCoefficient[f, t, n] = 2/π ∫_0^π f sin(nt) dt (Wolfram); symbolic n checked against direct integrals |
| `FourierSinSeries` | added | 3 | FourierSinSeries[f, t, k] = Σ_{n=1}^{k} FourierSinCoefficient[f, t, n] Sin[n t] (odd extension of f on [0, π]) |
| `FourierTrigSeries` | added | 3 | FourierTrigSeries[f, t, k] = a0 + Σ_{n=1}^{k} (a_n Cos[n t] + b_n Sin[n t]) with a0 = 1/(2π)∫f, a_n = 1/π ∫_{-π}^{π} f cos(nt), b_n = 1/π ∫_{-π}^{π} f sin(nt) (Wolfram default parameters) |
| `HeavisideTheta` | added | 8 | Wolfram HeavisideTheta[x1, ...]: 1 when every argument is positive, 0 when any is negative, unevaluated at 0 (unlike UnitStep[0] = 1 and SymPy's Heaviside(0) = 1/2); D gives DiracDelta |
| `ImplicitD` | added | 5 | ImplicitD[f == g, y, x] = dy/dx for y defined implicitly (ImplicitD[x^2 + y^2 == 1, y, x] -> -x/y), ImplicitD[eqn, y, {x, n}] higher derivatives (expressed in x and y), ImplicitD[{eqns}, {y1, y2}, x] for systems |
| `Integrate` | fixed | 53 | batch 4: indefinite integrals (verified by differentiating back), definite, improper and multiple integrals (the last specification is innermost); options Assumptions, GenerateConditions (Automatic/True give ConditionalExpression[value, cond] when the result needs a parameter condition, e.g. Integrate[x^a, {x, 0, 1}] -> ConditionalExpression[1/(1 + a), Re[a] > -1]; False gives the generic value) and PrincipalValue -> True (Cauchy principal value, Integrate[1/x, {x, -1, 2}, PrincipalValue -> True] -> Log[2]); SymPy convergence conditions are rewritten in Wolfram form (Abs[Arg[a]] < Pi/2 -> Re[a] > 0); integrals that diverge to +-Infinity give that limit, indeterminate (oscillating/cancelling) ones raise; HeavisideTheta and DiracDelta integrands are supported; integrals SymPy cannot do stay unevaluated |
| `InverseSeries` | added | 3 | InverseSeries[s] / InverseSeries[s, y] for a power series s = a1 x + a2 x^2 + ... + O[x]^n about 0 with a1 != 0: the series of the inverse function to the same order (InverseSeries[Series[Sin[x], {x, 0, 5}]] -> x + x^3/6 + 3 x^5/40 + O[x]^6); the result is checked by composing back |
| `Limit` | fixed | 15 | batch 4: Limit[f, x -> a] (two-sided: Indeterminate when the one-sided limits differ), Direction -> "FromAbove" / "FromBelow" / -1 / 1 / "TwoSided", Assumptions, limits at +-Infinity, lists of functions, and multivariate limits Limit[f, {x, y} -> {a, b}] (continuity after cancellation, Indeterminate when two paths disagree, otherwise a clear error); oscillating limits raise (use MaxLimit/MinLimit); legacy rejected every option |
| `MaxLimit` | added | 4 | upper limit (limsup) MaxLimit[f, x -> a] with Direction and Assumptions: the larger one-sided limit, or A + \|B\| for f = A + B Sin[g] / A + B Cos[g] with g -> +-Infinity (MaxLimit[Sin[1/x], x -> 0] -> 1); other oscillating functions raise |
| `MinLimit` | added | 3 | lower limit (liminf) MinLimit[f, x -> a]; see MaxLimit (MinLimit[Sign[x], x -> 0] -> -1) |
| `Normal` | fixed | 47 | batch 4 adds: Normal[series] drops every O term structurally (also the inner ones of multivariate series) and evaluates the remaining polynomial; associations and other heads as before (batch 1) |
| `O` | added | 3 | order term O[x]^n / O[x - x0]^n / O[1/x]^n of Series results (stays symbolic); Normal removes it; SeriesCoefficient, InverseSeries and ComposeSeries read it |
| `Product` | fixed | 7 | batch 4: as Sum (finite, symbolic and infinite products via SymPy; parametric products give the generic value, ConditionalExpression with GenerateConditions -> True) |
| `RSolve` | fixed | 10 | batch 4: RSolve[eqns, a[n], n] -> {{a[n] -> ...}} and RSolve[eqns, a, n] -> {{a -> Function[{n}, ...]}}; linear recurrences with constant or polynomial coefficients and initial conditions (SymPy rsolve); every solution is substituted back into the recurrence and the initial conditions; free constants are C[1], C[2], ...; systems raise |
| `RSolveValue` | added | 4 | RSolveValue[eqns, a[n], n] gives the solution value, RSolveValue[eqns, a, n] a pure function, RSolveValue[eqns, expr, n] the value of expr (e.g. a[10]); checked like RSolve |
| `Series` | fixed | 27 | batch 4: Series[f, {x, x0, n}] with Laurent and Puiseux terms, expansions at +-Infinity in powers of 1/x (O[1/x]^(n+1)), the O term always present (Series[1/x, {x, 0, 2}] -> 1/x + O[x]^3; previously dropped), expansions about x0 grouped in powers of (x - x0) (O[x - x0]^k); several variables Series[f, {x, x0, nx}, {y, y0, ny}] expand in x with coefficients that are series in y; threads over lists; functions SymPy cannot expand raise |
| `SeriesCoefficient` | added | 9 | SeriesCoefficient[f, {x, x0, n}] (negative n for Laurent series, x0 may be Infinity: coefficient of (1/x)^n), several variables {x, x0, nx}, {y, y0, ny}, and SeriesCoefficient[series, n]; a symbolic n gives Piecewise[{{general term, n >= 0}}, 0] when SymPy finds a formal power series (checked against 10 Taylor coefficients); a coefficient beyond a given series' order is Indeterminate |
| `Sum` | fixed | 21 | batch 4: as before ({i, n}, {i, a, b}, {i, a, b, di}, {i, {values}}, several iterators, first outermost; divergent sums to +-Infinity give that limit, other non-convergent sums stay unevaluated), plus parametric series that SymPy sums under a convergence condition: Wolfram's generic value by default (Sum[x^k, {k, 0, Infinity}] -> 1/(1 - x); previously a recursion error) and ConditionalExpression[value, cond] with GenerateConditions -> True |
| `SumConvergence` | added | 7 | SumConvergence[f, n]: True/False for the convergence of Sum[f, {n, k, Infinity}] (SymPy's ratio, root, integral, comparison, alternating-series tests); the p-series family 1/n^p and n^p r^n with a parameter gives the Wolfram condition (Re[p] > 1, Abs[r] < 1); undecided cases stay unevaluated |

### calculus extras (W06)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Antihermitian` | added | 3 | Antihermitian[{s1, s2}]: sign change under conjugate transposition; inert symmetry specification used by TensorSymmetry, ArraySymbol, MatrixSymbol, Matrices and Arrays (Symmetric/Antisymmetric/ZeroSymmetric[All]: every slot); malformed slot lists raise |
| `Antisymmetric` | added | 12 | Antisymmetric[{s1, ..., sn}]: sign change under odd permutations; inert symmetry specification used by TensorSymmetry, ArraySymbol, MatrixSymbol, Matrices and Arrays (Symmetric/Antisymmetric/ZeroSymmetric[All]: every slot); malformed slot lists raise |
