| `ArrayExpand` | added | 4 | ArrayExpand[expr]: expands symbolic-array expressions built from MatrixSymbol/VectorSymbol/ArraySymbol (rank <= 2) and Symbolic*Array with Plus, Times, Dot, Transpose, Inverse, MatrixPower, Tr, Det: distributes Dot over Plus, Transpose[a.b] -> Transpose[b].Transpose[a], Transpose[a + b] -> ..., Inverse[a.b] -> Inverse[b].Inverse[a] (SymPy matrix expressions); explicit arrays are expanded elementwise; anything else raises |
| `ArraySimplify` | added | 7 | ArraySimplify[expr]: simplifies symbolic-array expressions (Transpose[Transpose[a]] -> a, a.Inverse[a] -> identity, a.SymbolicIdentityArray[{n}] -> a, a.SymbolicZerosArray -> zeros, Transpose[a] -> a for MatrixSymbol[.., Symmetric[{1, 2}]] and -a for Antisymmetric) via SymPy matrix expressions; explicit arrays are simplified elementwise |
| `ArraySymbol` | added | 4 | ArraySymbol["A", dims] / [.., dom] / [.., dom, sym]: a symbolic array (inert); rank-1 and rank-2 ArraySymbols take part in ArrayExpand / ArraySimplify like VectorSymbol / MatrixSymbol |
| `Arrays` | added | 3 | Arrays[{d1, ..., dk}] / Arrays[dims, dom] / Arrays[dims, dom, sym]: the domain of arrays of the given dimensions; inert; malformed specifications raise |
| `Asymptotic` | added | 12 | Asymptotic[f, x -> x0]: the leading term of the asymptotic expansion (verified: f/term -> 1); Asymptotic[f, {x, x0, n}]: the expansion to order n; SeriesTermGoal -> k: the first k terms (Taylor, Laurent or Puiseux at finite x0; at Infinity down to x^-n, with exponential/power prefactors and Stirling-type expansions of Gamma, Factorial, Binomial, HarmonicNumber via LogGamma); unexpandable input raises |
| `AsymptoticDSolveValue` | added | 4 | AsymptoticDSolveValue[{ode, ics}, y[x], {x, x0, n} \| x -> x0]: the power-series solution of a (linear or nonlinear) ODE at an ordinary point x0 (Taylor coefficients by repeated differentiation of the solved equation; missing initial values become C[1], C[2], ...); AsymptoticDSolveValue[{ode, ics}, y[x], x, {e, 0, n}]: regular perturbation expansion in a parameter (each order solved with DSolve, residual checked); singular points, singular perturbations and systems raise |
| `AsymptoticEqual` | added | 3 | AsymptoticEqual[f, g, x -> x0]: True when f = Theta(g): \|f/g\| bounded above and away from 0 as x -> x0 (decided by the limit of \|f/g\|; oscillating ratios with finite bounds are decided where the bounds suffice), False otherwise; unevaluated when the limit cannot be decided or depends on parameters |
| `AsymptoticEquivalent` | added | 3 | AsymptoticEquivalent[f, g, x -> x0]: True when f ~ g: f/g -> 1 as x -> x0 (decided by the limit of \|f/g\|; oscillating ratios with finite bounds are decided where the bounds suffice), False otherwise; unevaluated when the limit cannot be decided or depends on parameters |
| `AsymptoticExpectation` | added | 3 | AsymptoticExpectation[expr, x \[Distributed] dist, a -> a0 \| {a, a0, n}]: the asymptotic expansion in a parameter of the exact Expectation (computed by Expectation, then expanded); raises when the expectation has no closed form |
| `AsymptoticGreater` | added | 3 | AsymptoticGreater[f, g, x -> x0]: True when f = omega(g): \|f/g\| -> Infinity as x -> x0 (decided by the limit of \|f/g\|; oscillating ratios with finite bounds are decided where the bounds suffice), False otherwise; unevaluated when the limit cannot be decided or depends on parameters |
| `AsymptoticGreaterEqual` | added | 3 | AsymptoticGreaterEqual[f, g, x -> x0]: True when f = Omega(g): \|f/g\| bounded away from 0 as x -> x0 (decided by the limit of \|f/g\|; oscillating ratios with finite bounds are decided where the bounds suffice), False otherwise; unevaluated when the limit cannot be decided or depends on parameters |
| `AsymptoticIntegrate` | added | 3 | AsymptoticIntegrate[f, x, x -> x0 \| {x, x0, n}]: asymptotic expansion of an antiderivative (SymPy's antiderivative checked by differentiation, else term-wise integration of the expansion of f, constant 0); AsymptoticIntegrate[f, {t, a, b}, {p, p0, n}]: expansion of a parametric integral (exact integral checked numerically, else term-wise integration of the expansion in p (finite p0, each term finite; the result is checked against numerical quadrature near p0)); divergent term-wise integrals raise |
| `AsymptoticLess` | added | 3 | AsymptoticLess[f, g, x -> x0]: True when f = o(g): f/g -> 0 as x -> x0 (decided by the limit of \|f/g\|; oscillating ratios with finite bounds are decided where the bounds suffice), False otherwise; unevaluated when the limit cannot be decided or depends on parameters |
| `AsymptoticLessEqual` | added | 3 | AsymptoticLessEqual[f, g, x -> x0]: True when f = O(g): \|f/g\| bounded as x -> x0 (decided by the limit of \|f/g\|; oscillating ratios with finite bounds are decided where the bounds suffice), False otherwise; unevaluated when the limit cannot be decided or depends on parameters |
| `AsymptoticProbability` | added | 3 | AsymptoticProbability[pred, x \[Distributed] dist, a -> a0 \| {a, a0, n}]: the asymptotic expansion in a parameter of the exact probability (Probability, or 1 - CDF / CDF for one-sided bounds of continuous distributions); e.g. the Gaussian tail Erfc[a/Sqrt[2]]/2 ~ E^(-a^2/2)/(a Sqrt[2 Pi]); no closed form -> error |
| `AsymptoticProduct` | added | 3 | AsymptoticProduct[f, {k, a, n}, n -> Infinity \| {n, Infinity, m}], AsymptoticProduct[f, {k, a, Infinity}, {p, p0, m}] and the indefinite AsymptoticProduct[f, k, {k, Infinity, m}] (= Product over 1..k-1): from SymPy's closed form (Gamma / rising factorials, checked numerically) expanded with Stirling's series (Product[k, {k, 2, n}] ~ Sqrt[2 Pi] n^(n + 1/2) E^-n); no closed form -> error |
| `AsymptoticRSolveValue` | added | 3 | AsymptoticRSolveValue[{eqn, ics}, y[n], n -> Infinity \| {n, Infinity, m}]: asymptotics of the solution of a recurrence: first-order linear homogeneous recurrences are solved as C[1] times a closed-form product (Gamma functions, verified); others via RSolveValue (verified by substitution); each free constant's part is expanded separately (Stirling / exponential scales; Fibonacci -> GoldenRatio^n/Sqrt[5]); parameter (perturbation) forms and recurrences without a verified closed form raise |
| `AsymptoticSolve` | added | 4 | AsymptoticSolve[eqn, {y, y0}, {x, x0, n}]: the branch of y(x) through (x0, y0) as a Taylor series (implicit function theorem, requires D[eqn, y] != 0 there; Newton iteration on truncated series, residual checked); AsymptoticSolve[eqn, y \| {y}, {x, x0, n} \| x -> x0]: every branch, from SymPy's closed-form roots expanded in Puiseux series; systems raise |
| `AsymptoticSum` | added | 5 | AsymptoticSum[f, {k, a, n}, n -> Infinity \| {n, Infinity, m}]: asymptotics of a partial sum, AsymptoticSum[f, {k, a, Infinity}, {p, p0, m}]: expansion of a parametric infinite sum, AsymptoticSum[f, k, {k, Infinity, m}]: the indefinite sum (the tail convention F(Infinity) = 0 for convergent sums, else Sum over 0..k-1); computed from SymPy's closed form (checked numerically against direct summation) with Euler-Maclaurin expansions of Hurwitz zeta / generalized harmonic numbers; no closed form -> error |
| `C` | added | 3 | C[i] is the i-th constant of integration generated by DSolve, RSolve, AsymptoticDSolveValue and GreenFunction-related solvers (inert; C[i] + C[i] = 2 C[i]) |
| `CaputoD` | added | 8 | CaputoD[f, {x, alpha}]: the Caputo differintegral I^(m-alpha) D^m f (m = Ceiling[alpha]) with lower terminal 0; powers in closed form (constants and x^k with k < alpha give 0; symbolic alpha gives a Piecewise for polynomial terms); other f with numeric alpha via the defining integral, checked against quadrature; threads over lists |
| `Casoratian` | added | 4 | Casoratian[{y1, ..., yk}, n] = Det[{y_j[n + i]}] (i = 0..k-1), simplified; Casoratian[..., n, True] gives the matrix; the equation form Casoratian[eqn, y, n] raises (its normalisation constant is not determined by the equation) |
| `ContourIntegrate` | added | 13 | ContourIntegrate[f, z ∈ cont]: complex contour integral; closed contours (Circle, Disk, Rectangle, Triangle, Polygon boundaries, counterclockwise) of meromorphic f by the residue theorem (poles found exactly); open contours (Line, HalfLine, InfiniteLine, arcs, ParametricRegion[{x[t], y[t]}, ...]) by an antiderivative or direct parametrised integration; every result is checked against numerical quadrature (parameters sampled); poles on the contour and symbolic pole locations raise |
| `CoordinateTransform` | added | 5 | CoordinateTransform["chart1" -> "chart2", pt]: the coordinates of pt in chart2 (charts Cartesian, Polar (n-dimensional hyperspherical), Spherical, Cylindrical; {"chart", n} gives the dimension); threads over a list of points |
| `DEigensystem` | added | 3 | DEigensystem[{L[u[x]], bcs}, u[x], {x, a, b}, n]: {eigenvalues, eigenfunctions} (unnormalised, e.g. Sin[k x]) for the same class of problems as DEigenvalues |
| `DEigenvalues` | added | 3 | DEigenvalues[{L[u[x]], bcs}, u[x], {x, a, b}, n]: the n smallest-magnitude eigenvalues of L u == lambda u for constant-coefficient second-order operators on an interval with Dirichlet (DirichletCondition or u[a] == 0) or Neumann (u'[a] == 0, NeumannValue[0, ...], the default) ends, in closed form; other regions, variable coefficients and Robin/periodic conditions raise |
| `DFixedPoints` | added | 3 | DFixedPoints[eqns, x[t] \| {x1, ...}, t]: the equilibria of an autonomous ODE system, as points of the first-order state (x, x', ..., x^(k-1)) for higher-order equations; sorted; raises for continua |
| `DSolveChangeVariables` | added | 7 | DSolveChangeVariables[Inactive[DSolve][ode, y \| y[x], x], u, t, trans]: changes the variables of a single ODE by the chain rule; trans is t == phi[x] or x == psi[t] (independent variable), y[x] == G[x, u[x]] (dependent variable, t = x) or a list of one of each; returns Inactive[DSolve][ode', u \| u[t], t]; PDEs, systems and named coordinate charts raise |
| `DStabilityConditions` | added | 3 | DStabilityConditions[eqns, x, t] (optionally with a list of points): {{point, condition}, ...} for asymptotic stability from the linearisation (all eigenvalues with negative real part: True/False for numeric Jacobians, Re[l] < 0 conditions otherwise); non-hyperbolic numeric cases raise |
| `DependentVariables` | added | 3 | option of NDSolve/DSolve listing the dependent variables (inert symbol) |
| `DifferenceQuotient` | added | 4 | DifferenceQuotient[f, {x, h}] = (f[x + h] - f[x])/h; {x, n, h}: the n-th forward difference over h^n; several specifications apply in turn; threads over lists |
| `DifferenceRoot` | added | 3 | DifferenceRoot[Function[{y, n}, {rec, ics}]][k]: the solution of a linear recurrence with initial values; evaluated exactly at integers (iterating the recurrence), unevaluated otherwise |
| `DifferenceRootReduce` | added | 4 | DifferenceRootReduce[expr, n]: DifferenceRoot[Function[{y, n}, {rec == 0, ics}]][n] with a linear recurrence with polynomial coefficients (guessed from exact values, then verified symbolically or numerically at non-integer points); DifferenceRootReduce[f &]; DifferenceRootReduce[lhs == rhs, n] gives True when both sides reduce to the same sequence; non-holonomic inputs raise |
| `DifferentialRoot` | added | 3 | DifferentialRoot[Function[{y, x}, {ode, ics}]][x]: the solution of an ODE with initial conditions; exact at the initial point, numerical (mpmath Taylor ODE solver) at inexact arguments, unevaluated otherwise |
| `DifferentialRootReduce` | added | 4 | DifferentialRootReduce[expr, x]: DifferentialRoot[Function[{y, x}, {ode == 0, ics}]][x], a linear ODE with polynomial coefficients (SymPy's holonomic conversion, verified by substitution) with initial conditions at a regular point (0, else 1, 2, ...); DifferentialRootReduce[f &]; non-holonomic inputs raise |
| `DiscreteAsymptotic` | added | 5 | DiscreteAsymptotic[f, n -> Infinity] / {n, Infinity, k}: asymptotic approximation of a sequence (n an integer): Fibonacci, LucasL, factorials, binomials, HarmonicNumber, CatalanNumber are rewritten (Binet, Stirling/LogGamma), (-1)^n-type factors are kept as oscillating factors; dominated terms are dropped; leading terms are verified |
| `DiscreteLimit` | added | 7 | DiscreteLimit[f, n -> Infinity]: the limit of the sequence f(n) over integers n; sequences with periodic oscillation ((-1)^n, Sin[Pi q n], ...) are split into residue classes n = p m + r (Indeterminate when the subsequence limits differ); Fibonacci/factorial/binomial forms are rewritten; threads over lists; unevaluated when undecided (e.g. Sin[n]) |
| `DiscreteLyapunovSolve` | added | 3 | DiscreteLyapunovSolve[a, c]: a.x.a^H - x == c; [a, b, c]: a.x.b - x == c; [{a, d}, c]: a.x.a^H - d.x.d^H == c; [{a, d}, {b, e}, c]: a.x.b - d.x.e == c; exact or numerical; raises when the solution is not unique |
| `DiscreteMaxLimit` | added | 4 | DiscreteMaxLimit[f, n -> Infinity]: lim sup of the sequence f(n) (residue-class splitting for periodic oscillation; e.g. (-1)^n -> 1, Sin[n Pi/3] -> Sqrt[3]/2); unevaluated when undecided |
| `DiscreteMinLimit` | added | 4 | DiscreteMinLimit[f, n -> Infinity]: lim inf of the sequence f(n) (residue-class splitting for periodic oscillation); unevaluated when undecided |
| `DiscreteRiccatiSolve` | added | 3 | DiscreteRiccatiSolve[{a, b}, {q, r}] (or {q, r, p}): the stabilising solution of the discrete algebraic Riccati equation; exact via the stable eigenvectors of the symplectic pencil (invertible a, verified by substitution) for exact input, SciPy's solve_discrete_are for inexact input; unevaluated otherwise |
| `DiscreteVariables` | added | 3 | option of NDSolve listing variables that change only at discrete events (inert symbol) |
| `ExponentialGeneratingFunction` | added | 4 | ExponentialGeneratingFunction[expr, n, x] = Sum[expr x^n/n!, {n, 0, Infinity}] in closed form (SymPy summation, checked against the first Taylor coefficients); raises when no closed form is found |
| `FactorialMomentGeneratingFunction` | added | 4 | FactorialMomentGeneratingFunction[dist, t] = Expectation[t^x] = MomentGeneratingFunction[dist, Log[t]], simplified; raises when the moment generating function is not known |
| `FindGeneratingFunction` | added | 3 | FindGeneratingFunction[{a0, a1, ...}, x]: a rational generating function from a constant-coefficient linear recurrence (at least two confirming terms), else the sum of the FindSequenceFunction term; verified against the series; unevaluated when nothing is found |
| `FindSequenceFunction` | added | 5 | FindSequenceFunction[{a1, a2, ...}, n] (or {{n1, a1}, ...}): rational functions of n, hypergeometric terms (rational successive ratios: products, factorials, powers), constant-coefficient linear recurrences (solved exactly; Fibonacci/LucasL recognised) and sums of hypergeometric differences, each fitted exactly with at least two confirming terms; FindSequenceFunction[list] gives a pure function; stays unevaluated when nothing is found |
| `FractionalD` | added | 10 | FractionalD[f, {x, alpha}]: the Riemann-Liouville differintegral with lower terminal 0; sums of powers c x^p (p > -1) in closed form for any (also symbolic) alpha, Gamma(p+1)/Gamma(p+1-alpha) x^(p-alpha); integer alpha gives D or repeated integrals; other f with numeric alpha via the defining integral (D^m I^(m-alpha) f), checked against quadrature; threads over lists |
| `FrenetSerretSystem` | added | 4 | FrenetSerretSystem[{x[t], y[t]}, t] = {{k}, {T, N}} with the signed curvature k = (x' y'' - y' x'')/\|r'\|^3 and N = T rotated by Pi/2; FrenetSerretSystem[{x, y, z}, t] = {{k, tau}, {T, N, B}} (curvature, torsion, tangent, normal, binormal); t is assumed real; curves of other dimensions raise |
| `FromPolarCoordinates` | added | 4 | FromPolarCoordinates[{r, t}] = {r Cos[t], r Sin[t]}; {r, t1, ..., t(n-1)} -> {r Cos[t1], r Sin[t1] Cos[t2], ..., r Sin[t1] ... Sin[t(n-1)]}; threads over a list of points |
| `FromSphericalCoordinates` | added | 3 | FromSphericalCoordinates[{r, t, p}] = {r Cos[p] Sin[t], r Sin[t] Sin[p], r Cos[t]}; threads over a list of points |
| `FunctionInterpolation` | added | 7 | FunctionInterpolation[expr, {x, xmin, xmax}]: samples expr on a uniform grid (31 points, doubled until the piecewise interpolant of InterpolationOrder (default 3) agrees with expr at every midpoint to 1e-7 relative, at most 8193 points) and returns an InterpolatingFunction; several variables and region specifications raise |
| `GreenFunction` | added | 4 | GreenFunction[{L[u[x]], bcs}, u[x] \| u, {x, a, b}, y]: the Green's function of a linear ODE of any order with homogeneous two-point or initial conditions (homogeneous solutions by DSolve; continuity of the first n-2 derivatives and the jump 1/c_n(y) of the (n-1)th), as HeavisideTheta pieces; u gives Function[{x, y}, ...]; PDEs and problems without a unique Green's function raise |
| `Hermitian` | added | 4 | Hermitian[{s1, s2}]: invariance under conjugate transposition of the two slots; inert symmetry specification used by TensorSymmetry, ArraySymbol, MatrixSymbol, Matrices and Arrays (Symmetric/Antisymmetric/ZeroSymmetric[All]: every slot); malformed slot lists raise |
| `HodgeDual` | promoted | 4 | HodgeDual[t]: the Hodge dual of an explicit array whose slots all have dimension n: (1/k!) LeviCivita_{i1..ik j1..j(n-k)} t_{i1..ik} (t is antisymmetrized in effect); HodgeDual[{a, b, c}] = {{0, c, -b}, {-c, 0, a}, {b, -a, 0}}; HodgeDual[x, n] for a scalar is x LeviCivitaTensor[n]; HodgeDual[t, n, slots] dualizes the given slots (the others become the last slots of the result); previously quarantined |
| `IncludeConstantBasis` | added | 3 | option of DesignMatrix, LinearModelFit and related fitting functions: whether to include a constant basis function (default True; inert symbol) |
| `IntegrateChangeVariables` | added | 9 | IntegrateChangeVariables[Inactive[Integrate][f, x \| {x, a, b}], u, u == phi[x] \| x == psi[u]]: integration by substitution, f(psi(u)) psi'(u), returned as Inactive[Integrate][...]; for definite integrals the inverse branch mapping [ua, ub] onto [a, b] is chosen and the result is checked by quadrature; multiple integrals, regions and named coordinate charts raise |
| `InterpolatingPolynomial` | added | 8 | InterpolatingPolynomial[{f1, f2, ...}, x] (nodes 1, 2, ...), [{{x1, f1}, ...}, x] and Hermite data {{x1, f1, df1, ...}, ...} or {{x1, {f1, df1, ...}}, ...}: the lowest-degree interpolating polynomial in Newton/Horner form by (confluent) divided differences; symbolic nodes and values allowed; multidimensional data and the Modulus option raise |
| `InterpolationOrder` | added | 4 | option of Interpolation, ListInterpolation and FunctionInterpolation: the polynomial order of the interpolation (default 3) |
| `InterpolationPoints` | added | 3 | option of FunctionInterpolation: the number of sample points (default 31, refined until the interpolant agrees with the function at the midpoints) |
| `LineIntegrate` | added | 11 | LineIntegrate[f, {x, y, ...} ∈ curve] (scalar, f \|r'\| dt) and LineIntegrate[{p, q, ...}, {x, y, ...} ∈ curve] (vector, F.r' dt) over Line (points in order), HalfLine, InfiniteLine, Circle (counterclockwise; arcs and ellipses) and one-parameter ParametricRegion (increasing parameter); symbolic integration checked against numerical quadrature (parameters sampled); unevaluable integrals raise |
| `LyapunovSolve` | added | 5 | LyapunovSolve[a, c]: a.x + x.a^H == c; [a, b, c]: a.x + x.b == c; [{a, d}, c]: a.x.d^H + d.x.a^H == c; [{a, d}, {b, e}, c]: a.x.e + d.x.b == c; exact (Kronecker linear system) or numerical for inexact input; raises when the solution is not unique |
| `Matrices` | added | 5 | Matrices[{m, n}] / Matrices[{m, n}, dom] / Matrices[{m, n}, dom, sym]: the domain of m x n matrices (sym a Symmetric/Antisymmetric/Hermitian/... specification); inert; malformed specifications raise |
| `MatrixSymbol` | added | 13 | MatrixSymbol["A", {m, n}] / [.., dom] / [.., dom, sym]: a symbolic m x n matrix (inert); ArrayExpand / ArraySimplify treat it as a SymPy MatrixSymbol (symmetry Symmetric[{1, 2}] makes Transpose[A] = A) |
| `NCaputoD` | added | 4 | NCaputoD[f, {x, alpha}, x0]: numerical Caputo differintegral at x0 > 0 (tanh-sinh quadrature of the defining integral of f^(m)) |
| `NContourIntegrate` | added | 3 | NContourIntegrate[f, z ∈ cont]: numerical contour integral along the contours supported by ContourIntegrate (Line, HalfLine, InfiniteLine, Circle, arcs, Disk/Rectangle/Triangle/Polygon boundaries counterclockwise, ParametricRegion) |
| `NFractionalD` | added | 6 | NFractionalD[f, {x, alpha}, x0]: numerical Riemann-Liouville differintegral at x0 > 0 (Caputo integral of f^(m) by tanh-sinh quadrature plus the initial-value terms, or numerical differentiation of the fractional integral when f^(k)(0) is singular) |
| `NLineIntegrate` | added | 3 | NLineIntegrate[f \| {p, q, ...}, {x, ...} ∈ curve]: numerical scalar or vector line integral over the curves supported by LineIntegrate (adaptive Gauss-Legendre/tanh-sinh quadrature) |
| `NSurfaceIntegrate` | added | 3 | NSurfaceIntegrate[f \| {p, q, r}, {x, y, z} ∈ surface]: numerical scalar or flux surface integral over the surfaces supported by SurfaceIntegrate |
| `PrincipalValue` | added | 4 | option of Integrate (and ContourIntegrate): PrincipalValue -> True gives Cauchy principal values (default False); handled by Integrate (batch 4); an inert symbol otherwise |
| `RFixedPoints` | added | 3 | RFixedPoints[eqns, a[n] \| {a1, ...}, n]: the fixed points of an autonomous recurrence system (all shifts equal), as points of the state (a[n], ..., a[n + k - 1]); sorted; raises for continua |
| `RStabilityConditions` | added | 3 | RStabilityConditions[eqns, a, n] (optionally with points): {{point, condition}, ...} for asymptotic stability of the fixed points from the linearisation (all eigenvalues inside the unit circle; a single real parameter gives an interval such as -1 < a < 1, otherwise Abs[l] < 1 conditions) |
| `ResidueSum` | added | 19 | ResidueSum[f, z]: the sum of the residues at all finite poles (rational f: -Res at infinity; entire f: 0; other functions with infinitely many poles raise); ResidueSum[{f, cons}, z]: the residues at the poles satisfying cons (bounded constraints Abs[z - c] < r, or any constraint for rational f), with poles found exactly (polynomial roots, periodic families enumerated, Gamma poles); non-meromorphic functions, poles on the constraint boundary and symbolic pole locations raise |
| `RiccatiSolve` | added | 3 | RiccatiSolve[{a, b}, {q, r}] (or {q, r, p}): the stabilising solution of the continuous algebraic Riccati equation; exact via the stable invariant subspace of the Hamiltonian matrix (verified by substitution) for exact input, SciPy's solve_continuous_are for inexact input; unevaluated when no exact solution is found |
| `SeriesTermGoal` | added | 12 | option of Asymptotic and the Asymptotic* functions: the number of (non-zero) terms of the asymptotic approximation to return for x -> x0 specifications (the expansion order is raised until that many terms appear, then the most dominant are kept; default Automatic: the leading term) |
| `SurfaceIntegrate` | added | 12 | SurfaceIntegrate[f, {x, y, z} ∈ surface] (scalar, f dS) and SurfaceIntegrate[{p, q, r}, ...] (flux F.n dS) over Sphere, Ball (its boundary), Cuboid and Cube (outward normals), Triangle (scalar only) and two-parameter ParametricRegion (normal r_u x r_v); in the plane, boundaries of Disk/Circle/Rectangle/Triangle/Polygon (outward normal) and parametric curves; results checked by numerical quadrature |
| `SymbolicDeltaProductArray` | added | 3 | SymbolicDeltaProductArray[dims, {{s1, t1}, ...}]: the array whose entries are the product of KroneckerDelta[i_s, i_t] over the given slot pairs (pairs disjoint, slots in range); Normal gives the dense array |
| `SymbolicIdentityArray` | added | 5 | SymbolicIdentityArray[{n1, ..., nk}]: the identity array of dimensions {n1, ..., nk, n1, ..., nk} (entries KroneckerDelta[i1, j1] ... KroneckerDelta[ik, jk]); SymbolicIdentityArray[{n}] is the n x n identity matrix; Normal gives the dense array; A.SymbolicIdentityArray[{n}] simplifies to A |
| `SymbolicOnesArray` | added | 3 | SymbolicOnesArray[{d1, ...}]: a symbolic all-ones array (inert); Normal gives the dense array for integer dimensions |
| `SymbolicZerosArray` | added | 4 | SymbolicZerosArray[{d1, ...}]: a symbolic all-zero array (inert); Normal gives the dense array for integer dimensions; ArrayExpand/ArraySimplify use A.0 = 0, A + 0 = A |
| `Symmetric` | added | 17 | Symmetric[{s1, ..., sn}]: symmetry under any permutation of the slots; inert symmetry specification used by TensorSymmetry, ArraySymbol, MatrixSymbol, Matrices and Arrays (Symmetric/Antisymmetric/ZeroSymmetric[All]: every slot); malformed slot lists raise |
| `TargetStructure` | added | 4 | option of symbolic-array functions (Normal of SymbolicIdentityArray etc.): "Dense" (default) gives a dense list; other structures raise |
| `TensorExpand` | added | 3 | TensorExpand[expr]: distributes TensorProduct, TensorWedge and Dot over Plus and pulls numeric factors out of them (multilinearity), recursively; symbolic factors are treated as tensors (numeric coefficients only are scalars), so the result is valid for any ranks |
| `TensorReduce` | added | 4 | TensorReduce[expr]: canonical form of tensor polynomials: TensorExpand, then TensorWedge factors of known rank (VectorSymbol, MatrixSymbol, ArraySymbol) are sorted with the Koszul sign (-1)^(p q), a repeated odd-rank factor gives 0, and like terms are collected; explicit arrays evaluate directly |
| `TensorSymmetry` | added | 7 | TensorSymmetry[tensor]: the symmetry of an explicit array: Symmetric[{..}] / Antisymmetric[{..}] when the symmetry group is the full symmetric group on a set of slots (the others fixed), ZeroSymmetric[{1, ..., r}] for a zero array, {} for none, else a generating set {{Cycles[..], phase}, ...} (phases +-1 or roots of unity); also reads the symmetry of MatrixSymbol/ArraySymbol and SymbolicIdentityArray; TensorSymmetry[t, slots] restricts to the given slots |
| `TensorWedge` | added | 11 | TensorWedge[t1, t2, ...]: the exterior product of explicit antisymmetric arrays of equal dimension, normalized as (p + q)!/(p! q!) Alt(t1 (x) t2) (TensorWedge[{a1, a2}, {b1, b2}] = {{0, a1 b2 - a2 b1}, {a2 b1 - a1 b2, 0}}); scalars multiply; symbolic arguments stay unevaluated |
| `ToPolarCoordinates` | added | 5 | ToPolarCoordinates[{x, y}] = {Sqrt[x^2 + y^2], ArcTan[x, y]}; {x1, ..., xn} -> hyperspherical {r, ArcCos[x1/Sqrt[x1^2 + ... + xn^2]], ..., ArcTan[x(n-1), xn]}; threads over a list of points |
| `ToSphericalCoordinates` | added | 3 | ToSphericalCoordinates[{x, y, z}] = {Sqrt[x^2 + y^2 + z^2], ArcTan[z, Sqrt[x^2 + y^2]], ArcTan[x, y]} (radius, polar angle from the z axis, azimuth); threads over a list of points |
| `TransformedField` | added | 4 | TransformedField["chart1" -> "chart2", f, {x1, ...} -> {y1, ...}]: a scalar field f (expression) or a vector field (list of components in the orthonormal frame of chart1) re-expressed in chart2 coordinates {y1, ...} (charts Cartesian, Polar, Spherical, Cylindrical); simplification assumes the standard chart ranges (r > 0, polar angles in (0, Pi)); rank >= 2 tensor fields raise |
| `TruncateSum` | added | 5 | TruncateSum[expr, m]: every Sum and Inactive[Sum] in expr is replaced by the sum of its first m terms (explicit, activated); symbolic m gives Inactive[Sum][f, {i, a, a + m - 1}]; ActivateResult -> False keeps the truncated sums inactive |
| `VectorSymbol` | added | 12 | VectorSymbol["v"] / VectorSymbol["v", n] / VectorSymbol["v", {n}] / VectorSymbol["v", n, dom]: a symbolic vector of length n (inert; used by ArrayExpand, ArraySimplify, TensorDimensions-style functions in this module); bad arguments raise |
| `Vectors` | added | 5 | Vectors[n] / Vectors[n, dom]: the domain of length-n vectors (dom Complexes by default, Reals, ...); inert domain used with Element and VectorSymbol; malformed specifications raise |
| `ZeroSymmetric` | added | 4 | ZeroSymmetric[{s1, ..., sn}]: the symmetry of a zero array; inert symmetry specification used by TensorSymmetry, ArraySymbol, MatrixSymbol, Matrices and Arrays (Symmetric/Antisymmetric/ZeroSymmetric[All]: every slot); malformed slot lists raise |

### combinatorics

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ArithmeticSequence` | fixed | 3 | first n terms a, a + d, ...; exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits) |
| `Binomial` | fixed | 15 | legacy truncated arguments with int() (Binomial[5/2, 1] = 2) and failed on symbols; now exact/symbolic with Wolfram's conventions for negative integers (Binomial[-1, -1] = 1) |
| `CartesianProduct` | verified | 3 | pairs {a, b} for a in the first list and b in the second (= Tuples[{l1, l2}]); now variadic |
| `Compositions` | fixed | 3 | legacy returned k-subsets of {1..n-1}; now Compositions[n, k] lists all ordered k-tuples of non-negative integers summing to n (Combinatorica convention), lexicographic |
| `Derangements` | verified | 4 | number of derangements !n (MikoshiLang; same as Subfactorial) |
| `DoubleFactorial` | fixed | 4 | negative odd integers supported ((-5)!! = 1/3); legacy truncated reals |
| `Factorial` | fixed | 5 | non-integers use Gamma[n + 1] (Factorial[1/2] = Sqrt[Pi]/2); negative integers give ComplexInfinity; symbols stay unevaluated; legacy truncated with int() |
| `Factorial2` | promoted | 3 | Wolfram name for the double factorial |
| `FibonacciSequence` | verified | 3 | first n Fibonacci numbers F0, F1, ... (MikoshiLang) |
| `GeometricSequence` | fixed | 3 | first n terms a, a r, ...; exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats and rounded) |
| `HookLengthFormula` | fixed | 5 | legacy subtracted the row index from each hook (wrong counts / ZeroDivisionError); now the number of standard Young tableaux of the shape |
| `IntegerPartitions` | fixed | 7 | legacy returned the number of partitions; Wolfram IntegerPartitions lists them in reverse lexicographic order; supports [n], [n, k], [n, {k}], [n, {kmin, kmax}], [n, kspec, {s1, ...} or All] and [n, kspec, sspec, m] (first m partitions, batch 6) |
| `Multinomial` | fixed | 4 | legacy Multinomial[n, {k...}] was not Wolfram's form; now Multinomial[n1, n2, ...] = (n1 + n2 + ...)!/(n1! n2! ...), exact for any exact arguments |
| `PartitionCount` | verified | 3 | MikoshiLang alias of PartitionsP |
| `PartitionsList` | fixed | 3 | legacy reused one mutable dict (every entry was the last partition); now the partitions of n in Wolfram IntegerPartitions order |
| `PartitionsP` | promoted | 3 | number of unrestricted partitions |
| `PartitionsQ` | promoted | 3 | number of partitions into distinct parts |
| `Permutations` | fixed | 4 | legacy Permutations[n, k] returned the count n!/(n-k)!; Wolfram lists permutations of a list (duplicates removed); Permutations[list, n] and [list, {n}] supported; integer arguments raise |
| `PowerSet` | verified | 3 | MikoshiLang alias of Subsets[list] |
| `Subfactorial` | promoted | 3 | !n |
| `Subsets` | fixed | 12 | Subsets[list], [list, n], [list, {n}], [list, {min, max}], [list, {min, max, step}] in Wolfram order (by size, then position); batch 6 adds Subsets[list, nspec, s] (first s), [list, nspec, {s}] (the s-th, computed by unranking) and [list, nspec, {m, n(, step)}], and any head (Subsets[f[a, b]]) |
| `Tuples` | promoted | 7 | Tuples[list, n] and Tuples[{l1, l2, ...}]; batch 6 adds Tuples[list, {n1, n2, ...}] (all n1 x n2 x ... arrays) and any head (Tuples[f[a, b], 2] = {f[a, a], f[a, b], f[b, a], f[b, b]}) |
| `YoungTableaux` | promoted | 22 | YoungTableaux[shape]: all standard Young tableaux of the partition shape (rows strictly increase left to right and down columns), sorted by row word, as Combinatorica Tableaux[shape]; their number equals HookLengthFormula[shape]. YoungTableaux[n]: all standard tableaux with n cells (over all partitions in reverse-lexicographic order), whose number is the number of involutions of n. Refuses more than 100000 tableaux. Legacy built one tableau and returned its size |

### combinatorics (batch 6)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `AbelianGroup` | added | 4 | AbelianGroup[{n1, n2, ...}] inert object (direct product of cyclic groups); GroupOrder supported |
| `AlternatingFactorial` | added | 3 | Sum[(-1)^(n - k) k!, {k, n}] for integers n >= 0 (OEIS A005165); other arguments stay unevaluated |
| `AlternatingGroup` | added | 12 | AlternatingGroup[n] inert group object (permutation representation on 1..n) for GroupOrder, GroupElements, GroupMultiplicationTable, GroupOrbits, Permute, ... |
| `Cycles` | added | 59 | canonical form: 1-cycles dropped, each cycle starts with its smallest point, cycles sorted by first point; repeated or non-positive points raise |
| `CyclicGroup` | added | 5 | CyclicGroup[n] inert group object (permutation representation on 1..n) for GroupOrder, GroupElements, GroupMultiplicationTable, GroupOrbits, Permute, ... |
| `DihedralGroup` | added | 7 | DihedralGroup[n] inert group object (permutation representation on 1..n) for GroupOrder, GroupElements, GroupMultiplicationTable, GroupOrbits, Permute, ... |
| `Fibonorial` | added | 3 | Product[Fibonacci[k], {k, n}] for integers n >= 0 (OEIS A003266); other arguments stay unevaluated |
| `FindPermutation` | added | 6 | FindPermutation[e1, e2]: Cycles p with Permute[e1, p] == e2; FindPermutation[e] relative to Sort[e]; repeated elements (ambiguous) raise |
| `FrobeniusNumber` | added | 7 | largest integer not a non-negative integer combination of the a_i; -1 if some a_i = 1; Infinity when GCD > 1 (round-robin shortest paths over residues of the smallest a_i) |
| `FrobeniusSolve` | added | 6 | all non-negative integer solutions of a.x == b, sorted lexicographically; the 3-argument form (first m solutions) raises because Wolfram's selection order is not reproduced |
| `GroupElementPosition` | added | 3 | position of a permutation (or list of them) in GroupElements[group]; non-members raise |
| `GroupElementQ` | added | 5 | membership test for a permutation in a group |
| `GroupElements` | added | 16 | all elements as Cycles objects sorted by image lists (Wolfram's standard order for a sorted base; the identity is first); GroupElements[g, {r1, ...}] picks positions (negative from the end) |
| `GroupGenerators` | added | 4 | generators of a PermutationGroup (as given, identities removed), SymmetricGroup[n] ({Cycles[{{1, 2}}], Cycles[{{1, ..., n}}]}) and CyclicGroup[n]; other named groups raise because Wolfram's chosen generating sets are not reproduced |
| `GroupMultiplicationTable` | added | 5 | m[[i, j]] = position of PermutationProduct[g_i, g_j] in GroupElements (array indexing) |
| `GroupOrbits` | added | 3 | GroupOrbits[group] orbits of the support points; GroupOrbits[group, {p1, ...}] orbits of the given points; each orbit sorted, orbits ordered by smallest point |
| `GroupOrder` | added | 15 | order of SymmetricGroup (n!), AlternatingGroup (n!/2, 1 for n <= 2), CyclicGroup (n), DihedralGroup (2n), AbelianGroup (product) or a PermutationGroup (Schreier-Sims via SymPy) |
| `Hyperfactorial` | added | 3 | Product[k^k, {k, n}] for integers n >= 0 (Hyperfactorial[0] = 1); approximate real arguments via mpmath hyperfac; exact non-integers stay unevaluated |
| `InversePermutation` | added | 4 | inverse of a Cycles object or permutation list (same representation as the input) |
| `PermutationCycles` | added | 4 | disjoint-cycle form of a permutation list (i -> p_i) or canonical form of a Cycles object |
| `PermutationCyclesQ` | added | 5 | True for a valid Cycles object (disjoint positive integer cycles), else False |
| `PermutationGroup` | added | 13 | PermutationGroup[{g1, ...}] inert group generated by Cycles objects or permutation lists |
| `PermutationLength` | added | 3 | number of points moved |
| `PermutationList` | added | 6 | PermutationList[perm] (length PermutationMax) and PermutationList[perm, n] padded to length n; n smaller than the largest moved point raises |
| `PermutationListQ` | added | 5 | True for a rearrangement of Range[n] (n >= 0) |
| `PermutationMax` | added | 6 | largest moved point; identity -> -Infinity (Max of the empty support) |
| `PermutationMin` | added | 3 | smallest moved point; identity -> Infinity |
| `PermutationOrder` | added | 3 | LCM of the cycle lengths; identity -> 1 |
| `PermutationPower` | added | 5 | n-th power (n any integer, negative = inverse powers) |
| `PermutationProduct` | added | 11 | left-to-right product (apply the first permutation first); PermutationProduct[] = Cycles[{}], PermutationProduct[x] = x; all permutation lists give a list, otherwise Cycles |
| `PermutationReplace` | added | 5 | replaces each positive integer in expr (at any depth in lists) by its image; Cycles objects in expr are relabelled (conjugated); a group gives the list of images under GroupElements |
| `PermutationSupport` | added | 3 | sorted list of the points moved |
| `Permute` | added | 7 | Permute[expr, perm] moves part i to position perm(i) (Permute[{a, b, c}, {2, 3, 1}] = {c, a, b}); a group gives the list over GroupElements |
| `PolygonalNumber` | added | 5 | PolygonalNumber[n] = n (n + 1)/2 (triangular); PolygonalNumber[r, n] = n ((r - 2) n - (r - 4))/2; exact numeric arguments of any kind; symbolic arguments stay unevaluated |
| `RandomPermutation` | added | 5 | RandomPermutation[n] (uniform on Range[n], a Cycles object), [n, k] k of them, [group], [group, k]; reproducible after SeedRandom (the stream differs from Wolfram's generator) |
| `Signature` | added | 8 | sign (+1/-1) of the permutation that sorts the list into canonical order; 0 if any element is repeated |
| `SymmetricGroup` | added | 17 | SymmetricGroup[n] inert group object (permutation representation on 1..n) for GroupOrder, GroupElements, GroupMultiplicationTable, GroupOrbits, Permute, ... |

| `IntegerExponent` | added | 5 | default base 10; IntegerExponent[0, b] = Infinity; negative n uses \|n\| |

| `IntervalIntersection` | added | 4 |  |

| `NumberExpand` | added | 7 | integers only: digit values d b^k, sign carried by every term; length pads with leading zeros |
| `PerfectNumber` | added | 4 | k <= 48 (verified Mersenne primes) |
| `PerfectNumberQ` | added | 5 | even perfect numbers via the 48 known Mersenne prime exponents; odd n beyond 10^1500 raise |
| `PowerModList` | added | 7 | all x in [0, m) with x^q == a^p (mod m) for s = p/q; brute force for m <= 10^6 |
| `PowersRepresentations` | added | 5 | non-decreasing k-tuples of non-negative integers whose p-th powers sum to n, in lexicographic order |
| `PrimeNu` | added | 4 | number of distinct prime factors of \|n\|; 0 raises |
| `PrimeOmega` | added | 3 | prime factors of \|n\| counted with multiplicity; 0 raises |
| `PrimitiveRootList` | added | 6 | sorted primitive roots modulo n; {} when none exist; n <= 10^7 |
| `QuadraticIrrationalQ` | added | 10 | True for irrational real roots of rational quadratics; False for rationals, inexact and symbolic input and non-real numbers; raises when algebraicity cannot be decided |
| `RecurrenceTable` | added | 8 | explicit recurrences: each equation must contain exactly one term of maximal shift, which is solved for; initial conditions are f[k] == value; iterator {n, nmax} (from 1) or {n, nmin, nmax} |
| `RudinShapiro` | added | 3 | (-1)^(number of possibly overlapping 11 blocks) |
| `SquareFreeQ` | added | 10 | integers (0 is not square-free) and polynomials with integer content 1 (via sqf_list) |
| `SquaresR` | added | 6 | d <= 200, n <= 10^5 (exact counting) |

| `ThueMorse` | added | 3 |  |

| `Log2` | verified | 3 | log base 2; Log2[0] = -Infinity |
| `Max` | fixed | 18 | returned floats for exact input (Max[1, 2] = 2.0) and only took 1-2 arguments; now exact, variadic, flattens lists, Max[] = -Infinity |
| `Min` | fixed | 7 | returned floats for exact input; now exact, variadic, flattens lists, Min[] = Infinity |
| `Mod` | fixed | 27 | Mod[m, n] = m - n Floor[m/n] (sign of n); Mod[m, n, d] uses the offset d (result in [d, d + n)); complex m or n use Round (Gaussian remainder, Mod[5 + 3 I, 2] = 1 - I); Mod[m, 0] raises. The offset form and complex arguments were missing |
| `Quotient` | fixed | 12 | Quotient[m, n] = Floor[m/n] as an integer (legacy gave Quotient[7.5, 2] = 3.); Quotient[m, n, d] = Floor[(m - d)/n]; complex arguments use Round |
| `QuotientRemainder` | added | 4 | {Quotient[m, n], Mod[m, n]} |
| `RealPart` | verified | 3 | verified against SymPy/mpmath |
| `Root` | fixed | 43 | Wolfram Root[f, k]: the k-th root of the polynomial f[x] (f a pure function), ordered as Wolfram: real roots ascending, then complex roots by real part, then \|Im\|, conjugate pairs adjacent (negative imaginary part first); SymPy's CRootOf index differs and is remapped. The polynomial is reduced to the irreducible factor that has the root; roots of linear, quadratic and binomial (x^n - a) factors become radicals, other roots stay Root[minpoly &, j] and evaluate numerically with N. Approximate coefficients give a numeric root. Legacy Root[a, n] meant a^(1/n) (now Surd/Power) |
| `Round` | fixed | 16 | Round[x] was not implemented and Round[x, a] returned floats (Round[7, 5] = 5.0); now Wolfram half-to-even rounding, exact for exact input |
| `Sec` | verified | 3 | verified against SymPy/mpmath |
| `Sech` | verified | 3 | verified against SymPy/mpmath |
| `Sign` | fixed | 12 | Sign[z] = z/Abs[z] for complex z (legacy left Sign[3 + 4 I] unevaluated); integer -1, 0, 1 for reals |
| `Sin` | verified | 166 | verified against SymPy/mpmath |
| `Sinh` | verified | 4 | verified against SymPy/mpmath |
| `Sqrt` | verified | 93 | verified against SymPy/mpmath |
| `Surd` | promoted | 4 | real n-th root (Wolfram Surd) |
| `Tan` | verified | 16 | verified against SymPy/mpmath |
| `Tanh` | verified | 3 | verified against SymPy/mpmath |

### evaluation control, definitions, contexts (W01)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `$Context` | added | 23 | current context (default "Global`"); set by Begin, BeginPackage, End, EndPackage; assignable (validated); Block localises it |
| `$ContextAliases` | added | 23 | association "alias`" -> "context`" used when parsing qualified names; $ContextAliases["a`"] = "ctx`" and =. update it; Needs[ctx -> alias] adds one |
| `$ContextPath` | added | 23 | contexts searched (before $Context) when a name is parsed; default {"System`", "Global`"}; modified by BeginPackage, EndPackage, Needs, DeclarePackage |
| `$Epilog` | added | 3 | expression evaluated when the REPL session ends |
| `$HistoryLength` | added | 3 | number of In/Out lines kept by the main loop (default Infinity) |
| `$IterationLimit` | added | 6 | maximum length of an evaluation chain (default 4096): each rewrite of an expression by a definition or rule counts once; exceeding it raises $IterationLimit::itlim |
| `$Line` | added | 3 | number of the current main-loop input line; assignable |
| `$ModuleNumber` | added | 4 | the number the next Module or Unique symbol gets (x$nnn); assignable |
| `$NewSymbol` | added | 3 | if set, $NewSymbol[name, context] is evaluated before a new symbol is created by the parser |
| `$Packages` | added | 21 | contexts of loaded packages (BeginPackage adds; Needs consults it) |
| `$Post` | added | 3 | function applied to each main-loop result before it is stored in Out |
| `$Pre` | added | 3 | function applied to each main-loop input before evaluation (unevaluated if it holds) |
| `$PrePrint` | added | 3 | function applied to each main-loop result before it is printed (not stored) |
| `$PreRead` | added | 3 | function applied to each main-loop input string before it is parsed |
| `$RecursionLimit` | added | 4 | maximum evaluation depth (default 1024, Infinity or an integer >= 20); exceeding it raises $RecursionLimit::reclim. MikoshiLang counts nested evaluator calls |
| `Activate` | added | 9 | Activate[expr] replaces Inactive[f] by f everywhere in expr and evaluates; Activate[expr, patt] only for f matching patt; Heads -> False does not enter heads |
| `ActivateResult` | added | 3 | option name: whether Inactive results are activated before being returned |
| `AllowedHeads` | added | 3 | option name: heads of subexpressions into which a function may descend |
| `Application` | added | 3 | Application[f, g] is a formal application with no built-in meaning (stays unevaluated) |
| `ApplyTo` | added | 3 | x //= f sets x to f[x] and returns the new value (like x = f[x]); x must have a value |
| `ArgumentsOptions` | added | 4 | ArgumentsOptions[f[args], n] gives {{positional args}, {options}} (checked against Options[f]); a Failure["ArgumentsOptions", ...] when the count or an option is wrong; {min, max}; "Head" key |
| `Attributes` | added | 20 | Attributes[f], Attributes["f"], Attributes[{f, g}] (built-in Protected included, Temporary for live Module symbols, Stub, user attributes); Attributes[f] = {...} replaces them |
| `Automatic` | added | 30 | inert option value: chosen automatically by the function that uses it |
| `Begin` | added | 5 | Begin["ctx`"] sets $Context (a context starting with ` extends the current one) and returns it; End[] restores $Context and $ContextAliases. Affects how later inputs are parsed |
| `BeginPackage` | added | 6 | BeginPackage["ctx`"] sets $Context to ctx` and $ContextPath to {ctx`, System`}, adds ctx` to $Packages; BeginPackage["ctx`", {"need1`", ...}] calls Needs on each and puts them on the path |
| `CheckArguments` | added | 6 | CheckArguments[f[args], n] is True when args is n positional arguments followed by options known to f (Options[f]), else False; {min, max} counts; <\|"ExtraOptions" -> ..., "OptionsMode" -> ...\|> |
| `Constant` | added | 3 | attribute: Dt treats the symbol as a constant (Dt[c] = 0, Dt[c[x], x] = 0) |
| `Constants` | added | 5 | option Constants -> {...} of Dt and D (symbols treated as constant / as depending on the variable) |
| `Context` | added | 28 | Context[] gives $Context; Context[symbol] and Context["name"] the context of a symbol (System` for built-ins, Global` for unqualified user symbols); a name that does not exist raises |
| `Contexts` | added | 4 | Contexts[] and Contexts["pattern"]: the contexts of all existing symbols (and their parent contexts), $ContextPath and $Packages, sorted; ResolveContextAliases option |
| `CurryApplied` | added | 6 | CurryApplied[f, n][x1]...[xn] = f[x1, ..., xn]; CurryApplied[n][f] = CurryApplied[f, n]; CurryApplied[f, {i1, ...}] and k -> {i1, ...} reorder |
| `DeclarePackage` | added | 4 | DeclarePackage["ctx`", {"name1", ...}] creates ctx`name with attribute Stub and prepends ctx` to $ContextPath; the first use of such a symbol calls Needs["ctx`"] and evaluates it again |
| `Default` | added | 11 | Default[f], Default[f, i], Default[f, i, n]: the value an omitted x_. takes in f[...] (user values set by Default[f, ...] = v; Plus 0, Times 1, Power exponent 1); unevaluated when none is defined |
| `DefaultValues` | added | 4 | DefaultValues[f]: {HoldPattern[Default[f, ...]] :> value, ...} set by Default[f, ...] = value |
| `Defer` | added | 4 | Defer[expr] keeps expr unevaluated and prints as expr itself (InputForm/ToString show the contents); evaluating the printed form later evaluates it |
| `Definition` | added | 7 | Definition[f] stays as a held object that prints the definitions of f (Attributes, values with = or :=, upvalues with ^= / ^:=, Default, Options), separated by blank lines as in Wolfram; ToString gives the text; ReadProtected hides everything but the attributes |
| `Distribute` | added | 7 | Distribute[f[a + b, c + d]] = f[a, c] + f[a, d] + f[b, c] + f[b, d]; Distribute[expr, g] over g; Distribute[expr, g, f] only when the head is f; Distribute[expr, g, f, gp, fp] uses gp and fp in the result |
| `DownValues` | added | 8 | DownValues[f]: the rules HoldPattern[f[...]] :> rhs in the order they are tried (pattern-free definitions first, then by specificity); DownValues[f] = {...} replaces them |
| `End` | added | 5 | End[] returns the current context and restores $Context and $ContextAliases to their values before the matching Begin; an unmatched End raises |
| `EndPackage` | added | 6 | EndPackage[] restores $Context and $ContextPath to their values before BeginPackage and prepends the package context to $ContextPath |
| `ExcludedContexts` | added | 3 | FullDefinition option: contexts whose symbols are not followed (System` always) |
| `FilterRules` | added | 6 | FilterRules[rules, patt] keeps rules whose lhs matches patt; FilterRules[rules, {k1, ...}] keeps the named options; FilterRules[rules, Except[...]] removes them; FilterRules[rules, Options[f]] keeps f's |
| `Full` | added | 3 | inert option value (e.g. PlotRange -> Full) |
| `FullDefinition` | added | 10 | FullDefinition[f] prints the definitions of f and of every symbol they refer to, recursively (System` symbols excluded); options ExcludedContexts and IncludedContexts |
| `General` | added | 7 | symbol to which general messages are attached (General::tag) |
| `Get` | added | 5 | Get["file"] (<<file) reads a file of Wolfram Language input and evaluates each expression in turn, returning the last result (a relative file name is also searched for in PACKAGE_PATH and $MIKOSHILANG_PATH); Get["ctx`"] loads ctx.wl, ctx.m, ctx/init.m or ctx/Kernel/init.m from the working directory, PACKAGE_PATH or $MIKOSHILANG_PATH. A file that cannot be opened raises Get::noopen |
| `Goto` | added | 4 | Goto[tag] continues evaluation after Label[tag] in the enclosing compound expression (the innermost one containing that label, searching outwards); a missing label raises Goto::nolabel |
| `Heads` | added | 8 | option Heads -> True/False of Inactivate, Activate (and the level functions) |
| `HoldAllComplete` | added | 3 | attribute: no argument is evaluated, Sequence is not spliced, Unevaluated is kept and upvalues are not used |
| `HoldCompleteForm` | added | 5 | HoldCompleteForm[expr] keeps expr completely unevaluated (no Sequence splicing, no upvalues, Unevaluated kept) and prints as expr without the wrapper |
| `IgnoringInactive` | added | 3 | IgnoringInactive[patt] matches as patt with Inactive removed from both the pattern and the expression (Cases[{Sin[m], Inactive[Sin][p]}, IgnoringInactive[Sin[a_]]] gives both) |
| `In` | added | 3 | In[n] re-evaluates input line n of the main loop; In[] the last line, In[-k] earlier lines; unevaluated when there is no such line |
| `InString` | added | 3 | InString[n] is the text of input line n of the main loop; InString[] the last, InString[-k] earlier |
| `Inactivate` | added | 11 | Inactivate[expr] replaces every head f (except List, Rule, Blank and other structural heads) by Inactive[f] before evaluation; Inactivate[expr, patt] only heads matching patt (a symbol, Alternatives, Except[...]); Heads -> False leaves compound heads alone |
| `Inactive` | added | 30 | Inactive[f][args] is an inert form of f[args] (the arguments are evaluated, f is not applied); Inactive[atom] gives atom for atoms other than symbols |
| `IncludedContexts` | added | 3 | FullDefinition option: only symbols in these contexts are followed |
| `Indexed` | added | 8 | Indexed[list, i] gives list[[i]], Indexed[list, {i, j, ...}] indexes recursively; for a symbolic expr, Indexed[x, i] stays as the component x_i (Indexed[x, {i}] is the same as Indexed[x, i]) |
| `Information` | added | 8 | Information[symbol] (also ?f, ??f) gives InformationData[<\|ObjectType, Usage, Documentation, OwnValues, UpValues, DownValues, SubValues, DefaultValues, NValues, FormatValues, Options, Attributes, FullName\|>]; Information[s, "prop"] gives one property; Information["patt*"] a list; LongForm -> False keeps only the summary. Usage messages (f::usage) are not available |
| `InformationData` | added | 3 | InformationData[assoc, False]["prop"] gives a property of an Information result |
| `Inherited` | added | 3 | inert option value: inherited from an enclosing setting |
| `InverseFunction` | added | 8 | InverseFunction[f]: ArcSin for Sin etc. (named inverses), the solved inverse of a pure function with a unique inverse (InverseFunction[2 # + 3 &] = (#1 - 3)/2 &), InverseFunction[InverseFunction[f]] = f; InverseFunction[f, n, tot] for the n-th of tot arguments; otherwise stays symbolic (InverseFunction[f][x]) |
| `InverseFunctions` | added | 3 | option name of Solve and Reduce: whether InverseFunction objects may be used |
| `Label` | added | 5 | Label[tag] marks a point in a compound expression for Goto[tag]; evaluates to Null |
| `LocalSymbol` | added | 6 | LocalSymbol["name"] gives the value stored persistently (a file under ~/.mikoshilang/LocalSymbols, or $MIKOSHILANG_LOCALSYMBOL_BASE) by LocalSymbol["name"] = val or := val; unevaluated when never assigned; LocalSymbol["name", "dir"] uses another base; =. deletes it |
| `Locked` | added | 5 | attribute: the attributes of the symbol can no longer be changed (Protect/Unprotect, SetAttributes, ClearAttributes, Attributes[f] = ... raise) |
| `Manual` | added | 3 | inert option value: selected manually |
| `Method` | added | 14 | option name Method -> "name" / {"name", opts} / Automatic for algorithm choices |
| `NHoldAll` | added | 3 | attribute: N does not affect any argument of f |
| `NHoldFirst` | added | 3 | attribute: N does not affect the first argument of f |
| `NHoldRest` | added | 3 | attribute: N affects only the first argument of f |
| `NameQ` | added | 4 | NameQ["string"]: True if any symbol name matches (abbreviated string patterns); ResolveContextAliases option |
| `Names` | added | 9 | Names["pattern"] (metacharacters * and @, a context prefix such as "Global`*", StringExpression or RegularExpression), Names[{p1, ...}], Names[] ; options IgnoreCase, ResolveContextAliases, SpellingCorrection -> False. Names are short when their context is $Context or on $ContextPath. System` contains the symbols MikoshiLang implements |
| `Needs` | added | 4 | Needs["ctx`"] loads the package (Get["ctx`"]) unless ctx` is in $Packages and puts ctx` on $ContextPath; Needs["ctx`" -> "alias`"] adds a context alias instead; Needs["ctx`" -> None] only loads; Needs["ctx`", "file"] loads the given file |
| `NonConstants` | added | 6 | option NonConstants -> {...} of Dt and D (symbols treated as constant / as depending on the variable) |
| `NonThreadable` | added | 3 | attribute: arithmetic and other Listable functions do not thread lists over f or f[...]; the threadable arguments still combine |
| `Once` | added | 5 | Once[expr] evaluates expr the first time and afterwards returns the cached result for the rest of the session (Once[expr, "KernelSession"]); other persistence locations raise; Once[expr] =. clears |
| `OperatorApplied` | added | 7 | OperatorApplied[f][y][x] = f[x, y]; OperatorApplied[f, n][x1]...[xn] = f[x1, ..., xn]; OperatorApplied[f, {i1, ...}] and k -> {i1, ...} reorder; bracket structure is irrelevant |
| `OptionValue` | added | 9 | OptionValue[name] inside f[..., OptionsPattern[]] := rhs gives the value of option name in the call (or f's default); OptionValue[f, name], OptionValue[f, opts, name], lists of names; OptionsPattern[{f, g}] searches several defaults |
| `Options` | added | 25 | Options[f] gives the default option rules of a user symbol (set by Options[f] = {...} or SetOptions); Options[f, name] or Options[f, {names}] selects; Options[expr] gives the options at the end of expr (f[x, a -> 1] gives {a -> 1}). Built-in option lists are not catalogued and raise |
| `OptionsPattern` | added | 9 | OptionsPattern[] matches a sequence of zero or more rules or lists of rules; OptionsPattern[f] or OptionsPattern[{f, ...}] names where OptionValue finds defaults |
| `Out` | added | 3 | Out[n] (%n) is the n-th output of the main loop (Session.evaluate_input / REPL / Jupyter), Out[] (%) the last, Out[-k] (%%...) the k-th previous; unevaluated when there is no such output |
| `OwnValues` | added | 4 | OwnValues[x]: {HoldPattern[x] :> value} for a symbol with a value, else {}; OwnValues[x] = {...} sets it |
| `Postfix` | added | 3 | Postfix[f[x]] prints as x//f and Postfix[f[x], h] as x<>h (display wrapper) |
| `Prefix` | added | 3 | Prefix[f[x]] prints as f@x and Prefix[f[x], h] as h<>x (display wrapper; the expression inside is evaluated) |
| `Protect` | added | 6 | Protect[s1, s2, ...] (symbols, names or string patterns) sets the Protected attribute (assignments then raise); returns the names whose protection changed |
| `ReadProtected` | added | 3 | attribute: Definition, FullDefinition and Information show only the attributes; the values are still used in evaluation and DownValues etc. still return them |
| `Remove` | added | 4 | Remove[s1, ...] removes symbols completely (values, attributes, options, the name: Names no longer lists it); Remove["ctx`*"] removes by pattern; protected symbols raise Remove::rmptc |
| `ResolveContextAliases` | added | 3 | option of Names, NameQ and Contexts: False ignores $ContextAliases |
| `SetOptions` | added | 4 | SetOptions[f, name -> value, ...] resets defaults that f already has (SetOptions::optnf for an unknown option), returning the new Options[f]; SetOptions[{f, g}, ...] sets several |
| `Stack` | added | 11 | Stack[] gives the tags (heads) of the evaluations in progress, outermost first, excluding Stack itself; Stack[patt] the expressions matching patt wrapped in HoldCompleteForm (Stack[_] all); MikoshiLang's internal evaluations made by library code also appear |
| `StackBegin` | added | 7 | StackBegin[expr] evaluates expr with a fresh evaluation stack (outer evaluations are not shown by Stack) |
| `StackComplete` | added | 3 | StackComplete[expr] evaluates expr keeping the earlier expressions of each evaluation chain on the stack (f[2] and g[4] both appear after f[x_] := g[x^2]) |
| `StackInhibit` | added | 3 | StackInhibit[expr] evaluates expr without adding its evaluations to the stack |
| `Stub` | added | 4 | attribute set by DeclarePackage: using the symbol loads its package |
| `SubValues` | added | 3 | SubValues[f]: rules for f[...][...] (HoldPattern[lhs] :> rhs); SubValues[f] = {...} replaces them |
| `SubValuesHoldAll` | added | 4 | attribute: in f[a...][s...], the subvalue arguments s are held (Unevaluated stripped, Sequence spliced) |
| `SyntaxInformation` | added | 4 | SyntaxInformation[f] gives the syntax properties set by SyntaxInformation[f] = {"ArgumentsPattern" -> {...}, ...} ({} if none); used only for advisories, not evaluation. Built-in syntax data is not catalogued and raises |
| `TagSet` | added | 4 | f /: lhs = rhs: attaches the rule to f, which must be the head of lhs (a DownValue or SubValue) or an argument of lhs or an argument's head (an UpValue); otherwise TagSet::tagnf is raised |
| `TagSetDelayed` | added | 3 | f /: lhs := rhs (delayed TagSet); returns Null |
| `TagUnset` | added | 3 | f /: lhs =. removes the rule for lhs attached to f (an UpValue, DownValue or SubValue); raises if there is none |
| `Temporary` | added | 3 | attribute of Module local symbols (and Unique[x, {Temporary}]); reported by Attributes |
| `Threaded` | added | 6 | f[a, Threaded[b]] for Listable f combines b with the lowest-level subarrays of a; Threaded[b, alev] and Threaded[b, 1 -> alev] choose the level (other blev raise) |
| `TraceAbove` | added | 3 | Trace option: True adds the first and last forms of every chain containing a match; All adds all forms of those chains |
| `TraceBackward` | added | 4 | Trace option: True also keeps the earlier forms of a chain in which form matched; All also their subevaluations |
| `TraceDepth` | added | 3 | Trace option: TraceDepth -> n keeps only the first n levels of nested lists |
| `TraceForward` | added | 3 | Trace option: True also keeps the later forms of a chain in which form matched; All also their subevaluations |
| `TraceOff` | added | 3 | Trace option: TraceOff -> patt does not trace inside evaluations of forms matching patt (the forms themselves and their results are kept) |
| `TraceOn` | added | 3 | Trace option: TraceOn -> patt traces only inside evaluations of forms matching patt |
| `TracePrint` | added | 3 | TracePrint[expr] prints the forms of the evaluation of expr indented by nesting level and returns the value; TracePrint[expr, form] only matching forms; Trace options accepted (forms are printed after evaluation completes) |
| `TraceScan` | added | 4 | TraceScan[f, expr] applies f to HoldForm of every form in the evaluation of expr and returns its value; TraceScan[f, expr, form] only matching forms; TraceScan[f, expr, form, fp] also applies fp to (HoldForm[form], HoldForm[result]) of each matching chain; f runs after the evaluation completes |
| `Unevaluated` | added | 6 | Unevaluated[expr] passes expr unevaluated as an argument of a function; the wrapper is removed when the function uses it, kept when the function returns the expression unchanged (Length[Unevaluated[1 + 2]] = 2) |
| `Unique` | added | 11 | Unique[] gives $nnn, Unique[x] gives x$nnn (using $ModuleNumber, then incrementing it), Unique["xxx"] gives xxxn with n counting up from 1 per prefix, Unique[{x, y}] gives a list; Unique[x, {attrs}] sets attributes |
| `Unprotect` | added | 4 | Unprotect[s1, ...] removes Protected, also from built-in symbols (definitions for them are then used before the built-in rules); returns the names whose protection changed. Special forms evaluated by the kernel itself (If, Set, Module, ...) still cannot get definitions |
| `Until` | added | 6 | Until[test, body] evaluates body then test, repeating until test is True (at least once); Until[test] has an empty body; Break[] exits, Continue[] skips to the test; returns Null |
| `UpSet` | added | 4 | lhs ^= rhs: rhs is evaluated and the rule is attached (as an upvalue) to every symbol that is an argument of lhs or the head of an argument (h[x_] in f[h[x_]] attaches to h); returns rhs |
| `UpSetDelayed` | added | 3 | lhs ^:= rhs: like UpSet with rhs kept unevaluated until the rule is used; returns Null |
| `UpValues` | added | 8 | UpValues[f]: rules attached to f by UpSet/TagSet (HoldPattern[lhs] :> rhs); UpValues[f] = {...} replaces them |
| `Update` | added | 3 | Update[s] / Update[] tell the system that values depending on s may have changed; MikoshiLang keeps no evaluation caches that could go stale, so this returns Null |
| `ValueQ` | added | 15 | ValueQ[expr] is True when expr has a value: a symbol with an OwnValue, or an expression changed by evaluation (Method -> "Legacy" default compares the result; "SymbolDefinitionsPresent" and "TrialEvaluation" also accepted) |

### finance

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `APRtoEAR` | fixed | 3 | alias of EffectiveRate; exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `AnnuityFV` | fixed | 3 | PMT ((1 + r)^n - 1)/r (rate 0 -> PMT n); exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `AnnuityPV` | fixed | 3 | PMT (1 - (1 + r)^-n)/r (rate 0 -> PMT n); exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `BondPrice` | fixed | 3 | sum of discounted annual coupons plus discounted face value; years must be a positive integer (legacy truncated with int()) |
| `BondYieldApprox` | fixed | 3 | approximate yield to maturity (C + (F - P)/n)/((F + P)/2); exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `CAGR` | fixed | 3 | (end/start)^(1/years) - 1; exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `CompoundInterest` | fixed | 3 | P (1 + r)^t (amount); exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `ContinuousCompounding` | fixed | 3 | P E^(r t); exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `DoublingTime` | fixed | 3 | Log[2]/Log[1 + r]; exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `EffectiveRate` | fixed | 3 | (1 + r/m)^m - 1; exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `FutureValue` | fixed | 3 | PV (1 + r)^n; exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `GrowingAnnuityPV` | fixed | 3 | PMT (1 - ((1 + g)/(1 + r))^n)/(r - g); exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `GrowingPerpetuityPV` | fixed | 3 | PMT/(r - g); exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `IRR` | fixed | 3 | the rate r > -1 with NPV = 0, found exactly from the cash-flow polynomial and returned as a machine number; no root or several roots raise (legacy ran Newton from 10% and returned whatever it reached, rounded) |
| `LoanPayment` | fixed | 3 | P r (1 + r)^n/((1 + r)^n - 1) (rate 0 -> P/n; legacy divided by zero); exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `NPV` | fixed | 3 | NPV[r, {cf0, cf1, ...}] = Sum[cf_t/(1 + r)^t] with cf0 undiscounted at t = 0; exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `PMT` | fixed | 3 | PMT[rate, periods, pv] payment per period; exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `PerpetuityPV` | fixed | 3 | PMT/r; exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `PresentValue` | fixed | 3 | FV/(1 + r)^n; exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |
| `RemainingBalance` | fixed | 3 | balance after k payments; exact for exact input; legacy rounded to cents/6 digits or used floats |

### geometry

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CircleArea` | fixed | 3 | Pi r^2 (exact); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `CircleCircumference` | fixed | 3 | 2 Pi r (exact); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `ConeSurfaceArea` | fixed | 3 | total area Pi r (r + slant), exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `ConeVolume` | fixed | 3 | Pi r^2 h/3 (exact); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `CubeSurfaceArea` | fixed | 3 | 6 a^2, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `CubeVolume` | fixed | 3 | a^3, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `CylinderSurfaceArea` | fixed | 3 | 2 Pi r (r + h), exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `CylinderVolume` | fixed | 3 | Pi r^2 h (exact); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `Distance` | fixed | 3 | Euclidean distance, exact (legacy rounded floats and zip-truncated) |
| `Distance3D` | fixed | 3 | Euclidean distance of 3D points, exact |
| `EllipsoidVolume` | fixed | 3 | 4/3 Pi a b c, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |

| `Hypotenuse` | fixed | 3 | Sqrt[a^2 + b^2], exact (legacy rounded floats); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `LineSphereIntersection` | fixed | 4 | intersection points of p + t d with the sphere \|x - c\| = r, exact; tangency gives one point; zero direction raises (legacy divided by zero) |

| `Midpoint3D` | fixed | 3 | exact midpoint of 3D points |
| `PlaneEquation` | fixed | 3 | n.({x, y, z} - p) == 0 expanded, exact (legacy converted to floats) |
| `PointToPlaneDistance` | fixed | 3 | \|a x0 + b y0 + c z0 + d\|/Sqrt[a^2 + b^2 + c^2] for plane {a, b, c, d}, exact |
| `PolygonArea` | fixed | 3 | shoelace formula, exact (legacy rounded floats) |
| `PrismVolume` | fixed | 3 | base area x height, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `PyramidVolume` | fixed | 3 | base area x height/3, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `RegularPolygonArea` | fixed | 3 | n s^2/(4 Tan[Pi/n]), exact; n must be an integer >= 3 (legacy truncated n and rounded) |
| `Slope` | fixed | 3 | (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1), exact; a vertical line now raises (legacy returned +Infinity even for downward lines) |
| `SphereSurfaceArea` | fixed | 3 | 4 Pi r^2 (exact); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `SphereVolume` | fixed | 3 | 4/3 Pi r^3 (exact); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `TetrahedronVolume` | fixed | 3 | regular tetrahedron a^3/(6 Sqrt[2]), exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `TorusSurfaceArea` | fixed | 3 | 4 Pi^2 R r, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `TorusVolume` | fixed | 3 | 2 Pi^2 R r^2, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats); negative lengths raise (legacy returned e.g. CircleArea[-1] = Pi) |
| `TriangleArea` | fixed | 3 | Heron's formula, exact; sides violating the triangle inequality raise (legacy returned a complex number or a math domain error) |

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BetweennessCentrality` | fixed | 9 | Graph or edge-rule input: list of unnormalised betweenness centralities in VertexList order (machine reals; directed graphs count ordered pairs; EdgeWeight respected); legacy: {v -> c} unnormalised betweenness (Wolfram BetweennessCentrality); legacy returned NetworkX's normalised values; exact rationals (Brandes, unweighted) |
| `BipartiteQ` | verified | 3 | 2-colourability test (BFS) |
| `ChromaticNumber` | fixed | 4 | exact chromatic number by backtracking (legacy used a greedy colouring, an upper bound only); graphs with more than 40 vertices raise |
| `ClusteringCoefficient` | fixed | 3 | ClusteringCoefficient[g] = mean local clustering (vertices of degree < 2 count 0, as Wolfram MeanClusteringCoefficient), [g, v] local coefficient; exact rationals (legacy floats) |
| `ConnectedComponents` | fixed | 9 | Graph or edge-rule input: connected components (strongly connected for directed graphs), largest first, vertices in VertexList order; legacy: components largest first (Wolfram), vertices in order of appearance; legacy returned NetworkX set order |
| `ConnectedQ` | verified | 3 | True when the graph is connected |
| `Dijkstra` | fixed | 3 | {v -> distance} for every reachable vertex, in vertex order |
| `GraphCenter` | fixed | 7 | vertices of minimum eccentricity; disconnected graphs raise; legacy: vertices of minimum eccentricity; disconnected graphs raise (legacy returned {} silently) |
| `GraphDiameter` | fixed | 7 | maximum eccentricity (Infinity when not (strongly) connected); weighted with EdgeWeight; legacy: maximum eccentricity; Infinity for disconnected graphs (legacy returned None) |
| `GraphRadius` | fixed | 7 | minimum eccentricity (Infinity when not (strongly) connected); legacy: minimum eccentricity; Infinity for disconnected graphs (legacy returned None) |
| `MaximalMatching` | verified | 3 | a maximum-cardinality matching (NetworkX max_weight_matching with unit weights), edges sorted; requires NetworkX |
| `MinimumSpanningTree` | fixed | 3 | edges of a minimum spanning forest (Kruskal, weights {u, v, w} supported); legacy needed NetworkX |
| `PageRank` | fixed | 3 | {v -> rank} PageRank with damping 0.85 treating each edge as two directed links (power iteration to 1e-12, matches NetworkX); legacy required NetworkX |
| `PathLength` | fixed | 3 | (weighted) shortest-path distance, Infinity when unreachable; unknown vertices raise |
| `ShortestPath` | fixed | 4 | vertex list of a shortest (weighted) path, {} when unreachable (Wolfram FindShortestPath); unknown vertices raise |
| `SpanningTree` | fixed | 3 | edges of a spanning forest (Kruskal) |
| `VertexDegree` | fixed | 12 | VertexDegree[g] = degrees in VertexList order, VertexDegree[g, v]; self-loops count 2, multi-edges counted; directed: in + out; legacy: number of incident edges (self-loops count twice, multi-edges counted); unknown vertices raise |

| `Optional` | added | 3 | x_:d and x_. (Plus/Times/Power defaults) |
| `Repeated` | added | 3 | p.. and Repeated[p, n \| {m, n}] in sequences |
| `RepeatedNull` | added | 3 | p... in sequences |
| `RightComposition` | added | 3 | f /* g; RightComposition[f, g][x] = g[f[x]] |
| `SameAs` | added | 3 | operator x === y |
| `Span` | added | 3 | i ;; j ;; k in Part, Take and Drop |
| `Splice` | added | 4 | spliced into lists |
| `UnequalTo` | added | 3 | operator x != y |
| `UpTo` | added | 11 | UpTo[n] in Take, Drop, Partition, ... |

### linear algebra

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CharacteristicPolynomial` | fixed | 4 | legacy returned Det[x I - m]; Wolfram CharacteristicPolynomial[m, x] = Det[m - x I] (differs by (-1)^n) |
| `CholeskyDecomposition` | fixed | 3 | legacy returned the lower factor; Wolfram returns the upper triangular u with ConjugateTranspose[u].u = m; non-Hermitian or non-positive-definite matrices raise |
| `ColumnSpace` | verified | 3 | basis of the column space (pivot columns; MikoshiLang) |
| `ConditionNumber` | fixed | 3 | 2-norm condition number sigma_max/sigma_min (MikoshiLang); exact for exact matrices, Infinity for singular matrices (legacy returned a huge float or an Error expression without NumPy) |
| `Cross` | fixed | 3 | 3D cross product, exact; Cross[{x, y}] = {-y, x}; non-3D vectors raise |
| `Cross3D` | fixed | 3 | alias of Cross; legacy converted to floats and returned an Error expression for bad input |
| `CrossProduct` | fixed | 3 | alias of Cross (exact) |
| `DiagonalMatrix` | fixed | 4 | DiagonalMatrix[list] and DiagonalMatrix[list, k] (k-th diagonal) |
| `DiagonalMatrixQ` | fixed | 3 | False for non-matrices (legacy raised); exact test on the entries |
| `Eigenvalues` | fixed | 13 | eigenvalues with multiplicity in Wolfram order (decreasing absolute value; legacy returned SymPy's dict order). Eigenvalues[m, k] gives the k largest in absolute value (k < 0: the \|k\| smallest, i.e. Take[Eigenvalues[m], k]); Eigenvalues[m, UpTo[k]]; generalized {m, a} raises |
| `Eigenvectors` | fixed | 6 | ordered like Eigenvalues, exact vectors scaled so the last non-zero entry is 1, zero vectors pad defective matrices (Wolfram); machine matrices give unit vectors; Eigenvectors[m, k] and UpTo[k] as for Eigenvalues |
| `FrobeniusNorm` | fixed | 3 | exact Frobenius norm (legacy returned floats) |
| `HermitianQ` | fixed | 3 | False for non-matrices (legacy raised); exact test on the entries |
| `HessenbergDecomposition` | fixed | 3 | Wolfram {p, h} with m = p.h.ConjugateTranspose[p] (legacy returned {H -> h, Q -> q} rules); numeric |
| `IdentityMatrix` | fixed | 13 | IdentityMatrix[n] and IdentityMatrix[{m, n}]; non-integer sizes raise (legacy truncated) |
| `JordanForm` | verified | 3 | {s, j} with m = s.j.Inverse[s] (Wolfram JordanDecomposition convention) |
| `KroneckerProduct` | fixed | 4 | variadic Kronecker product of matrices and/or vectors (vectors give vectors, as Wolfram) |
| `LUDecomposition` | fixed | 3 | legacy ignored the row permutation (L.U != m whenever pivoting occurs); now Wolfram's {lu, p, c}: lu holds L (unit lower, below the diagonal) and U, m[[p]] == L.U, c = 0 for exact matrices and the infinity-norm condition number for machine matrices |
| `LinearSolve` | fixed | 11 | solves m.x == b for a vector or matrix b; underdetermined systems give one particular solution (free parameters 0, as Wolfram); inconsistent systems raise |
| `LowerTriangularMatrixQ` | fixed | 3 | False for non-matrices (legacy raised); exact test on the entries |
| `MatrixCos` | fixed | 3 | exact (legacy summed a truncated Taylor series) |
| `MatrixExp` | fixed | 5 | exact via the Jordan form (SymPy); machine matrices via SciPy expm |
| `MatrixLog` | fixed | 3 | principal logarithm; legacy discarded the imaginary part (MatrixLog[{{-1, 0}, {0, 1}}] was wrong) |
| `MatrixNullity` | verified | 3 | columns - rank (MikoshiLang) |
| `MatrixPower` | fixed | 3 | integer powers (negative powers of singular matrices raise); symbolic n via diagonalisation |
| `MatrixRank` | verified | 4 | exact rank; machine matrices use an SVD tolerance; W05: option ZeroTest -> f (f decides which pivot candidates are zero) |
| `MatrixSin` | fixed | 3 | exact (legacy summed a truncated Taylor series) |
| `MatrixSqrt` | fixed | 3 | principal square root; legacy discarded the imaginary part |
| `MatrixTan` | fixed | 3 | exact (legacy summed a truncated Taylor series) |
| `Norm` | fixed | 13 | vectors: Norm[v] and Norm[v, p] (p >= 1 or Infinity); matrices: Norm[m] is the spectral 2-norm as in Wolfram (legacy flattened the matrix, giving the Frobenius norm), Norm[m, 1], Norm[m, Infinity], Norm[m, "Frobenius"] |
| `Normalize` | fixed | 3 | v / Norm[v]; the zero vector is returned unchanged as in Wolfram (legacy divided by zero); Normalize[z] for a number gives z/Abs[z] |
| `NullSpace` | fixed | 4 | basis vectors in Wolfram order (legacy reversed it), exact; empty list for full column rank; W05: option ZeroTest -> f (f decides which pivot candidates are zero) |
| `PseudoInverse` | verified | 3 | Moore-Penrose pseudo-inverse (exact; NumPy for machine matrices) |
| `QRDecomposition` | fixed | 3 | legacy returned {Q, R} with m = Q.R; Wolfram returns {q, r} with m = ConjugateTranspose[q].r (q has orthonormal rows) |
| `Rank` | verified | 3 | MikoshiLang alias of MatrixRank |
| `RowReduce` | verified | 6 | reduced row echelon form; W05: option ZeroTest -> f (f decides which pivot candidates are zero) |
| `RowSpace` | verified | 3 | basis of the row space (non-zero rows of the RREF; MikoshiLang) |
| `SVD` | fixed | 3 | MikoshiLang alias of SingularValueDecomposition; Wolfram {u, s, v} with m = u.s.ConjugateTranspose[v] and singular values in decreasing order (legacy did not order them); machine matrices give the full SVD |
| `ScalarTripleProduct` | fixed | 3 | a.(b x c), exact (legacy converted to floats) |
| `SchurDecomposition` | fixed | 3 | Wolfram {q, t} with m = q.t.ConjugateTranspose[q] (legacy returned {T -> t, Z -> z} rules); numeric, real Schur form for real matrices |
| `SingularValueDecomposition` | fixed | 3 | Wolfram {u, s, v} with m = u.s.ConjugateTranspose[v] and singular values in decreasing order (legacy did not order them); machine matrices give the full SVD |
| `SpectralNorm` | fixed | 3 | largest singular value, exact for exact matrices (legacy always returned a float via NumPy) |
| `SymmetricMatrixQ` | fixed | 4 | False for non-matrices (legacy raised); exact test on the entries |
| `Tr` | promoted | 5 | trace of a matrix (sum of the diagonal); Tr[list] sums a vector |
| `Trace` | fixed | 36 | Wolfram evaluation trace: Trace[expr] evaluates expr and returns the chain of forms as HoldForm[...] with subevaluations as nested lists (Trace[1 + 2 3] -> {{2 3, 6}, 1 + 6, 7}); Trace[expr, form] keeps only forms matching the pattern. Internal evaluations made by library functions can add extra steps that Wolfram would not show. For the matrix trace use Tr (legacy Trace[m] meant Tr[m]); options TraceDepth, TraceForward, TraceBackward, TraceAbove, TraceOn, TraceOff (W01) |
| `TransposeList` | fixed | 3 | transpose of a rectangular list of lists (MikoshiLang); ragged input now raises (legacy zip-truncated) |
| `UpperTriangularMatrixQ` | fixed | 3 | False for non-matrices (legacy raised); exact test on the entries |
| `VectorAngle` | fixed | 4 | ArcCos[u.Conjugate[v]/(\|u\| \|v\|)]; zero vectors and different lengths raise |
| `ZeroMatrix` | fixed | 3 | m x n zero matrix (MikoshiLang); ZeroMatrix[n] is n x n; non-integer sizes raise |
