| `CheckAbort` | added | 14 | CheckAbort[expr, failexpr] (HoldAll) returns failexpr if an abort occurs in expr; option PropagateAborts: Automatic (default: absorb Abort[], propagate constraint aborts after failexpr), True (propagate all), False (absorb all) |
| `ClearSystemCache` | added | 4 | ClearSystemCache[] clears MikoshiLang's internal caches (SymPy's cache and the evaluator memo table); ClearSystemCache["Symbolic"] / ["Numeric"] clear the symbolic (SymPy) / numeric (mpmath) caches; returns Null |
| `Confirm` | added | 10 | Confirm[expr] returns expr unless it is a Failure, Missing, $Failed, $Canceled or $Aborted, in which case Failure["ConfirmationFailed", <\|...\|>] is thrown to the lexically enclosing Enclose (info is evaluated only on failure; a ConfirmationFailed failure with Null info is rethrown unchanged); Confirm[expr, info, tag] is caught dynamically by Enclose[..., f, tag]; with no tag and no Enclose: message confirmnotag |
| `ConfirmAssert` | added | 3 | ConfirmAssert[test] returns Null if test is True, else throws Failure["ConfirmationFailed", <\|"MessageTemplate" -> "Assertion failed.", ..., "Test" -> value\|>] to the enclosing Enclose |
| `ConfirmBy` | added | 3 | ConfirmBy[expr, f] returns expr if f[expr] is True, else throws Failure["ConfirmationFailed", ...] with "Expression" and "Function" to the enclosing Enclose; info/tag as for Confirm |
| `ConfirmMatch` | added | 3 | ConfirmMatch[expr, form] returns expr if it matches form, else throws Failure["ConfirmationFailed", ...] with "Expression" and "Pattern" to the enclosing Enclose; info/tag as for Confirm |
| `ConfirmQuiet` | added | 4 | ConfirmQuiet[expr, mspec] evaluates expr and returns it unless a matching message is generated (not switched off and not quieted inside); evaluation stops at the first such message, which is quieted, and Failure["ConfirmationFailed", ...] with "HeldExpression", "HeldMessageName" and "HeldMessageCall" is thrown to the enclosing Enclose; a raised MikoshiLang error counts as f::evalerr |
| `Echo` | added | 5 | Echo[expr] prints >> expr and returns expr; Echo[expr, label] prints >> label  expr; Echo[expr, label, f] prints f[expr]; output is suppressed inside QuietEcho |
| `EchoEvaluation` | added | 4 | EchoEvaluation[expr] (HoldFirst) prints << expr before and >> result after evaluating (nested calls use <<<< / >>>>); EchoEvaluation[expr, l1 -> l2], EchoEvaluation[expr, labels, f] and EchoEvaluation[expr, labels, g -> f] as documented; returns the result |
| `EchoFunction` | added | 3 | operator form: EchoFunction[f][expr] prints >> f[expr], EchoFunction[label, f][expr] prints >> label  f[expr]; both return expr; EchoFunction[][expr] is Echo[expr] |
| `EchoLabel` | added | 3 | operator form: EchoLabel[label][expr] prints >> label  expr and returns expr; EchoLabel[][expr] is Echo[expr] |
| `EchoTiming` | added | 3 | EchoTiming[expr] / EchoTiming[expr, label] (HoldFirst) evaluates expr, prints >> [label  ]seconds and returns the result; Method -> AbsoluteTiming (default), Timing, RepeatedTiming or a holding function returning {timing, result} |
| `Enclose` | added | 23 | Enclose[expr] (HoldAll) evaluates expr; an untagged confirmation lexically inside throws its Failure to it (untagged confirmations are rewritten with a private tag, inner untagged Enclose blocks keep their own); Enclose[expr, f] gives f[failure] (f a string: failure[f]); Enclose[expr, f, form] catches only confirmations whose tag matches form, dynamically |
| `Exception` | added | 9 | Exception[spec] / Exception[spec, payload] builds the validated object Exception[{tags...}, <\|data..., "ExceptionValidated" -> True, "ExceptionAPIVersion" -> "1"\|>] with tag expansion through registered parent types; spec may be a tag, a list of tags, an Exception, a Failure carrying "ExceptionTagList" or an association; malformed input issues Exception::untagged / Exception::invargs and stays unevaluated; exc["prop"] gives the documented properties |
| `ExceptionQ` | added | 7 | ExceptionQ[expr] is True for a validated Exception object; ExceptionQ[expr, tag \| {tags}] also requires one of the tags in its (expanded) tag list |
| `ExceptionTypeRegisteredQ` | added | 4 | ExceptionTypeRegisteredQ[sym] is True if sym is a registered exception type; with a second argument, True if it is registered and one of the given types is among its ancestors |
| `ExceptionTypes` | added | 4 | ExceptionTypes[] / ExceptionTypes[All] lists the registered exception types in registration order; ExceptionTypes[tag \| {tags}] lists the registered types that are the given types or their descendants |
| `Failsafe` | added | 8 | operator form: Failsafe[f][x1, ...] gives f[x1, ...] unless some xi is a failure (Failure, Missing, $Failed, $Canceled, $Aborted), then the first such xi; Failsafe[f, test][...] gives Failure["FailsafeFailed", ...] when test[x1, ...] is not True; Failsafe[f, test, failf] gives failf[...] |
| `Failure` | added | 8 | Failure["tag", assoc] is inert; failure["prop"] gives assoc values and the derived properties "Message" (template applied to "MessageParameters"), "MessageTemplate", "MessageName", "HeldMessageTemplate", "Tag" and "Properties" (sorted); failure[{p1, p2}] gives a list |
| `FailureQ` | added | 6 | FailureQ[expr] is True for Failure[...], $Failed and $Aborted, False otherwise |
| `HandlerFunctions` | added | 7 | option of TestReport: <\|event -> f, ...\|> handlers for the report's events; inert as an expression |
| `HandlerFunctionsKeys` | added | 4 | option of TestReport: the keys of the association passed to the handler functions (Automatic or a list); inert as an expression |
| `IntermediateTest` | added | 6 | IntermediateTest[input, expected, messages, opts] (HoldAllComplete) evaluates a test (without printing messages) and returns the evaluated input; inside VerificationTest / TestEvaluate a failing intermediate test makes the enclosing test fail (IntermediateTestFailure / IntermediateTestMessagesFailure) |
| `Interrupt` | added | 3 | Interrupt[] generates an interactive interrupt: in MikoshiLang (Python) that is KeyboardInterrupt, the same effect as pressing Ctrl-C at that point |
| `MaxMemoryUsed` | added | 3 | MaxMemoryUsed[] gives the peak resident memory (bytes) of the process (getrusage); MaxMemoryUsed[expr] (HoldAll) gives the peak additional Python heap bytes (tracemalloc) used while evaluating expr |
| `MemoryAvailable` | added | 3 | MemoryAvailable[] gives the bytes the operating system reports as available (MemAvailable in /proc/meminfo on Linux, psutil elsewhere) |
| `MemoryConstrained` | added | 5 | MemoryConstrained[expr, b] / MemoryConstrained[expr, b, failexpr] (HoldAll) evaluates expr, aborting it when the additional Python heap memory it allocates (tracemalloc) exceeds b bytes, and returns $Aborted (or failexpr); b may be Infinity or a byte Quantity |
| `MemoryConstraint` | added | 6 | option of the test functions: bytes a test may allocate (default Infinity; exceeding it gives MemoryConstraintFailure); inert as an expression |
| `MemoryInUse` | added | 3 | MemoryInUse[] gives the resident memory (bytes) of the MikoshiLang process (/proc/self/statm on Linux, psutil elsewhere); MemoryInUse[$FrontEnd] raises because there is no front end |
| `Message` | added | 34 | Message[s::t, e1, ...] prints s::t: text (StringForm of the message text with the ei) on $Messages unless switched off or quieted, appends HoldForm[s::t] to $MessageList and returns Null; the text is looked up as s::t, then General::t, then via $NewMessage, else '-- Message text not found --' |
| `MessageName` | added | 4 | s::t (parsed as MessageName[s, "t"], HoldFirst) evaluates to the message text assigned with s::t = "text" or to MikoshiLang's built-in text; otherwise it stays unevaluated |
| `Messages` | added | 4 | Messages[s] gives {HoldPattern[s::t] :> text, ...} for the messages assigned to s (sorted by tag); messages switched off are given as HoldPattern[s::t] :> $Off[text] |
| `Monitor` | added | 3 | Monitor[expr, mon] (HoldAll) evaluates and returns expr; the monitor display is a notebook feature, so in MikoshiLang's text interface nothing is shown (as in a Wolfram text-based session) |
| `Off` | added | 9 | Off[s::t, ...] switches messages off (Off[General::t] switches off every s::t that has not been switched on explicitly); Off["group"] uses $MessageGroups; Off[Assert] disables assertions; Off[s] (tracing) and Off[] are accepted and change nothing because symbol tracing is never on |
| `On` | added | 7 | On[s::t, ...] and On["group"] switch messages back on; On[Assert] enables assertions.  On[s] for symbol tracing is not supported (the evaluator has no per-symbol trace printing) and raises |
| `Pause` | added | 16 | Pause[n] waits at least n seconds (n a non-negative real number or a Quantity of time) and returns Null |
| `Print` | added | 5 | Print[e1, e2, ...] writes the ei concatenated (OutputForm: strings without quotes) and a newline to $Output (stdout) and returns Null |
| `PrintTemporary` | added | 3 | text interface: PrintTemporary[e1, ...] prints like Print (there are no notebook cells to delete afterwards) and returns Null |
| `PropagateAborts` | added | 5 | option name for CheckAbort (Automatic, True or False); used as a function it stays unevaluated |
| `Quiet` | added | 20 | Quiet[expr] / Quiet[expr, moff] / Quiet[expr, moff, mon] (HoldAll): messages in moff are not printed, those in mon are; the innermost Quiet specification for a message wins; Check outside is unaffected. A raised MikoshiLang error is not a message and still propagates |
| `QuietEcho` | added | 3 | QuietEcho[expr] (HoldFirst) evaluates expr without Echo, EchoLabel, EchoFunction, EchoEvaluation or EchoTiming printing anything; messages and Print are not affected |
| `RegisterExceptionType` | added | 9 | RegisterExceptionType[sym] / [sym, parent \| {parents}] (HoldFirst) registers the symbol sym as an exception type in this session and returns Success["RegisteredExceptionType", <\|"ExceptionType" -> sym, "ParentExceptionTypes" -> {...}, "OwnerContext" -> "Global`"\|>]; registering twice issues RegisterExceptionType::regd and returns $Failed |
| `RepeatedTiming` | added | 3 | RepeatedTiming[expr] / RepeatedTiming[expr, t] (HoldFirst) evaluates expr at least 4 times and for at least t seconds (default 1) and gives {trimmed mean of the timings without the lower and upper quartiles, result of the first evaluation} |
| `SameTest` | added | 11 | option of TestCreate / VerificationTest / IntermediateTest / TestReport giving the function that compares the actual and expected outputs (default SameQ); inert as an expression |
| `SessionTime` | added | 3 | SessionTime[] gives the real seconds since the MikoshiLang (Python) process started (procfs on Linux; elsewhere since the library was loaded) |
| `Success` | added | 5 | Success["tag", assoc] is inert; success["prop"] gives assoc values, "Message", "Tag" and "Properties" (the association keys in order) |
| `TerminatedEvaluation` | added | 4 | TerminatedEvaluation["reason"] is the inert representation of an evaluation stopped by a session limit ("IterationLimit", "RecursionLimit", ...); a non-string reason raises |
| `TestCreate` | added | 46 | TestCreate[input] / [input, expected] / [input, expected, messages] (HoldAllComplete) creates an unevaluated TestObject; options MemoryConstraint, MetaInformation, SameTest, TestID (Automatic = hash of the test), TimeConstraint |
| `TestEvaluate` | added | 8 | TestEvaluate[test] runs a TestObject (messages are recorded, not printed) and gives the evaluated TestObject with "ActualOutput", "ActualMessages", timings and the "Outcome" / "FailureType"; TestEvaluate[<\|"Input" :> ..., "ExpectedOutput" -> ..., ...\|>] builds the test from an association |
| `TestEvaluationFunction` | added | 4 | option of TestReport: the function used to evaluate each created test (default TestEvaluate); inert as an expression |
| `TestID` | added | 8 | option of TestCreate / VerificationTest / IntermediateTest: the test's identifier (Automatic = a hash of the test); inert as an expression |
| `TestObject` | added | 4 | TestObject[<\|...\|>] is the inert test result (from TestCreate / TestEvaluate / VerificationTest); test["prop"] for "Outcome" (Success, Failure, MessagesFailure, NotEvaluated), "FailureType", "Input", "ExpectedOutput", "ActualOutput", "ActualMessages", "TestID", "TestKey", "TestCreate", "Created", timings, ...; test[{p1, ...}] gives an association; "Properties" |
| `TestReport` | added | 30 | TestReport[{tests}] / TestReport[test] evaluates the NotEvaluated TestObjects and gives a TestReportObject; TestReport[{reports}] merges reports; TestReport["file.wl"] evaluates a plain-text test file with $TestFileName set (notebooks raise). Options HandlerFunctions (events ReportStarted, TestCreated, TestEvaluated, RuntimeFailure, FileStarted, FileCompleted, ReportCompleted, UnhandledEvent), HandlerFunctionsKeys, TestEvaluationFunction, SameTest, TimeConstraint, MemoryConstraint |
| `TestReportObject` | added | 4 | TestReportObject[<\|...\|>] is the inert result of TestReport; report["prop"] for "Results", "ResultsByOutcome", "ResultsByTestID", "ResultsByTestFileName", "ResultsDataset", "ReportSucceeded", "RuntimeFailures", "Title", timings, "TestsSucceededCount", the key lists ... |
| `ThrowException` | added | 9 | ThrowException[args] throws Exception[args] (to CatchExceptions; Catch[..., _] also intercepts it); malformed arguments throw an "ErrorHandlingException" exception after an Exception:: message; uncaught exceptions reach the top level as an Uncaught Throw error |
| `TimeConstrained` | added | 11 | TimeConstrained[expr, t] / TimeConstrained[expr, t, failexpr] (HoldAll) evaluates expr, aborting it after t seconds of real time and returning $Aborted (or failexpr, evaluated only then); t may be Infinity or a Quantity in seconds; AbortProtect postpones the abort |
| `TimeConstraint` | added | 6 | option of the test functions: seconds a test may run (default Infinity; exceeding it gives TimeConstraintFailure); inert as an expression |
| `TimeRemaining` | added | 3 | TimeRemaining[] gives the seconds (real time) left before the earliest enclosing TimeConstrained aborts, or Infinity outside TimeConstrained |
| `TimeUsed` | added | 4 | TimeUsed[] gives the CPU seconds used so far by the MikoshiLang (Python) process (time.process_time); Pause is not counted |
| `Timing` | added | 4 | Timing[expr] (HoldAll) gives {CPU seconds used by this process while evaluating expr, result} (time.process_time); time spent in Pause is not counted |
| `VerificationTest` | added | 27 | VerificationTest[input, expected, messages, opts] (HoldAllComplete) = TestEvaluate[TestCreate[...]]: evaluates the test immediately without printing messages and gives the evaluated TestObject |
| `WithCleanup` | added | 5 | WithCleanup[expr, cleanup] / WithCleanup[init, expr, cleanup] (HoldAll) returns expr's value and always evaluates cleanup, also after an abort, Throw, Confirm or error in expr; aborts during init and cleanup are postponed until cleanup is done (an abort in init skips expr) |

### ml

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DecisionTreeTrain` | verified | 4 | -> {accuracy (training set), depth}; scikit-learn DecisionTreeClassifier(max_depth, random_state=42) |
| `KMeans` | verified | 5 | KMeans[data, k, maxIter=100] -> {labels -> ..., centers -> ...} (scikit-learn, random_state=42, n_init=10); k must not exceed the number of points |
| `LogisticRegressionTrain` | verified | 5 | -> {coefficients, intercept, accuracy (training set)}; scikit-learn LogisticRegression (L2, C=1); binary labels only |
| `PCA` | verified | 4 | PCA[data, n=2] -> {transformed -> ..., explainedVariance -> ratios} (scikit-learn; component signs are arbitrary) |

### number theory

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Ackermann` | fixed | 4 | exact closed forms for m <= 3; larger m raise instead of recursing until RecursionError |
| `BaseConvert` | fixed | 4 | BaseConvert[n, from, to] reads the decimal digits of n as base-'from' digits; legacy accepted invalid digits (BaseConvert[19, 2, 10] = 3); now raises. Returns an integer for to = 10, else a digit list |
| `Bell` | fixed | 3 | alias of BellB; legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |
| `BellB` | promoted | 3 | BellB[n] and Bell polynomials BellB[n, x] |
| `BellNumber` | fixed | 3 | alias of BellB; legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |
| `Bernoulli` | fixed | 3 | Bernoulli[1] returned +1/2 (SymPy >= 1.12 convention); Wolfram BernoulliB[1] = -1/2 |
| `BernoulliB` | promoted | 3 | BernoulliB[n] and BernoulliB[n, x] |
| `BernoulliNumber` | fixed | 3 | Bernoulli[1] returned +1/2 (SymPy >= 1.12 convention); Wolfram BernoulliB[1] = -1/2 |
| `CarmichaelLambda` | fixed | 4 | CarmichaelLambda[-n] = CarmichaelLambda[n]; 0 raises; legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |
| `Catalan` | fixed | 5 | Wolfram constant Catalan = 0.915965594177219... (SymPy Catalan; N[Catalan, n] to any precision, Sum[(-1)^k/(2k+1)^2, {k, 0, Infinity}] -> Catalan); Catalan[...] used as a function raises ValueError pointing to CatalanNumber; legacy Catalan[n] gave Catalan numbers |
| `CatalanNumber` | fixed | 6 | exact for any exact argument, symbolic n stays unevaluated; legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |
| `ChineseRemainder` | fixed | 3 | smallest non-negative solution; non-coprime moduli handled; no solution leaves the call unevaluated (legacy returned an Error expression) |
| `CollatzLength` | fixed | 4 | number of terms of CollatzSequence[n] (including n and 1); legacy looped forever for n <= 0 |
| `CollatzSequence` | fixed | 3 | legacy looped forever for n <= 0; now n must be a positive integer |
| `ContinuedFractionConvergents` | fixed | 3 | convergents p/q of [a0; a1, ...] as {p, q} pairs (MikoshiLang); legacy raised for integer convergents |
| `DigitCount` | fixed | 3 | legacy returned the number of digits; Wolfram DigitCount[n] is the list of counts of digits 1, 2, ..., 9, 0; DigitCount[n, b] and DigitCount[n, b, d] also supported |
| `DigitSum` | fixed | 3 | DigitSum[n] and DigitSum[n, b] (sum of base-b digits of \|n\|); legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |
| `DiophantineSolve` | fixed | 3 | legacy called sympy.diophantine.diophantine (AttributeError for every input); now the general integer solution via SymPy diophantine, parameters t0, t1, ... in sorted order |
| `DivisorCount` | fixed | 3 | number of divisors; legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |
| `DivisorSigma` | fixed | 3 | DivisorSigma[k, n] = sum of d^k over divisors of n; exact for any exact k (legacy truncated k) |
| `Divisors` | fixed | 4 | Divisors[-n] = Divisors[n]; 0 raises; legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |
| `EulerE` | promoted | 3 | EulerE[n] and EulerE[n, x] |
| `EulerNumber` | verified | 3 | Euler numbers E_n (E_1 = 0, E_4 = 5) |
| `EulerPhi` | fixed | 4 | EulerPhi[0] = 0 and EulerPhi[-n] = EulerPhi[n] (legacy raised / gave wrong values) |
| `ExtendedGCD` | fixed | 3 | returned {x, y, g}; Wolfram order is {g, {x, y}} with g = a x + b y |
| `FactorInteger` | fixed | 4 | FactorInteger[1] returned {} (Wolfram {{1,1}}), 0 and rationals failed; now {{-1,1},...} for negatives, negative exponents for rationals |
| `Fibonacci` | fixed | 7 | symbolic n raised TypeError (now unevaluated); negative n, real n and Fibonacci[n, x] added |
| `FibonacciNumber` | fixed | 3 | MikoshiLang alias of Fibonacci |
| `FromDigits` | fixed | 9 | now accepts digit strings (letters a-z = 10-35), symbolic digits and digits >= base as Wolfram; legacy silently used base 10 for invalid bases |
| `GCD` | fixed | 4 | now variadic, exact for rationals, GCD[] = 0; legacy truncated reals (GCD[2.5, 5] = 1) |
| `HarmonicNumber` | fixed | 10 | HarmonicNumber[n] for rational n in closed form (HarmonicNumber[1/2] = 2 - 2 Log[2], legacy left it unevaluated); HarmonicNumber[n, r] |
| `IntegerDigits` | fixed | 5 | legacy silently used base 10 for a non-integer base; now IntegerDigits[n], [n, b], [n, b, len] (digits of \|n\|, padded/truncated on the left) |
| `IsPrime` | verified | 4 | MikoshiLang alias of PrimeQ (same kernel implementation) |
| `JacobiSymbol` | fixed | 4 | m must be a positive odd integer (clear error otherwise); legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |
| `JordanTotient` | fixed | 4 | legacy called a SymPy function that does not exist (AttributeError); now J_k(n) = n^k prod(1 - p^-k), JordanTotient[n] = EulerPhi[n] |
| `KroneckerSymbol` | fixed | 4 | legacy called a SymPy function that does not exist (AttributeError); now the full Kronecker symbol (n = 0, even and negative n) |
| `LCM` | fixed | 3 | now variadic, exact for rationals, LCM[] = 1; legacy truncated reals |
| `LegendreSymbol` | fixed | 4 | p must be an odd prime (clear error otherwise); legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |
| `Lucas` | fixed | 3 | MikoshiLang alias of LucasL; negative and real n added; legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |
| `LucasL` | fixed | 8 | LucasL[n, x] Lucas polynomials L_0 = 2, L_1 = x, L_n = x L_(n-1) + L_(n-2) (LucasL[3, x] = x^3 + 3 x); the 2-argument form was missing |
| `ModularInverse` | fixed | 3 | raises a clear error when no inverse exists (legacy leaked a Python ValueError) |
| `MoebiusMu` | fixed | 4 | MoebiusMu[-n] = MoebiusMu[n]; 0 raises |
| `MultiplicativeOrder` | fixed | 7 | raises a clear error when gcd(k, n) != 1; legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |
| `NextPrime` | fixed | 4 | legacy truncated reals and ignored negative primes; now NextPrime[x] is the smallest prime > x (primes include -2, -3, ... as in Wolfram) and NextPrime[x, k] the k-th |
| `PowerMod` | fixed | 9 | negative exponents use the modular inverse and raise when it does not exist; rational exponents raise |
| `Prime` | fixed | 4 | Prime[n] for positive integers only; legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |
| `PrimeFactorization` | fixed | 3 | MikoshiLang alias of FactorInteger |
| `PrimePi` | fixed | 4 | PrimePi[x] for real x counts primes <= x (legacy truncated with int(), wrong for negative floats) |
| `PrimePowerQ` | fixed | 4 | True for p^k with k >= 1; False for 0, 1 and non-integers; negative integers raise (Gaussian/negative conventions not supported); legacy truncated reals |
| `PrimitiveRoot` | fixed | 6 | smallest primitive root; legacy returned None (displayed as nothing) when none exists, now unevaluated like Wolfram |
| `PrimitiveRootQ` | fixed | 3 | PrimitiveRootQ[a, n]: True when a generates the units mod n; False when gcd(a, n) != 1 (legacy raised a SymPy ValueError) |
| `QuadraticResidues` | verified | 3 | sorted residues x^2 mod n including 0 (MikoshiLang function); n must be positive |
| `Radical` | fixed | 3 | product of the distinct primes dividing n; Radical[0] raises; legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |
| `RationalReconstruct` | fixed | 3 | legacy used nsimplify, so RationalReconstruct[1.4142135623730951] returned Sqrt[2] (not a rational); now the exact rational with the same decimal digits, simplest within machine precision |
| `Stirling` | fixed | 3 | Stirling[n, k] = StirlingS2[n, k]; Stirling[n, k, 1] now gives the signed numbers like StirlingS1 |
| `StirlingS1` | fixed | 4 | returned unsigned Stirling numbers (StirlingS1[3, 2] = 3); Wolfram uses signed numbers (-3) |
| `StirlingS2` | fixed | 3 | Stirling numbers of the second kind; legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |

### number theory (batch 2)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DivisorSum` | added | 5 | DivisorSum[n, form] = Sum of form[d] over the positive divisors d of n; DivisorSum[n, form, cond] keeps the divisors for which cond[d] is True |
| `LiouvilleLambda` | added | 5 | Liouville function (-1)^Omega(n), Omega counting prime factors with multiplicity; LiouvilleLambda[1] = 1 |
| `MangoldtLambda` | added | 5 | von Mangoldt function: Log[p] if n is a power of the prime p, else 0; MangoldtLambda[1] = 0 |
| `RandomPrime` | added | 6 | RandomPrime[n] is a uniformly random prime in [2, n]; RandomPrime[{a, b}]; RandomPrime[range, k] a list of k primes (independent draws); raises when the range contains no prime |

### numerics

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Bisection` | fixed | 5 | Bisection[f, x, a, b, tol=1e-6] root of f in [a, b]; raises unless f(a), f(b) bracket a root (legacy returned an Error[...] expression) |
| `EulerMethod` | verified | 4 | EulerMethod[f, {x, y}, x0, y0, xEnd, n=100] for y' = f(x, y): y(xEnd) with n fixed steps |
| `GaussQuadrature` | fixed | 5 | n-point Gauss-Legendre rule (default n = 5); legacy ignored n and used adaptive quadrature |
| `MonteCarloIntegration` | verified | 4 | plain Monte Carlo estimate with n uniform samples (default 10000); random, reproducible after SeedRandom[s]; standard error ~ (b-a)·σ/√n |
| `NewtonRaphson` | fixed | 5 | NewtonRaphson[f, x, x0, tol=1e-10, maxIter=100] with the exact derivative; raises on zero derivative or non-convergence (legacy returned Error[...] expressions) |
| `RungeKutta4` | verified | 5 | RungeKutta4[f, {x, y}, x0, y0, xEnd, n=100] for y' = f(x, y): y(xEnd) with n fixed steps |
| `SecantMethod` | fixed | 4 | SecantMethod[f, x, x0, x1, tol=1e-10, maxIter=100]; raises on failure (legacy returned Error[...]) |
| `SimpsonsRule` | fixed | 4 | composite Simpson rule; n (default 100) must be even (legacy silently used n+1) |
| `TrapezoidalRule` | verified | 4 | composite trapezoid rule with n panels (default 100) |

### numerics (batch 2)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `$MachineEpsilon` | added | 4 | 2^-52 = 2.22045*10^-16 |
| `$MachinePrecision` | added | 3 | 53 Log10[2] = 15.9546 |
| `$MaxMachineNumber` | added | 3 | 1.79769*10^308 |
| `$MinMachineNumber` | added | 3 | 2.22507*10^-308 |
| `AbsArg` | added | 3 | {Abs[z], Arg[z]} |
| `Accuracy` | added | 6 | Accuracy[x] = Precision[x] - Log10[Abs[x]] (digits after the decimal point); Infinity for exact numbers; machine zero has Accuracy -Log10[$MinMachineNumber] = 307.653; the minimum over an expression |
| `Arg` | added | 6 | argument in (-Pi, Pi]; Arg[0] = 0 (Wolfram); Arg[-1] = Pi; exact for exact input |
| `Chop` | added | 13 | Chop[expr, delta] replaces approximate real numbers (and real or imaginary parts) with absolute value below delta (default 10^-10) by exact 0; exact numbers are untouched |
| `Clip` | added | 9 | Clip[x] clips to [-1, 1]; Clip[x, {min, max}]; Clip[x, {min, max}, {vmin, vmax}] gives vmin below and vmax above; threads over lists; symbolic x stays unevaluated |
| `Denominator` | added | 6 | denominator of a rational number or of the factors with negative exponents (Denominator[x/y^2] = y^2); 1 for sums and approximate numbers |
| `ExactNumberQ` | added | 5 | True for integers, rationals and exact complex numbers; False for approximate numbers and for numeric expressions such as Pi or Sqrt[2] (which are not numbers) |
| `FindFit` | added | 3 | FindFit[data, expr, pars, vars] nonlinear least squares -> {a -> a*, ...}; pars may be {{a, a0}, ...} with starting values (default 1); constraints FindFit[data, {expr, cons}, ...] are not supported |
| `FindMaximum` | added | 3 | FindMaximum[f, {x, x0}] / {{x, x0}, {y, y0}} -> {fmax, {x -> xmax, ...}}: FindMinimum of -f (local, BFGS); non-convergence raises |
| `FindRoot` | added | 6 | FindRoot[f, {x, x0}] / [lhs == rhs, {x, x0}] / {x, x0, x1} (secant) / {x, x0, xmin, xmax} (bounded) and systems FindRoot[{eqs}, {{x, x0}, {y, y0}}] -> {x -> root, ...}; complex starting values search in the complex plane; WorkingPrecision -> p; raises when no root is found |
| `Fit` | added | 5 | Fit[data, {f1, f2, ...}, vars] linear least-squares fit -> c1 f1 + c2 f2 + ... (data {y1, y2, ...} at x = 1, 2, ... or {{x, y}, ...} or {{x1, x2, y}, ...}); machine-precision coefficients |
| `FractionalPart` | added | 9 | FractionalPart[x] = x - IntegerPart[x] (same sign as x: FractionalPart[-2.4] = -0.4), separately for real and imaginary parts; exact for exact input |
| `Im` | added | 4 | imaginary part (Wolfram name; RealPart/ImaginaryPart are the legacy MikoshiLang names); exact for exact input, 0 (exact) for the imaginary part of a real |
| `InexactNumberQ` | added | 3 | True for approximate (machine or arbitrary-precision) real and complex numbers |
| `IntegerPart` | added | 8 | integer part (truncation toward zero) as an exact integer, separately for real and imaginary parts; IntegerPart[Infinity] = Infinity |
| `InterpolatingFunction` | added | 4 | InterpolatingFunction[{{xmin, xmax}}, id] objects from Interpolation / ListInterpolation / NDSolve: ifun[x], ifun'[x], Derivative[n][ifun][x], lists of points; real arguments; outside the domain the end polynomials extrapolate |
| `Interpolation` | added | 15 | Interpolation[{f1, f2, ...}] (x = 1, 2, ...) and Interpolation[{{x1, f1}, ...}] -> InterpolatingFunction: piecewise polynomials of InterpolationOrder (default 3) through the nearest order+1 points (Wolfram's default method; exact through the data); Interpolation[data, x] evaluates at x; one-dimensional data only (derivative data and grids raise) |
| `ListInterpolation` | added | 5 | ListInterpolation[{f1, ..., fn}] on x = 1..n and ListInterpolation[list, {{xmin, xmax}}] on an equally spaced grid -> InterpolatingFunction (same piecewise polynomials as Interpolation); 1-D only |
| `MachineNumberQ` | added | 7 | True for machine-precision real and complex numbers only |
| `MachinePrecision` | added | 6 | symbol for machine precision; N[MachinePrecision] = 15.9546 (53 Log10[2]) |
| `NDSolve` | added | 7 | NDSolve[{odes, ics}, y, {x, xmin, xmax}] (also y[x], and systems {y, z}) for initial-value problems of any order that can be solved for the highest derivatives -> {{y -> InterpolatingFunction[...]}} (or {{y[x] -> InterpolatingFunction[...][x]}}); SciPy DOP853 (Radau fallback for stiff problems), rtol 1e-10; the initial point may be inside the range; machine precision only; boundary-value problems, DAEs, PDEs and delay equations raise |
| `NDSolveValue` | added | 6 | NDSolveValue[{odes, ics}, y, {x, xmin, xmax}] -> InterpolatingFunction (y[x] -> ifun[x], {y, z} -> list); same solver and limits as NDSolve |
| `NIntegrate` | added | 13 | numerical integration NIntegrate[f, {x, a, b}, {y, c, d}, ...] (later limits may depend on earlier variables, Wolfram order), infinite ranges, intermediate points {x, a, x1, ..., b} for interior singularities, complex-valued integrands and complex contours; WorkingPrecision -> p for arbitrary precision (mpmath tanh-sinh / Gauss-Legendre: Method -> "DoubleExponential" \| "GaussKronrod") |
| `NMaximize` | added | 3 | NMaximize[f, vars] / [{f, cons}, vars] -> {fmax, {x -> x*, ...}} (NMinimize of -f) |
| `NMinimize` | added | 5 | NMinimize[f, vars] / NMinimize[{f, cons}, vars] -> {fmin, {x -> x*, ...}}: global numeric search (differential evolution when every variable is bounded, plus 40 seeded multi-start SLSQP/BFGS local searches; constraints: <, <=, >, >=, ==, Inequality, And); like Wolfram's it is a heuristic global method; integer domains raise |
| `NProduct` | added | 4 | NProduct[f, {n, nmin, nmax}] (also infinite products, mpmath nprod); WorkingPrecision -> p |
| `NRoots` | added | 4 | NRoots[poly == 0, x] gives x == r1 \|\| x == r2 \|\| ... with roots repeated by multiplicity (machine precision, or the precision of approximate coefficients); non-polynomials raise |
| `NSolve` | added | 9 | NSolve[eqns, vars] numerical solutions of polynomial (and rational) equations and systems as {{x -> x1}, ...} with every complex root repeated by multiplicity; NSolve[eqns, vars, Reals] keeps real solutions; WorkingPrecision -> p; transcendental equations raise (use FindRoot) |
| `NSum` | added | 7 | NSum[f, {n, nmin, nmax}] and {n, nmin, nmax, dn}, multiple iterators, nmax = Infinity (mpmath nsum: Richardson/Shanks/Euler-Maclaurin acceleration, alternating series); WorkingPrecision -> p; finite short ranges are summed directly; divergent sums raise |
| `Numerator` | added | 5 | numerator of a rational number or of a product with negative exponents (Numerator[x^2 y^-3] = x^2); sums are not combined over a common denominator (Numerator[a/b + c/d] is the whole sum) |
| `Precision` | added | 18 | Precision[x]: Infinity for exact numbers and symbolic expressions without approximate numbers, MachinePrecision for machine numbers, the decimal precision of a bignum; the minimum over an expression (a machine number makes the whole expression MachinePrecision). An arbitrary-precision zero has Precision 0. |
| `Rationalize` | added | 16 | Rationalize[x] gives the first continued-fraction convergent p/q with \|p/q - x\| < 10^-4/q^2, or x unchanged when there is none (Rationalize[N[Pi]] = 3.14159); Rationalize[x, dx] gives the first convergent within dx, refined to the smallest semiconvergent within dx (Rationalize[Pi, 1/1000] = 201/64); Rationalize[x, 0] gives the simplest rational within the precision of x; applies to every approximate number of an expression |
| `Re` | added | 5 | real part (Wolfram name; RealPart/ImaginaryPart are the legacy MikoshiLang names); exact for exact input, 0 (exact) for the imaginary part of a real |
| `ReIm` | added | 3 | {Re[z], Im[z]} |
| `RealAbs` | added | 5 | absolute value for real numbers; stays unevaluated for symbolic and non-real arguments |
| `RealDigits` | added | 23 | RealDigits[x, b, len, n]: {digits, exponent}. Machine numbers give their full precision (16 decimal digits, 53 binary), digits beyond the precision of an approximate number are Indeterminate; exact rationals give the exact digits with the recurring block as a nested list (RealDigits[19/7] = {{2, {7, 1, 4, 2, 8, 5}}, 1}); exact irrationals need len. Digits are of Abs[x]; an approximate number's digits are those of its exact binary value, rounded at its precision |
| `Rescale` | added | 6 | Rescale[x, {min, max}] = (x - min)/(max - min); Rescale[x, {min, max}, {ymin, ymax}]; Rescale[list] uses Min[list] and Max[list]; works symbolically |
| `SetAccuracy` | added | 3 | SetAccuracy[expr, a]: each number gets precision a + Log10[Abs[x]] (an arbitrary-precision zero keeps accuracy a) |
| `SetPrecision` | added | 10 | SetPrecision[expr, p] sets every number in expr (and the numeric constants Pi, E, ...) to precision p: machine numbers keep their exact binary value padded with binary zeros (SetPrecision[0.1, 20] = 0.10000000000000000555), p = Infinity converts approximate numbers to the exact rationals they represent, p = MachinePrecision gives machine numbers; the result is re-evaluated |
| `Unitize` | added | 4 | Unitize[x] is 0 for x == 0 and 1 otherwise (exact integers, also for 0.); Unitize[x, dx] is 0 when Abs[x] < dx; threads over lists |

### physics

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Acceleration` | fixed | 3 | velocity change/time, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `BeatFrequency` | fixed | 3 | \|f1 - f2\|, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `BraggLaw` | fixed | 3 | BraggLaw[d, theta] and [d, theta, n] give the wavelength 2 d Sin[theta]/n (theta in degrees); BraggLaw[d, theta, n, lambda] gives the Bragg angle in degrees for lambda (theta ignored); impossible angles raise; exact for exact input |
| `Capacitance` | fixed | 3 | Q/V, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `CapacitorEnergy` | fixed | 3 | C V^2/2, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `CarnotEfficiency` | fixed | 4 | CarnotEfficiency[Th, Tc] = 1 - Tc/Th (kelvin), exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats); Th <= 0, Tc < 0 or Tc > Th raise (legacy returned negative efficiencies) |
| `CentripetalForce` | fixed | 3 | m v^2/r, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |

### special functions (batch 2)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `AiryAiPrime` | added | 3 | derivative of AiryAi; AiryAiPrime[0] = -1/(3^(1/3) Gamma[1/3]) |
| `AiryBiPrime` | added | 3 | derivative of AiryBi; AiryBiPrime[0] = 3^(1/6)/Gamma[1/3] |
| `BarnesG` | added | 6 | Barnes G-function, G(z + 1) = Gamma(z) G(z); BarnesG[n] = Prod[k!, {k, 0, n - 2}] for positive integers, 0 at non-positive integers; mpmath barnesg |
| `BetaRegularized` | added | 5 | BetaRegularized[z, a, b] = Beta[z, a, b]/Beta[a, b]; exact for positive integer a, b |
| `CoshIntegral` | added | 4 | Chi(z) = EulerGamma + Log[z] + Integral[(Cosh[t] - 1)/t, {t, 0, z}], branch cut along the negative real axis (CoshIntegral[-1.] = 0.837867 + 3.14159 I); CoshIntegral[0] = -Infinity |
| `ExpIntegralE` | fixed | 8 | ExpIntegralE[n, 0] = 1/(n - 1) for n > 1 and ComplexInfinity for n <= 1 (legacy left the integer cases unevaluated); numeric values at any precision via mpmath |
| `FactorialPower` | added | 5 | falling factorial x (x - 1) ... (x - n + 1) = Gamma[x + 1]/Gamma[x - n + 1]; FactorialPower[x, n, h] = x (x - h) ... (x - (n - 1) h); stays unevaluated for symbolic x |
| `FresnelC` | added | 4 | FresnelC[z] = Integral[Cos[Pi t^2/2], {t, 0, z}]; FresnelC[Infinity] = 1/2 |
| `FresnelS` | added | 4 | FresnelS[z] = Integral[Sin[Pi t^2/2], {t, 0, z}]; FresnelS[Infinity] = 1/2 |
| `GammaRegularized` | added | 6 | GammaRegularized[a, z] = Gamma[a, z]/Gamma[a] (regularized upper incomplete gamma Q); exact for positive integer a; mpmath gammainc(regularized=True) |
| `HurwitzLerchPhi` | added | 3 | HurwitzLerchPhi[z, s, a] = Sum[z^k/(k + a)^s, {k, 0, Infinity}] (principal powers) |
| `Hypergeometric0F1Regularized` | added | 3 | regularized 0F1: Hypergeometric0F1/Gamma[b] (finite for non-positive integer b, e.g. via the limit formula z^(m+1) (a)_(m+1)/(m+1)! 0F1(a + m + 1; m + 2; z) for b = -m) |
| `Hypergeometric1F1Regularized` | added | 3 | regularized 1F1: Hypergeometric1F1/Gamma[b] (finite for non-positive integer b, e.g. via the limit formula z^(m+1) (a)_(m+1)/(m+1)! 1F1(a + m + 1; m + 2; z) for b = -m) |
| `Hypergeometric2F1Regularized` | added | 3 | regularized 2F1: Hypergeometric2F1/Gamma[b] (finite for non-positive integer b, e.g. via the limit formula z^(m+1) (a)_(m+1)/(m+1)! 2F1(a + m + 1; m + 2; z) for b = -m) |
| `HypergeometricPFQ` | added | 5 | generalized hypergeometric function pFq(a1, ..., ap; b1, ..., bq; z); exact closed forms via SymPy hyperexpand; p = q + 1 has the branch cut [1, Infinity) |
| `HypergeometricPFQRegularized` | added | 3 | regularized generalized hypergeometric function pFq(a1, ..., ap; b1, ..., bq; z)/Prod[Gamma[bj]] (finite for non-positive integer bj) |
| `HypergeometricU` | added | 4 | Tricomi confluent hypergeometric function U(a, b, z) (branch cut along the negative real axis); HypergeometricU[0, b, z] = 1 |
| `InverseErf` | added | 7 | inverse of Erf on [-1, 1]: InverseErf[0] = 0, InverseErf[1] = Infinity; complex and \|x\| > 1 arguments raise (not supported) |
| `InverseErfc` | added | 6 | inverse of Erfc on [0, 2] (Newton refinement keeps full relative precision for tiny arguments); complex arguments raise (not supported) |

| `MeijerG` | added | 3 | MeijerG[{{a1, ..., an}, {an+1, ..., ap}}, {{b1, ..., bm}, {bm+1, ..., bq}}, z] (and the form with a final argument r) evaluated with mpmath meijerg for approximate arguments; exact arguments stay unevaluated (FunctionExpand is not implemented) |
| `Pochhammer` | added | 6 | rising factorial (a)_n = Gamma[a + n]/Gamma[a]; exact for integer n (including negative n: Pochhammer[a, -n] = 1/((a - 1)...(a - n))); stays unevaluated for symbolic a (Wolfram) |
| `PolyLog` | added | 11 | PolyLog[n, z] = Sum[z^k/k^n, {k, 1, Infinity}] continued analytically, branch cut [1, Infinity) (PolyLog[2, 2.] = 2.4674 - 2.17759 I); PolyLog[1, z] = -Log[1 - z], PolyLog[n, z] for integer n <= 0 is rational, PolyLog[s, 1] = Zeta[s], PolyLog[2, 1/2] = Pi^2/12 - Log[2]^2/2. The Nielsen form PolyLog[n, p, z] is not supported |
| `SinhIntegral` | added | 4 | Shi(z) = Integral[Sinh[t]/t, {t, 0, z}]; mpmath shi |

| `SphericalBesselY` | added | 3 | y_n(z) = Sqrt[Pi/(2 z)] BesselY[n + 1/2, z]; SphericalBesselY[n, 0] = ComplexInfinity |
| `SphericalHankelH1` | added | 3 | h1_n(z) = SphericalBesselJ[n, z] + I SphericalBesselY[n, z] |
| `SphericalHankelH2` | added | 3 | h2_n(z) = SphericalBesselJ[n, z] - I SphericalBesselY[n, z] |
| `StieltjesGamma` | added | 5 | Stieltjes constants gamma_n (Laurent coefficients of Zeta at s = 1); StieltjesGamma[0] = EulerGamma |
| `WeierstrassP` | added | 4 | WeierstrassP[u, {g2, g3}] via the Jacobi form e3 + (e1 - e3)/sn^2(Sqrt[e1 - e3] u \| m), m = (e2 - e3)/(e1 - e3), with e_i the roots of 4 t^3 - g2 t - g3 (valid for any ordering of the roots); approximate arguments only; degenerate invariants raise |
| `WeierstrassPPrime` | added | 4 | derivative of WeierstrassP[u, {g2, g3}] with respect to u |
| `Zeta` | fixed | 8 | Zeta[s, a] = Sum[((k + a)^2)^(-s/2), {k, 0, Infinity}] (terms with k + a = 0 excluded); this differs from HurwitzZeta[s, a] = Sum[(k + a)^-s] only for Re(a) < 0 (legacy returned the Hurwitz value there: Zeta[3, -1.5] = 0.118 instead of 16.71); exact values from SymPy (Zeta[2, 1/2] = Pi^2/2) |

### statistics

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ANOVA` | fixed | 3 | one-way ANOVA {F, p_value} for 2 or more groups (SciPy), unrounded |
| `AndersonDarling` | fixed | 3 | {statistic, criticalValues, significanceLevels} for normality with estimated mean and sd (Stephens critical values with the 1 + 0.75/n + 2.25/n^2 correction, identical to SciPy anderson); computed directly, no SciPy needed (legacy relied on an API SciPy is removing) |
| `BinomialDistribution` | verified | 12 | inert distribution object [n, p] |
| `CDF` | fixed | 76 | batch 5 distributions: exact CDFs (regularised gamma/beta forms; discrete CDFs at Floor[x]); exact CDFs (normal via Erfc, chi-squared via incomplete gamma, t and F via regularised incomplete beta); legacy rounded to 10 digits and truncated discrete arguments |
| `ChiSquaredDistribution` | verified | 3 | inert distribution object (nu degrees of freedom) |
| `ChiSquaredTest` | fixed | 3 | {chi2 -> statistic, p_value -> p} for observed vs expected counts (SciPy chisquare), unrounded |
| `CoefficientOfVariation` | fixed | 3 | StandardDeviation/Mean, exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits) |
| `CohensD` | fixed | 3 | (mean1 - mean2)/pooled SD, exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits) |
| `Commonest` | promoted | 3 | Commonest[list]: the most frequent elements in order of first appearance; Commonest[list, n]: the n most frequent, by decreasing frequency (ties by first appearance) |
| `ConfidenceInterval` | fixed | 3 | t-based interval for the mean at the given level; legacy silently used 0.95 when the level was not a machine number (e.g. 9/10); now any level in (0, 1) and no rounding |
| `Correlation` | fixed | 5 | Correlation[matrix] = correlation matrix of the columns, exact; Pearson correlation, exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits) |
| `Covariance` | fixed | 8 | Covariance[dist] = covariance matrix of a multivariate distribution; Covariance[matrix] = covariance matrix of the columns (unbiased), exact; unbiased sample covariance, exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits) |
| `EffectSize` | fixed | 3 | alias of CohensD, exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits) |
| `ExponentialDistribution` | verified | 34 | inert distribution object (rate lambda) |
| `FDistribution` | verified | 5 | inert F-ratio distribution [n, m] (Wolfram FRatioDistribution) |
| `GeometricMean` | fixed | 3 | matrix data: the statistic of each column (Wolfram); (x1 ... xn)^(1/n), exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits); requires positive data |
| `HarmonicMean` | fixed | 3 | matrix data: the statistic of each column (Wolfram); n / Sum[1/x], exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits); zero values raise |
| `IQR` | fixed | 3 | Wolfram InterquartileRange: Q3 - Q1 with Quantile parameters {{1/2, 0}, {0, 1}}; legacy used NumPy's linear percentiles (a different estimator) |
| `InverseCDF` | fixed | 51 | batch 5 distributions: closed-form quantiles where they exist, smallest k with CDF >= p for discrete distributions, SciPy for machine-number p otherwise (exact p without a closed form stays unevaluated); exact for normal, exponential and uniform (InverseCDF[NormalDistribution[0, 1], 1/2] = 0); numeric (mpmath root of the CDF) for t, chi-squared and F with machine-number p; legacy rounded to 10 digits and returned an Error expression for other distributions |
| `KendallCorrelation` | verified | 3 | {tau -> Kendall tau-b, p -> p-value} (SciPy) |
| `KruskalWallis` | fixed | 3 | {H, p_value} for 2 or more groups (SciPy), unrounded |
| `Kurtosis` | fixed | 34 | matrix data: the statistic of each column (Wolfram); Kurtosis[dist] for every supported distribution (Indeterminate when undefined; Kurtosis is the non-excess kurtosis, 3 for the normal distribution); m4/m2^2 (Wolfram; normal data -> 3); legacy returned SciPy's excess (Fisher) kurtosis, i.e. 3 too small, rounded to 10 digits |
| `LinearRegression` | fixed | 4 | {slope, intercept, r_squared} exact least squares plus p_value and std_error of the slope (t-test, as scipy.stats.linregress; no SciPy needed); legacy rounded |
| `MAD` | fixed | 3 | median absolute deviation Median[Abs[x - Median[x]]] (Wolfram MedianDeviation), exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits) |
| `MannWhitneyU` | fixed | 3 | {U -> statistic, p_value -> two-sided p} (SciPy), unrounded |
| `Mean` | fixed | 63 | matrix data: the statistic of each column (Wolfram); Mean[dist] for the batch 5 distributions (Indeterminate when the moment does not exist); exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits); Mean[dist] gives the distribution mean |
| `Median` | fixed | 17 | matrix data: the statistic of each column (Wolfram); Median[dist] for every supported distribution (Indeterminate when undefined; Kurtosis is the non-excess kurtosis, 3 for the normal distribution); exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits); even length averages the two middle values |
| `Mode` | fixed | 3 | the unique most frequent value (MikoshiLang); multimodal data now raises (legacy returned the first mode silently) - use Commonest |
| `NormalDistribution` | verified | 39 | inert distribution object NormalDistribution[mu, sigma] (NormalDistribution[] = [0, 1]) |
| `PDF` | fixed | 79 | batch 5 distributions: Wolfram densities/PMFs with Piecewise support conditions for symbolic x; exact/symbolic densities (legacy forced floats, rounded to 10 digits so PDF[NormalDistribution[0, 1], 10] was 0., and truncated Poisson/Binomial arguments with int()); PMF for discrete distributions |
| `PearsonCorrelation` | fixed | 3 | {r -> exact Pearson r, p -> two-sided p-value (SciPy)} (MikoshiLang) |
| `Percentile` | fixed | 3 | Percentile[list, p] with p in [0, 100] using linear interpolation between order statistics (NumPy/Excel convention, = Quantile[list, p/100, {{1, -1}, {0, 1}}]); now exact for exact data |
| `PoissonDistribution` | verified | 25 | inert distribution object (mean mu) |
| `Quantile` | fixed | 5 | Wolfram definition s[[Ceiling[n q]]] (q = 0 gives the minimum), lists of q and the 4-parameter form Quantile[list, q, {{a, b}, {c, d}}]; legacy used NumPy's linear interpolation (a different estimator) returning floats |
| `RandomSample` | fixed | 3 | Wolfram RandomSample[list] / [list, n]: a random sample without replacement; legacy drew numbers from a distribution (now RandomVariate) and fell back to N(0, 1) for unknown input |
| `RandomVariate` | fixed | 18 | batch 5 distributions (including multivariate, empirical and transformed ones); RandomVariate[d, {n, m}] arrays; reproducible with SeedRandom (the random stream differs from Wolfram's); RandomVariate[dist] and RandomVariate[dist, n] by inverse-CDF sampling (machine numbers; integers for discrete distributions) |
| `SeedRandom` | promoted | 14 | SeedRandom[n] seeds RandomSample/RandomVariate |
| `ShapiroWilk` | fixed | 3 | {W -> statistic, p_value -> p} (SciPy), unrounded; n >= 3 |
| `Skewness` | fixed | 31 | matrix data: the statistic of each column (Wolfram); Skewness[dist] for every supported distribution (Indeterminate when undefined; Kurtosis is the non-excess kurtosis, 3 for the normal distribution); m3/m2^(3/2) with population moments (Wolfram), exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits) |
| `SpearmanCorrelation` | fixed | 3 | {rho -> exact Spearman rho (average ranks for ties), p -> p-value (SciPy)} (MikoshiLang) |
| `StandardDeviation` | fixed | 27 | matrix data: the statistic of each column (Wolfram); StandardDeviation[dist] for the batch 5 distributions (Indeterminate when the moment does not exist); Sqrt[Variance], exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits); StandardDeviation[dist] |
| `StandardError` | fixed | 3 | StandardDeviation/Sqrt[n] (standard error of the mean), exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits) |
| `StudentTDistribution` | fixed | 18 | StudentTDistribution[mu, sigma, nu] (location-scale form): every distribution function via batch 5; inert Student t distribution [nu] |
| `TDistribution` | verified | 3 | inert Student t distribution (MikoshiLang name; StudentTDistribution is the Wolfram name) |
| `TTest` | fixed | 4 | Wolfram TTest[data] and TTest[data, mu0] return the two-sided one-sample p-value; TTest[{x, y}] the Welch two-sample p-value; legacy returned rounded rule lists and TTest[x, y] used the pooled-variance test (now raises with a pointer to TTest[{x, y}]) |
| `TrimmedMean` | fixed | 3 | mean after dropping Floor[f n] values from each end (0 <= f < 1/2), exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits) |
| `UniformDistribution` | verified | 20 | inert distribution UniformDistribution[{min, max}] (Wolfram); the legacy form [min, max] is accepted |
| `Variance` | fixed | 52 | matrix data: the statistic of each column (Wolfram); Variance[dist] for the batch 5 distributions (Indeterminate when the moment does not exist); unbiased sample variance (n - 1), exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits); Variance[dist] |
| `WilcoxonTest` | fixed | 3 | paired signed-rank test {W -> statistic, p_value -> p} (SciPy), unrounded |
| `ZScore` | fixed | 3 | (x - mu)/sigma, exact; sigma <= 0 raises (legacy rounded floats) |
| `ZScoreNormalize` | fixed | 3 | (x - Mean)/StandardDeviation (Wolfram Standardize), exact for exact data (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits) |

| `GumbelDistribution` | added | 21 | inert distribution object GumbelDistribution; parameters validated |
| `HazardFunction` | added | 7 | PDF/SurvivalFunction; exact; numeric x outside the support gives 0 |

### strings (batch 1)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Alphabet` | added | 3 | Alphabet[] and Alphabet["English"] give the 26 lower-case letters; other languages are not supported (curated data) |
| `Capitalize` | added | 4 | upper-cases the first character and leaves the rest; Capitalize[s, "AllWords"] does that for every word, "LongWords" for words longer than three characters, "FirstWord" = default ("TitleCase" with its small-word rules is not supported) |
| `CharacterRange` | added | 3 | characters from c1 to c2 by code point (strings or integer codes); empty when c2 < c1 |
| `Decapitalize` | added | 3 | lower-cases the first character only |
| `DigitCharacter` | added | 14 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `DigitQ` | added | 3 | True when every character of the string satisfies the test (so True for ""); non-strings False |
| `EditDistance` | added | 32 | Levenshtein distance (insertions, deletions, substitutions) between strings or lists; IgnoreCase |
| `EndOfLine` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `EndOfString` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `FromLetterNumber` | added | 4 | the n-th lower-case English letter for 1 <= n <= 26 (negative n counts from z, 0 gives " "); other values raise; threads over lists |
| `FromRomanNumeral` | added | 13 | integer value of a Roman numeral string ("N" -> 0) |
| `HammingDistance` | added | 4 | number of differing positions between equal-length strings or lists (unequal lengths raise); IgnoreCase |
| `HexadecimalCharacter` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `IgnoreCase` | added | 10 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `IntegerString` | added | 10 | digits of \|n\| (the sign is dropped, as Wolfram) in base 2-36 with lower-case letters, padded with zeros or truncated to the last len digits; IntegerString[n, "Roman"]; threads over lists |
| `LetterCharacter` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `LetterNumber` | added | 3 | position of a letter in the English alphabet (either case), 0 for other characters; a string of several characters gives a list; threads over lists |
| `LetterQ` | added | 4 | True when every character of the string satisfies the test (so True for ""); non-strings False |
| `Longest` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `LongestCommonSequence` | added | 3 | longest common (not necessarily contiguous) subsequence of two strings or lists by dynamic programming; among equal-length answers the one found by the standard back-trace is returned |
| `LongestCommonSubsequence` | added | 3 | longest contiguous common run of two strings or lists (Wolfram's meaning; the non-contiguous one is LongestCommonSequence); ties go to the earliest position in the first argument |
| `LowerCaseQ` | added | 4 | True when every character of the string satisfies the test (so True for ""); non-strings False |
| `NumberString` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `Overlaps` | added | 5 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `PalindromeQ` | added | 5 | True for a string, list or non-negative integer (decimal digits) equal to its reverse; other expressions False |
| `PrintableASCIIQ` | added | 3 | True when every character of the string satisfies the test (so True for ""); non-strings False |
| `PunctuationCharacter` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `RegularExpression` | added | 4 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `RemoveDiacritics` | added | 4 | drops combining marks after Unicode NFKD decomposition (ligatures such as æ are kept) |
| `RomanNumeral` | added | 14 | Roman numeral string of n for 1-4999 (sign dropped); RomanNumeral[0] gives "N"; threads over lists; the overbar forms for n >= 5000 are not supported |
| `Shortest` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `StartOfLine` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `StartOfString` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `StringCases` | added | 32 | substrings matching a string pattern, or rule right-hand sides with named parts substituted; StringCases[s, p, n]; Overlaps -> False by default; IgnoreCase |
| `StringContainsQ` | fixed | 8 | string patterns (legacy: literal substrings only); IgnoreCase; threads over lists |
| `StringCount` | fixed | 10 | non-overlapping matches of a string pattern (legacy: literal substrings only); Overlaps -> True counts one match per start position; IgnoreCase; the empty literal raises |
| `StringDelete` | fixed | 7 | deletes every match of a string pattern (or list of patterns); IgnoreCase; the legacy slice form StringDelete[s, i, j] raises (use StringDrop[s, {i, j}]) |
| `StringEndsQ` | fixed | 6 | string patterns (legacy: literal suffix only); IgnoreCase |
| `StringExpression` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `StringExtract` | added | 5 | StringExtract[s, n] gives the n-th whitespace-separated piece (spans, lists and negative n allowed); StringExtract[s, sep -> n] splits at sep |
| `StringFreeQ` | added | 3 | not StringContainsQ; string patterns; IgnoreCase |
| `StringMatchQ` | added | 18 | whole-string match against a string pattern; literal patterns use the metacharacters * (any characters) and @ (characters other than upper-case letters); IgnoreCase; threads over lists |
| `StringPartition` | added | 3 | substrings of length n (incomplete final piece dropped), offset d, or UpTo[n] keeping the remainder |
| `StringPosition` | added | 4 | {{start, end}, ...} 1-based; Overlaps -> True by default (one match per start position), StringPosition[s, p, n]; IgnoreCase |
| `StringReplace` | fixed | 21 | StringReplace[s, p -> r], [s, {rules}], [s, rules, n] with string patterns; named pattern parts substitute into the right-hand side; RegularExpression rules accept "$1" group references; non-string replacements give a StringExpression; the legacy form StringReplace[s, old, new] raises |
| `StringReplaceList` | added | 3 | every string obtained by replacing a single match of the rules (one result per match position and rule, left to right); StringReplaceList[s, rules, n] keeps at most n |
| `StringReplacePart` | added | 3 | StringReplacePart[s, new, {m, n}], a list of ranges with one new string, or a list of new strings matched to the ranges; negative positions count from the end |
| `StringRiffle` | added | 7 | StringRiffle[list] joins with spaces (nested lists: spaces at the lowest level, one more newline per level above), StringRiffle[list, sep], StringRiffle[list, {left, sep, right}], StringRiffle[list, sep1, sep2, ...] per level; non-strings are converted with ToString |
| `StringRotateLeft` | added | 3 | cyclic rotation by n characters (default 1) |
| `StringRotateRight` | added | 3 | cyclic rotation by n characters (default 1) |
| `StringSplit` | added | 15 | splits at whitespace or at matches of a string pattern; empty pieces at the ends are dropped (kept with StringSplit[s, p, All]), interior ones kept; p -> r inserts r at each split; StringSplit[s, p, n] gives at most n pieces; IgnoreCase; threads over lists |
| `StringStartsQ` | fixed | 6 | string patterns (legacy: literal prefix only); IgnoreCase |
| `StringTakeDrop` | added | 3 | {StringTake[s, spec], StringDrop[s, spec]} |
| `StringTrim` | fixed | 7 | StringTrim[s] removes leading and trailing whitespace; StringTrim[s, p] removes a leading and a trailing match of the string pattern p |
| `Symbol` | added | 10 | Symbol["name"] gives the symbol (evaluated); invalid names raise |
| `SymbolName` | added | 4 | name of a symbol as a string (no context prefix) |
| `TextWords` | added | 3 | words of the text: runs of letters/digits, keeping internal apostrophes and hyphens (rule-based; Wolfram's tokenizer may differ on unusual punctuation) |
| `ToExpression` | added | 14 | parses and evaluates InputForm text; ToExpression[s, InputForm, h] wraps the unevaluated parse in h (e.g. Hold); invalid syntax gives $Failed; non-strings are returned evaluated |
| `ToLowerCase` | added | 3 | lower-cases every letter; threads over lists |
| `ToUpperCase` | added | 6 | upper-cases every letter; threads over lists |
| `UpperCaseQ` | added | 4 | True when every character of the string satisfies the test (so True for ""); non-strings False |

| `WhitespaceCharacter` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `WordBoundary` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |

| `DayOfWeek` | fixed | 3 | day of the week of a Gregorian date as a symbol (Friday), DayOfWeek[y, m, d] or DayOfWeek[{y, m, d}] (Wolfram DayName); legacy returned a string and truncated reals |

| `FromCharacterCode` | fixed | 3 | FromCharacterCode[n] and FromCharacterCode[{n1, n2, ...}] (legacy accepted only one code and truncated reals) |
| `HexDecode` | fixed | 3 | invalid hex now raises (legacy returned an Error expression) |
| `HexEncode` | verified | 3 | hex of the UTF-8 bytes (MikoshiLang) |
